Last amendment: 28 April 2023

JUAN CARLOS I

KING OF SPAIN
To all who see and understand this present document.
Know ye: That the General Courts have approved and I hereby sanction the following Organic Law:

PREAMBLE
If the legal order has been defined as a set of norms that regulate the use of force, the importance of the Criminal Code in any civilized society can be easily understood. The Criminal Code defines the crimes and misdemeanors that constitute the prerequisites for the application of the supreme form that the coercive power of the State may take: criminal punishment. Consequently, it occupies a preeminent place in the legal order as a whole, to the point that, not without reason, it has been considered as a kind of
"negative Constitution". The Criminal Code must protect the basic values and principles of social coexistence. When those values and principles change, it must also change. In our country, however, despite profound social, economic and political changes, the current text dates, in what might be considered its basic core, from the last century. The need for its reform cannot, therefore, be disputed.
Based on the various reform attempts carried out since the establishment of the democratic regime, the Government has prepared the bill that it submits for discussion and approval by the Chambers. It must, therefore, set out, albeit briefly, the criteria that inspire it, although these may be easily deduced from reading its text.
The axis of these criteria has been, as is logical, the positive adaptation of the new Criminal Code to constitutional values. The changes it introduces in that direction are innumerable, but some are worth highlighting.
First, a total reform of the current system of punishments is proposed, so as to achieve, as far as possible, the objectives of resocialization that the Constitution assigns to it. The proposed system simplifies, on the one hand, the regulation of custodial sentences, while expanding the possibilities of replacing them with others that affect less basic legal rights, and, on the other hand, introduces changes in pecuniary penalties, adopting the day-fine system and adding community service.
Second, the antinomy between the principle of minimum intervention and the growing need for protection in an increasingly complex society has been addressed, prudently accommodating new forms of crime, while eliminating criminal offenses that have lost their raison d'être. In the first sense, the introduction of crimes against the socio-economic order or the new regulation of crimes relating to land use planning and natural resources deserves mention; in the second, the disappearance of the complex offenses of robbery with violence and intimidation against persons which, having arisen in the context of the fight against banditry, must disappear giving way to the application of general rules.
Third, special emphasis has been placed on the protection of fundamental rights and care has been taken to design with special restraint the use of the punitive instrument where the exercise of any of them is at stake: as an example, on the one hand, the specific protection of moral integrity and, on the other, the new regulation of crimes against honor. By specifically protecting moral integrity, citizens are given stronger protection against torture, and by configuring crimes against honor in the manner proposed, freedom of expression is given all the relevance that a democratic regime can and should recognize.

Fourth, and in line with the objective of protecting and respecting fundamental rights, the privileged regime that unlawful interference by public officials in the sphere of citizens' rights and freedoms has enjoyed until now has been eliminated. Therefore, it is proposed that detentions, entries and searches of homes carried out by an authority or official outside the cases permitted by law, be treated as aggravated forms of the corresponding common crimes, and not as they have been until now, that is, as special crimes that have been incomprehensibly and unjustifiably mitigated.
Fifth, efforts have been made to advance on the path of real and effective equality, seeking to fulfill the task that, in this regard, the Constitution imposes on public authorities. Certainly the Criminal Code is not the most important instrument for carrying out this task; however, it can contribute to it by eliminating regulations that are an obstacle to its realization or by introducing protective measures against discriminatory situations. In addition to the rules that provide specific protection against activities tending toward discrimination, mention must be made here of the new regulation of crimes against sexual freedom. The aim is to adapt the criminal offenses to the protected legal interest, which is no longer, as it was historically, the honesty of women, but the sexual freedom of all. Under the protection of women's honesty lay an intolerable situation of grievance, which the proposed regulation completely eliminates. The novelty of the punitive techniques used may be surprising; but, in this case, departing from tradition seems wise.
Leaving the realm of principles and descending to that of drafting techniques, this bill differs from previous ones in its claim to universality. The idea had been that the Criminal Code should constitute a complete regulation of the punitive power of the State. The realization of this idea already started from a deficit, given the importance that the sanctioning power of the Administration has in our country; but, moreover, it was unnecessary and disruptive.
Unnecessary, because the nineteenth-century option in favor of the Criminal Code and against special laws was based on the undeniable fact that the legislator, when drafting a Code, was constrained, for external reasons of social importance, to respect constitutional principles, which did not occur, or occurred to a lesser extent, in the case of a particular law. In the framework of flexible constitutionalism, this was an argument of special importance to support the claim of absolute universality of the Code. Today, however, both the Criminal Code and special laws are hierarchically subordinate to the Constitution and obliged to submit to it, not only because of that hierarchy, but also because of the existence of judicial review of constitutionality. Consequently, special laws cannot raise the concerns they historically provoked.
Disruptive, because, although it is undeniable that a Code would not deserve that name if it did not contain most of the criminal norms and, certainly, the basic principles informing all regulation, the truth is that there are matters that can hardly be introduced into it. For, if a relative claim to universality is inherent in the idea of a Code, so are those of stability and permanence, and there are areas in which, due to the special situation of the rest of the legal order, or due to the very nature of things, such stability and permanence are impossible. Such is, for example, the case of crimes relating to exchange controls. In them, the constant modification of economic conditions and the normative context, in which, whether one likes it or not, such crimes are integrated, advises placing the criminal norms in that context and leaving them outside the Code: moreover, this is our tradition, and there is no shortage, in the countries around us, of characteristic examples of similar proceedings.
Thus, in this and other similar cases, the option has been to refer to the corresponding special laws the criminal regulation of the respective matters. The same technique has been used for the rules regulating the decriminalization of voluntary termination of pregnancy. In this case, along with reasons similar to those previously stated, it could be argued that these are not incriminating norms, but norms that regulate cases of non-incrimination. The Constitutional Court required that, in the configuration of these cases, guarantees be adopted that do not seem proper to a Criminal Code, but rather to another type of norm.

In preparing the bill, the parliamentary discussions of the 1992 bill, the opinion of the General Council of the Judiciary, the state of case law and the opinions of legal scholarship have been taken very much into account. It has been carried out from the deeply felt idea that the Criminal Code must belong to everyone and that, consequently, all opinions must be heard and the most reasonable solutions chosen, that is, those that everyone should be able to accept.
The aim is not to have produced a perfect work, but simply a useful one. The Government does not have the last word here, but only the first. It therefore limits itself, with this bill, to pronouncing it, inviting all political forces and all citizens to collaborate in the task of its improvement. Only if we all wish to have a better Criminal Code and contribute to achieving it can an objective be achieved whose importance for coexistence and the peaceful enjoyment of the rights and freedoms that the Constitution proclaims could hardly be exaggerated.

PRELIMINARY TITLE
On criminal guarantees and the application of criminal law

This excerpt sits under: CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION > BOOK III. Misdemeanors and their punishments

Article 1

1.	No act or omission that is not provided for as an offence by law prior to its commission shall be punished.
2.	Security measures may only be applied when the prerequisites previously established by law are met.

Article 2

1.	No offence shall be punished with a penalty that is not provided for by law prior to its commission. Laws establishing security measures shall likewise have no retroactive effect.
2.	Notwithstanding, criminal laws that favour the offender shall have retroactive effect, even if a final judgment has been handed down and the subject is serving the sentence when they enter into force. In case of doubt as to the determination of the more favourable law, the offender shall be heard. Acts committed under the validity of a temporary law shall nevertheless be tried in accordance with it, unless expressly provided otherwise.

Article 3

1.	No penalty or security measure may be executed except by virtue of a final judgment handed down by the competent judge or court, in accordance with procedural laws.
2.	Nor may any penalty or security measure be executed in any form other than that prescribed by law and the regulations that develop it, nor with circumstances or incidents other than those expressed in its text. The execution of the penalty or security measure shall be carried out under the control of the competent judges and courts.

Article 4

1.	Criminal laws shall not be applied to cases other than those expressly included in them.
2.	In the event that a judge or court, in the exercise of its jurisdiction, becomes aware of any act or omission that, without being punished by law, it deems worthy of punishment, it shall refrain from all proceedings concerning it and shall set out to the Government the reasons it has to believe that it should be the subject of criminal sanction.
3.	In the same way it shall approach the Government setting out what is appropriate concerning the repeal or modification of the provision or the granting of a pardon, without prejudice to executing the judgment immediately, when from the rigorous application of the provisions of the law an act or omission is punished that, in the judgment of the judge or court, should not be, or when the penalty is notably excessive, having regard to the harm caused by the infringement and the personal circumstances of the offender.

4.	If a petition for pardon is made, and the judge or court has found in a reasoned decision that the fulfilment of the penalty may result in a violation of the right to a trial without undue delay, it shall suspend the execution thereof until the petition submitted is resolved.
The judge or court may also suspend the execution of the penalty, while the pardon is not resolved, when, if the judgment were executed, its purpose could prove illusory.

Article 5

There is no penalty without intent or recklessness.

Article 6

1.	Security measures are based on the criminal dangerousness of the subject on whom they are imposed, manifested in the commission of an act provided for as an offence.
2.	Security measures cannot be more onerous or of longer duration than the penalty abstractly applicable to the act committed, nor exceed the limit of what is necessary to prevent the dangerousness of the perpetrator.

Article 7

For the purposes of determining the criminal law applicable in time, offences are considered committed at the moment when the subject performs the act or omits the act that he was obliged to perform.

Article 8

Acts capable of being classified according to two or more provisions of this Code, and not covered by articles 73 to 77, shall be punished observing the following rules:
1.	ª The special provision shall be applied in preference to the general one.
2.	ª The subsidiary provision shall be applied only in the absence of the principal one, whether such subsidiarity is expressly declared or is tacitly deducible.
3.	ª The broader or more complex criminal provision shall absorb those that punish the infringements subsumed in it.
4.	ª In the absence of the foregoing criteria, the more serious criminal provision shall exclude those that punish the act with a lesser penalty.

Article 9

The provisions of this Title shall apply to offences that are punished by special laws. The remaining provisions of this Code shall apply as supplementary provisions in matters not expressly provided for by them.

BOOK I

General provisions on offences, responsible persons, penalties, security measures and other consequences of criminal infringement.

TITLE I

On criminal infringement

CHAPTER I

On offences

Article 10

Offences are intentional or reckless acts and omissions punished by law.

Article 11

Offences consisting in the production of a result shall only be understood as committed by omission when the failure to prevent it, by infringing a special legal duty of the perpetrator, is equivalent, according to the meaning of the text of the law, to its causation. For this purpose, omission shall be equated with action:
a)	When there is a specific legal or contractual obligation to act.
b)	When the person who omits has created an occasion of risk for the legally protected interest by means of a preceding act or omission.

Article 12

Reckless acts or omissions shall only be punished when the law expressly so provides.

Article 13

1.	Serious offences are infringements that the law punishes with a serious penalty.
2.	Less serious offences are infringements that the law punishes with a less serious penalty.
3.	Minor offences are infringements that the law punishes with a minor penalty.
4.	When the penalty, by its extent, may be included at the same time among those mentioned in the first two paragraphs of this article, the offence shall be considered, in any case, as serious. When the penalty, by its extent, may be considered as minor and as less serious, the offence shall be considered, in any case, as minor.

Article 14

1.	Unavoidable error concerning a fact constituting the criminal infringement excludes criminal liability. If the error, having regard to the circumstances of the act and the personal circumstances of the perpetrator, were avoidable, the infringement shall be punished, where appropriate, as reckless.
2.	Error concerning a fact that qualifies the infringement or concerning an aggravating circumstance shall prevent its appreciation.
3.	Unavoidable error concerning the unlawfulness of the act constituting the criminal infringement excludes criminal liability. If the error were avoidable, the penalty lower by one or two degrees shall be applied.

Article 15

The completed offence and the attempt to commit an offence are punishable.

Article 16

1.	There is an attempt when the subject begins the execution of the offence directly by external acts, performing all or part of the acts that should objectively produce the result, and nevertheless this does not occur for reasons independent of the perpetrator's will.
2.	A person who voluntarily prevents the completion of the offence shall be exempt from criminal liability for the attempted offence, either by desisting from the execution already begun, or by preventing the production of the result, without prejudice to the liability that he may have incurred for the acts performed, if these already constitute another offence.
3.	When several subjects intervene in an act, that person or those persons who desist from the execution already begun, and prevent or attempt to prevent, seriously, firmly and decisively, the completion, shall be exempt from criminal liability, without prejudice to the liability they may have incurred for the acts performed, if these already constitute another offence.

Article 17

1. Conspiracy exists when two or more persons agree on the commission of a crime and resolve to commit it.
2. Solicitation exists when a person who has resolved to commit a crime invites another person or persons to participate in it.
3. Conspiracy and solicitation to commit a crime shall only be punished in cases specifically provided for by law.

Article 18

1. Incitement exists when there is direct incitement, by means of the press, broadcasting or any other medium of similar effectiveness that facilitates publicity, or before a gathering of persons, to the perpetration of a crime.
For the purposes of this Code, apology is the exposition, before a gathering of persons or by any means of dissemination, of ideas or doctrines that glorify crime or exalt its author. Apology shall only be criminal as a form of incitement and if by its nature and circumstances it constitutes direct incitement to commit a crime.
2. Incitement shall be punished exclusively in cases where the Law so provides.
If the perpetration of the crime has followed the incitement, it shall be punished as inducement.

CHAPTER II

Grounds that exempt from criminal liability

Article 19

Persons under eighteen years of age shall not be criminally liable under this Code.
When a minor of that age commits a criminal act, he or she may be liable in accordance with the provisions of the law regulating the criminal liability of minors.

Article 20

The following are exempt from criminal liability:
1. º A person who, at the time of committing the criminal offence, by reason of any mental anomaly or disorder, is unable to understand the unlawfulness of the act or to act in accordance with that understanding.
Transitory mental disorder shall not exempt from punishment when it has been provoked by the subject for the purpose of committing the crime or when he or she has foreseen or should have foreseen its commission.
2. º A person who, at the time of committing the criminal offence, is in a state of full intoxication from the consumption of alcoholic beverages, toxic drugs, narcotics, psychotropic substances or others that produce similar effects, provided that it has not been sought for the purpose of committing it or its commission has not been foreseen or should have been foreseen, or is under the influence of a withdrawal syndrome, due to dependence on such substances, that prevents him or her from understanding the unlawfulness of the act or acting in accordance with that understanding.
3. º A person who, by suffering alterations in perception from birth or from childhood, has a seriously altered awareness of reality.
4. º A person who acts in defence of his or her own person or rights or those of others, provided that the following requirements are met:
First. Unlawful aggression. In the case of defence of property, an attack on it that constitutes a crime and places it in serious danger of imminent damage or loss shall be deemed unlawful aggression. In the case of defence of the dwelling or its appurtenances, unlawful entry into it or them shall be deemed unlawful aggression.
Second. Rational necessity of the means employed to prevent or repel it. Third. Lack of sufficient provocation on the part of the defender.

5. º A person who, in a state of necessity, in order to avoid harm to himself or herself or to another, injures a legal interest of another person or breaches a duty, provided that the following requirements are met:
First. That the harm caused is not greater than that which is sought to be avoided.
Second. That the situation of necessity has not been intentionally provoked by the subject.
Third. That the person in need does not have, by virtue of his or her office or position, an obligation to sacrifice himself or herself.
6. º A person who acts impelled by insurmountable fear.
7. º A person who acts in fulfilment of a duty or in the lawful exercise of a right, office or position.
In the cases of the first three numbers, the security measures provided for in this Code shall be applied, where appropriate.

CHAPTER III

Circumstances that mitigate criminal liability

Article 21

The following are mitigating circumstances:
1. ª The grounds set out in the preceding chapter, when not all the requirements necessary to exempt from liability in their respective cases are met.
2. ª That the offender acted by reason of his or her serious addiction to the substances mentioned in number 2.º of the preceding article.
3. ª That of acting by reason of causes or stimuli so powerful that they have produced rage, mental confusion or another emotional state of similar nature.
4. ª That the offender has proceeded, before learning that judicial proceedings are directed against him or her, to confess the offence to the authorities.
5. ª That the offender has proceeded to repair the damage caused to the victim, or to diminish its effects, at any stage of the proceedings and prior to the holding of the oral trial.
6. ª Extraordinary and undue delay in the processing of the proceedings, provided that it is not attributable to the accused himself or herself and is not proportionate to the complexity of the case.
7. ª Any other circumstance of similar significance to the foregoing.

CHAPTER IV

Circumstances that aggravate criminal liability

Article 22

The following are aggravating circumstances:
1. ª Committing the act with treachery.
There is treachery when the offender commits any of the crimes against persons by employing in the commission means, methods or forms that tend directly or especially to ensure it, without the risk that might arise to his or her person from defence by the victim.
2. ª Committing the act by means of disguise, with abuse of superiority or by taking advantage of circumstances of place, time or assistance from other persons that weaken the defence of the victim or facilitate the impunity of the offender.
3. ª Committing the act for a price, reward or promise.
4. ª Committing the crime for racist, anti-Semitic, anti-Roma or other discriminatory motives relating to the ideology, religion or beliefs of the victim, the ethnicity, race or nation to which he or she belongs, his or her sex, age, sexual orientation or gender identity, gender reasons, aporophobia or social exclusion, the illness he or she suffers from or his or her

disability, regardless of whether such conditions or circumstances actually exist in the person upon whom the conduct falls.
5. ª Deliberately and inhumanly increasing the suffering of the victim, causing him or her unnecessary suffering for the commission of the crime.
6. ª Acting with abuse of trust.
7. ª Taking advantage of the public character held by the offender.
8. ª Being a recidivist.
There is recidivism when, upon committing a crime, the offender has been finally convicted of a crime included in the same title of this Code, provided that it is of the same nature.
For the purposes of this number, cancelled criminal records or those that should be cancelled shall not be counted, nor those corresponding to minor offences.
Final convictions by judges or courts imposed in other States of the European Union shall produce the effects of recidivism unless the criminal record has been cancelled or could be cancelled under Spanish law.

CHAPTER V

The mixed circumstance of kinship

Article 23

It is a circumstance that may mitigate or aggravate liability, depending on the nature, motives and effects of the crime, that the aggrieved party is or has been the spouse or person who is or has been stably linked by an analogous relationship of affection, or is an ascendant, descendant or sibling by nature or adoption of the offender or of his or her spouse or cohabitant.

CHAPTER VI

General provisions

Article 24

1.	For criminal purposes, authority shall be deemed to be anyone who, alone or as a member of any corporation, tribunal or collegiate body, has command or exercises jurisdiction of their own. In any case, members of the Congress of Deputies, the Senate, the Legislative Assemblies of the Autonomous Communities and the European Parliament shall be considered authorities. Officials of the Public Prosecutor's Office and Prosecutors of the European Public Prosecutor's Office shall also be considered authorities.
2.	Any person who, by immediate provision of law or by election or appointment by competent authority, participates in the exercise of public functions shall be considered a public official.

Article 25

For the purposes of this Code, disability means the situation in which a person finds themselves with permanent physical, mental, intellectual or sensory impairments which, in interaction with various barriers, may limit or prevent their full and effective participation in society, on equal terms with others.
Likewise, for the purposes of this Code, a person with a disability in need of special protection shall be understood to mean a person with a disability who, whether or not their capacity to act has been judicially modified, requires assistance or support for the exercise of their legal capacity and for decision-making regarding their person, their rights or interests due to their permanent intellectual or mental impairments.

Article 26

For the purposes of this Code, a document is considered to be any material medium that expresses or incorporates data, facts or narratives with probative efficacy or any other type of legal relevance.

TITLE II

Persons criminally liable for offences

Article 27

The perpetrators and accomplices are criminally liable for offences.

Article 28

Perpetrators are those who carry out the act by themselves, jointly or through another person whom they use as an instrument.
The following shall also be considered perpetrators:
a)	Those who directly induce another or others to execute it.
b)	Those who cooperate in its execution with an act without which it would not have been carried out.

Article 29

Accomplices are those who, not being included in the preceding article, cooperate in the execution of the act with prior or simultaneous acts.

Article 30

1.	In offences committed using mechanical means or media of dissemination, neither accomplices nor those who have favoured them personally or materially shall be criminally liable.
2.	The perpetrators referred to in Article 28 shall be liable in a staggered, exclusive and subsidiary manner in accordance with the following order:
1.	º Those who have actually written the text or produced the sign in question, and those who have induced them to do so.
2.	º The directors of the publication or programme in which it is disseminated.
3.	º The directors of the publishing, broadcasting or disseminating company.
4.	º The directors of the recording, reproducing or printing company.
3.	When, for any reason other than the extinction of criminal liability, including a declaration of default or residence outside Spain, none of the persons included in any of the numbers of the preceding paragraph can be prosecuted, proceedings shall be brought against those mentioned in the immediately following number.

Article 31

Anyone who acts as a de facto or de jure administrator of a legal person, or in the name or legal or voluntary representation of another, shall be personally liable, even if the conditions, qualities or relationships that the corresponding criminal offence requires in order to be an active subject thereof do not exist in them, if such circumstances exist in the entity or person in whose name or representation they act.

Article 31 bis

1.	In the cases provided for in this Code, legal persons shall be criminally liable:
a)	For offences committed in the name or on behalf of the same, and for their direct or indirect benefit, by their legal representatives or by those who, acting individually or as members of an organ of the legal person, are authorised

to take decisions in the name of the legal person or hold powers of organisation and control within the same.
b)	For offences committed, in the exercise of corporate activities and on behalf of and for the direct or indirect benefit of the same, by those who, being subject to the authority of the natural persons mentioned in the preceding paragraph, have been able to carry out the acts because those persons have seriously failed to comply with their duties of supervision, monitoring and control of their activity having regard to the specific circumstances of the case.
2.	If the offence were committed by the persons indicated in letter a) of the preceding paragraph, the legal person shall be exempt from liability if the following conditions are met:
1.	the governing body has adopted and effectively implemented, before the commission of the offence, organisation and management models that include appropriate monitoring and control measures to prevent offences of the same nature or to significantly reduce the risk of their commission;
2.	the supervision of the operation and compliance with the prevention model implemented has been entrusted to an organ of the legal person with autonomous powers of initiative and control or which has been legally assigned the function of supervising the effectiveness of the internal controls of the legal person;
3.	the individual perpetrators have committed the offence by fraudulently evading the organisation and prevention models and
4.	there has been no omission or insufficient exercise of its functions of supervision, monitoring and control by the organ referred to in condition 2.
In cases where the foregoing circumstances can only be partially proven, this circumstance shall be assessed for the purposes of mitigation of the penalty.
3.	In small legal persons, the supervisory functions referred to in condition 2 of paragraph 2 may be assumed directly by the governing body. For these purposes, small legal persons are those which, according to the applicable legislation, are authorised to present an abridged profit and loss account.
4.	If the offence were committed by the persons indicated in letter b) of paragraph 1, the legal person shall be exempt from liability if, before the commission of the offence, it has adopted and effectively implemented an organisation and management model that is adequate to prevent offences of the nature of that which was committed or to significantly reduce the risk of their commission.
In this case the mitigation provided for in the second subparagraph of paragraph 2 of this article shall also apply.
5.	The organisation and management models referred to in condition 1 of paragraph 2 and the preceding paragraph must meet the following requirements:
1.	They shall identify the activities within whose scope the offences that must be prevented may be committed.
2.	They shall establish the protocols or procedures that specify the process of forming the will of the legal person, of adopting decisions and of executing the same in relation to those.
3.	They shall provide for financial resource management models adequate to prevent the commission of the offences that must be prevented.
4.	They shall impose the obligation to report possible risks and breaches to the body responsible for monitoring the operation and observance of the prevention model.
5.	They shall establish a disciplinary system that adequately sanctions non-compliance with the measures established by the model.
6.	They shall carry out a periodic verification of the model and its possible modification when significant infringements of its provisions come to light, or when changes occur in the organisation, in the control structure or in the activity carried out that make them necessary.

Article 31 ter

1.	The criminal liability of legal persons shall be enforceable whenever the commission of an offence is established that had to be committed by a person holding the positions or functions referred to in the preceding article, even when the specific responsible natural person has not been identified or it has not been possible to bring proceedings against that person. When as a consequence of the same facts a fine is imposed on both, the judges or courts shall modulate the respective amounts, so that the resulting sum is not disproportionate in relation to the seriousness of those facts.
2.	The concurrence, in the persons who have materially carried out the acts or in those who have made them possible by not having exercised due control, of circumstances that affect the culpability of the accused or aggravate their liability, or the fact that such persons have died or have evaded the action of justice, shall not exclude or modify the criminal liability of legal persons, without prejudice to what is provided in the following article.

Article 31 quater

1.	Only the following activities carried out after the commission of the offence and through their legal representatives may be considered mitigating circumstances of the criminal liability of legal persons:
a)	Having proceeded, before learning that the judicial proceedings are directed against it, to confess the infringement to the authorities.
b)	Having cooperated in the investigation of the act by providing evidence, at any stage of the proceedings, that was new and decisive for clarifying the criminal liabilities arising from the acts.
c)	Having proceeded at any stage of the proceedings and before the oral trial to repair or reduce the damage caused by the offence.
d)	Having established, before the commencement of the oral trial, effective measures to prevent and detect offences that might be committed in the future with the means or under the cover of the legal person.

Article 31 quinquies

1.	The provisions relating to the criminal liability of legal persons shall not apply to the State, to territorial and institutional Public Administrations, to Regulatory Bodies, public Business Agencies and Entities, to international organisations under public law, nor to those others that exercise public powers of sovereignty or administrative powers.
2.	In the case of public commercial companies that implement public policies or provide services of general economic interest, only the penalties provided for in letters a) and g) of paragraph 7 of article 33 may be imposed on them. This limitation shall not apply when the judge or court finds that it is a legal form created by its promoters, founders, administrators or representatives for the purpose of evading possible criminal liability.

TITLE III

Of penalties

CHAPTER I

Of penalties, their classes and effects

Section 1 Of penalties and their classes

Article 32

The penalties that may be imposed in accordance with this Code, either as principal or as accessory penalties, are deprivation of liberty, deprivation of other rights and fines.

Article 33

1.	According to their nature and duration, penalties are classified as serious, less serious and minor.
2.	The following are serious penalties:
a)	Reviewable permanent imprisonment.
b)	Imprisonment exceeding five years.
c)	Absolute disqualification.
d)	Special disqualifications for a period exceeding five years.
e)	Suspension from employment or public office for a period exceeding five years.
f)	Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for a period exceeding eight years.
g)	Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for a period exceeding eight years.
h)	Deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or go to them, for a period exceeding five years.
i)	Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period exceeding five years.
j)	Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period exceeding five years.
k)	Deprivation of parental authority.
3.	The following are less serious penalties:
a)	Imprisonment from three months up to five years.
b)	Special disqualifications up to five years.
c)	Suspension from employment or public office up to five years.
d)	Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from one year and one day to eight years.
e)	Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from one year and one day to eight years.
f)	Special disqualification for the exercise of a profession, trade or business related to animals and for the possession of animals from one year and one day to five years.
g)	Deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or go to them, for a period of six months to five years.
h)	Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period of six months to five years.
i)	Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period of six months to five years.
j)	Fine of more than three months.
k)	Proportional fine, whatever its amount, except as provided in paragraph 7 of this article.
l)	Community service from thirty-one days to one year.
4.	The following are minor penalties:

a)	Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from three months to one year.
b)	Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from three months to one year.
c)	Special disqualification for the exercise of a profession, trade or business related to animals and for the possession of animals from three months to one year.
d)	Deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or go to them, for a period of less than six months.
e)	Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period of one month to less than six months.
f)	Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period of one month to less than six months.
g)	Fine of up to three months.
h)	Permanent location from one day to three months.
i)	Community service from one to thirty days.
5.	Subsidiary personal liability for non-payment of a fine shall be of a less serious or minor nature, according to that corresponding to the penalty it replaces.
6.	Accessory penalties shall have the duration that the principal penalty respectively has, except as expressly provided by other provisions of this Code.
7.	The penalties applicable to legal persons, all of which are considered serious, are as follows:
a)	Fine by instalments or proportional.
b)	Dissolution of the legal person. Dissolution shall result in the definitive loss of its legal personality, as well as of its capacity to act in any way in legal transactions, or to carry out any kind of activity, even if lawful.
c)	Suspension of its activities for a period that may not exceed five years.
d)	Closure of its premises and establishments for a period that may not exceed five years.
e)	Prohibition from carrying out in the future the activities in the exercise of which the offence was committed, facilitated or concealed. This prohibition may be temporary or permanent. If temporary, the period may not exceed fifteen years.
f)	Disqualification from obtaining subsidies and public aid, from contracting with the public sector and from enjoying tax or Social Security benefits and incentives, for a period that may not exceed fifteen years.
g)	Judicial intervention to safeguard the rights of workers or creditors for such time as deemed necessary, which may not exceed five years.
The intervention may affect the entire organisation or be limited to some of its facilities, sections or business units. The Judge or Court, in the judgment or, subsequently, by order, shall determine exactly the content of the intervention and shall determine who will be in charge of the intervention and within what time frames they must produce monitoring reports for the judicial body. The intervention may be modified or suspended at any time following a report from the receiver and the Public Prosecutor. The receiver shall have the right to access all facilities and premises of the company or legal person and to receive whatever information they deem necessary for the exercise of their functions. The aspects related to the exercise of the receiver's function, such as remuneration or necessary qualifications, shall be determined by regulation.
The temporary closure of premises or establishments, the suspension of corporate activities and judicial intervention may also be ordered by the Investigating Judge as a precautionary measure during the investigation of the case.

Article 34

The following shall not be considered penalties:
1.	Detention and pre-trial detention and other precautionary measures of a criminal nature.
2.	Fines and other corrections that, in the exercise of governmental or disciplinary powers, are imposed on subordinates or those subject to administration.

3.	Deprivations of rights and reparatory sanctions established by civil or administrative laws.

Section 2 Custodial penalties

Article 35

Custodial penalties are reviewable permanent imprisonment, imprisonment, permanent location and subsidiary personal liability for non-payment of a fine. Their enforcement, as well as penitentiary benefits that involve shortening of the sentence, shall be governed by the provisions of the laws and this Code.

Article 36

1.	The sentence of permanent imprisonment shall be reviewed in accordance with the provisions of Article 92.
The classification of the convicted person in the third grade must be authorized by the court following an individualized and favorable prognosis of social reintegration, after hearing the Public Prosecutor's Office and Penitentiary Institutions, and may not be carried out:
a)	Until the completion of twenty years of actual imprisonment, in the case that the convicted person had been sentenced for an offense under Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code.
b)	Until the completion of fifteen years of actual imprisonment, in all other cases.
In these cases, the convicted person may not enjoy leave permits until having served a minimum of twelve years of imprisonment, in the case provided for in letter a), and eight years of imprisonment, in that provided for in letter b).
2.	The sentence of imprisonment shall have a minimum duration of three months and a maximum of twenty years, except as may be exceptionally provided by other provisions of this Code.
When the duration of the sentence of imprisonment imposed is greater than five years, the judge or court may order that the classification of the convicted person in the third grade of penitentiary treatment not be carried out until the completion of half of the sentence imposed.
In any case, when the duration of the sentence of imprisonment imposed is greater than five years and involves the offenses listed below, the classification of the convicted person in the third grade of penitentiary treatment may not be carried out until the completion of half thereof:
a)	Offenses relating to terrorist organizations and groups and offenses of terrorism under Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code.
b)	Offenses committed within a criminal organization or group.
c)	Offenses under Title VII bis of Book II of this Code, when the victim is a minor or a person with a disability in need of special protection.
d)	Offenses under Article 181.
e)	Offenses under Chapter V of Title VIII of Book II of this Code, when the victim is under sixteen years of age.
In the cases under letters c), d) and e), if the sentence were greater than five years of imprisonment, the classification of the convicted person in the third grade of penitentiary treatment may not be carried out without specific assessment and report regarding the offender's use of the treatment program for those convicted of sexual assault.
3.	The judicial authority for penitentiary supervision, following an individualized and favorable prognosis of social reintegration and assessing, as appropriate, the personal circumstances of the convicted person and the evolution of the re-educational treatment, may decide with reasoning, after hearing the Public Prosecutor's Office, Penitentiary Institutions and the other parties, the application of the general regime of compliance, except in the cases contained in the preceding paragraph.
4.	In any case, the judicial authority for penitentiary supervision, as appropriate, may decide, following a report from the Public Prosecutor's Office, Penitentiary Institutions and the other parties, progression to third grade on humanitarian grounds and for reasons of personal dignity of convicted persons who are very seriously ill with incurable ailments and of septuagenarian persons, assessing, especially, their low dangerousness.

Article 37

1.	Permanent home confinement shall have a duration of up to six months. Its compliance requires the convicted person to remain at home or at a specific place fixed by the Judge in the judgment or subsequently by reasoned order.
Nevertheless, in cases where permanent home confinement is provided for as a principal sentence, taking into account the repetition in the commission of the offense and provided that the specific applicable provision expressly so provides, the Judge may decide in the judgment that the sentence of permanent home confinement be served on Saturdays, Sundays and public holidays at the penitentiary center closest to the convicted person's home.
2.	If the offender so requests and the circumstances so advise, after hearing the public prosecutor, the sentencing judge or court may decide that the sentence be served on Saturdays and Sundays or in a non-continuous manner.
3.	If the convicted person fails to comply with the sentence, the sentencing judge or court shall extract testimony to proceed in accordance with the provisions of Article 468.
4.	To guarantee effective compliance, the Judge or Court may order the use of mechanical or electronic means that allow the location of the offender.

Article 38

1.	When the offender is imprisoned, the duration of the sentences shall begin to be computed from the day on which the conviction judgment has become final.
2.	When the offender is not imprisoned, the duration of the sentences shall begin to be counted from the time of admission to the appropriate establishment for compliance.

Section 3 On sentences depriving rights

Article 39

The following are sentences depriving rights:
a)	Absolute disqualification.
b)	Special disqualification for public employment or office, profession, trade, industry or commerce, or other activities, whether or not remunerated, or from parental authority, guardianship, custody or curatorship, possession of animals, the right of passive suffrage or any other right.
c)	Suspension from public employment or office.
d)	Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds.
e)	Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons.
f)	Deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or to go to them.
g)	Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court.
h)	Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court.
i)	Community service work.
j)	Deprivation of parental authority.

Article 40

1.	The sentence of absolute disqualification shall have a duration of six to 20 years; those of special disqualification, from three months to 20 years, and that of suspension from public employment or office, from three months to six years.
2.	The sentence of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds, and that of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons, shall have a duration of three months to 10 years.
3.	The sentence of deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or to go to them shall have a duration of up to 10 years. The prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons, or from communicating with them, shall have a duration of one month to 10 years.
4.	The sentence of community service work shall have a duration of one day to one year.

5.	The duration of each of these sentences shall be as provided in the preceding paragraphs, except as may be exceptionally provided by other provisions of this Code.

Article 41

The sentence of absolute disqualification produces the definitive deprivation of all honors, public employments and offices held by the convicted person, even if they are elective. It also produces incapacity to obtain the same or any other honors, offices or public employments, and to be elected to public office, during the term of the sentence.

Article 42

The sentence of special disqualification for public employment or office produces the definitive deprivation of the employment or office upon which it falls, even if it is elective, and of the honors attached thereto. It also produces incapacity to obtain the same or similar ones, during the term of the sentence. The judgment must specify the employments, offices and honors upon which the disqualification falls.

Article 43

Suspension from public employment or office deprives the convicted person of its exercise during the term of the sentence.

Article 44

Special disqualification from passive suffrage deprives the convicted person, for the duration of the sentence, of the right to be elected to public office.

Article 45

Special disqualification from a profession, trade, industry or commerce or other activities, whether or not remunerated, or any other right, which must be specified expressly and with reasoning in the sentence, deprives the convicted person of the power to exercise them for the duration of the sentence. The judicial authority may restrict the disqualification to certain activities or functions of the profession or trade, remunerated or not, allowing, if possible, the exercise of those functions not directly related to the crime committed.

Article 46

Special disqualification from the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or foster care, deprives the convicted person of the rights inherent to the first, and entails the extinction of the others, as well as the incapacity to obtain appointment to such positions for the duration of the sentence. The penalty of deprivation of parental authority implies the loss of its ownership, with those rights of which the son or daughter is the holder with respect to the convicted person that are judicially determined subsisting. The judicial authority may order these penalties with respect to all or some of the minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection who are in the care of the convicted person.
To specify which rights of minors or persons with disabilities must subsist in case of deprivation of parental authority and to determine with respect to which persons the penalty is ordered, the judicial authority shall assess the best interests of the minor or person with a disability, in relation to the circumstances of the specific case.
For the purposes of this article, parental authority includes both that regulated in the Civil Code, including extended and rehabilitated parental authority, and analogous institutions provided for in the civil legislation of the autonomous communities.

Article 47

The imposition of the penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds shall disqualify the convicted person from exercising both rights for the time fixed in the sentence.
The imposition of the penalty of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons shall disqualify the convicted person from exercising this right for the time fixed in the sentence.
When the penalty imposed is for a period exceeding two years, it shall entail the loss of validity of the permit or license that enables driving or possession and carrying, respectively.

Article 48

1.	Deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or go to them prevents the convicted person from residing in or going to the place where he or she committed the crime, or to the place where the victim or his or her family resides, if they are different. In cases where an intellectual disability or a disability originating from a mental disorder has been declared, the specific case shall be studied in order to resolve bearing in mind the legal interests to be protected and the best interests of the person with a disability who, where appropriate, must have the necessary accompaniment and support means for compliance with the measure.
2.	The prohibition on approaching the victim, or those of his or her family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, prevents the convicted person from approaching them, wherever they may be, as well as approaching their home, their places of work and any other place frequented by them, with the visitation, communication and stay regime that, where appropriate, has been recognized in a civil judgment being suspended with respect to the children until the complete fulfillment of this penalty.
3.	The prohibition on communicating with the victim, or with those of his or her family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, prevents the convicted person from establishing with them, by any means of communication or computer or telematic means, written, verbal or visual contact.
4.	The judge or court may order that the monitoring of these measures be carried out through such electronic means as permit it.

Article 49

Community service, which may not be imposed without the consent of the convicted person, obliges him or her to provide unpaid cooperation in certain activities of public utility, which may consist, in relation to crimes of a similar nature to that committed by the convicted person, of work to repair the damage caused or to support or assist victims, as well as the participation of the convicted person in workshops or training programs for re-education, employment, cultural, road safety, sexual education, peaceful conflict resolution, positive parenting and other similar programs. Its daily duration may not exceed eight hours and its conditions shall be as follows:
1.	ª Execution shall be carried out under the supervision of the Penitentiary Surveillance Judge, who, for this purpose, shall request reports on the performance of the work from the Administration, public entity or association of general interest in which the services are provided.
2.	ª It shall not violate the dignity of the convicted person.
3.	ª Community service shall be facilitated by the Administration, which may establish appropriate agreements for this purpose.
4.	ª It shall enjoy the protection provided to convicted persons by penitentiary legislation on Social Security matters.
5.	ª It shall not be subject to the achievement of economic interests.
6.	ª The penitentiary social services, having made the necessary verifications, shall communicate to the Penitentiary Surveillance Judge the relevant incidents in the execution of the penalty and, in any case, if the convicted person:
a)	Is absent from work for at least two working days, provided that this constitutes a voluntary refusal on his or her part to comply with the penalty.

b)	Despite the requirements of the person responsible for the work center, his or her performance is significantly below the minimum required.
c)	Opposes or repeatedly and manifestly fails to comply with the instructions given to him or her by the person responsible for the work regarding its performance.
d)	For any other reason, his or her conduct is such that the person responsible for the work refuses to continue keeping him or her at the center.
Once the report has been assessed, the Penitentiary Surveillance Judge may order its execution at the same center, send the convicted person to complete its execution at another center or understand that the convicted person has breached the penalty.
In case of breach, testimony shall be taken to proceed in accordance with article 468.
7.	ª If the convicted person is absent from work for justified cause, it shall not be understood as abandonment of the activity. However, the lost work shall not be counted in the settlement of the sentence, in which the days or working days that he or she has actually worked out of the total imposed must be recorded.

Section 4 On the penalty of fine

Article 50

1.	The penalty of fine shall consist of the imposition on the convicted person of a pecuniary sanction.
2.	The penalty of fine shall be imposed, unless the Law provides otherwise, by the day-fine system.
3.	Its minimum extent shall be ten days and the maximum two years. Fines imposable on legal persons shall have a maximum extent of five years.
4.	The daily quota shall have a minimum of two and a maximum of 400 euros, except in the case of fines imposable on legal persons, in which the daily quota shall have a minimum of 30 and a maximum of 5,000 euros. For calculation purposes, when the duration is fixed by months or years, it shall be understood that months are of thirty days and years of three hundred and sixty.
5.	The Judges or Courts shall determine with reasons the extent of the penalty within the limits established for each offence and according to the rules of Chapter II of this Title. Likewise, they shall fix in the judgment the amount of these quotas, taking into account exclusively the economic situation of the offender, deduced from his assets, income, obligations and family burdens and other personal circumstances.
6.	The court, for justified cause, may authorise payment of the fine within a period not exceeding two years from the finality of the judgment, either in one payment or in the instalments determined. In this case, non-payment of two of them shall determine the maturity of the remaining ones.

Article 51

If, after the judgment, the economic situation of the convicted person changes, the judge or court, exceptionally and after due investigation of such situation, may modify both the amount of the periodic quotas and the periods for their payment.

Article 52

1.	Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding articles and when the Code so determines, the fine shall be established in proportion to the damage caused, the value of the object of the offence or the benefit obtained from it.
2.	In these cases, the judges and courts shall impose the fine within the limits fixed for each offence, considering to determine in each case its amount, not only the mitigating and aggravating circumstances of the act, but principally the economic situation of the guilty person.
3.	If, after the judgment, the economic situation of the convicted person worsens, the judge or court, exceptionally and after due investigation of such situation, may reduce the amount of the fine within the limits indicated by the law for the offence in question, or authorise its payment in the instalments determined.

4.	In cases in which this Code provides for a penalty of fine for legal persons in proportion to the benefit obtained or facilitated, the harm caused, the value of the object, or the amount defrauded or unduly obtained, if calculation based on such concepts is not possible, the Judge or Court shall give reasons for the impossibility of proceeding with such calculation and the fines provided shall be replaced by the following:
a)	Fine of two to five years, if the offence committed by the natural person has a prescribed penalty of imprisonment of more than five years.
b)	Fine of one to three years, if the offence committed by the natural person has a prescribed penalty of imprisonment of more than two years not included in the preceding subparagraph.
c)	Fine of six months to two years, in all other cases.

Article 53

1.	If the convicted person does not satisfy, voluntarily or by way of enforcement, the fine imposed, he shall be subject to subsidiary personal liability of one day of deprivation of liberty for every two daily quotas not satisfied, which, in the case of minor offences, may be served by means of permanent location. In this case, the limitation established in paragraph 1 of Article 37 on its duration shall not apply.
The judge or court may also, with the consent of the convicted person, order that the subsidiary liability be served by means of community service. In this case, each day of deprivation of liberty shall be equivalent to one day of work.
2.	In cases of proportional fine the Judges and Courts shall establish, according to their prudent discretion, the subsidiary personal liability that is appropriate, which may not exceed, in any case, one year in duration. The Judge or Court may also order, with the consent of the convicted person, that it be served by means of community service.
3.	This subsidiary liability shall not be imposed on those convicted to a penalty of deprivation of liberty exceeding five years.
4.	The fulfilment of the subsidiary liability extinguishes the obligation to pay the fine, even if the economic situation of the convicted person improves.
5.	Payment of the fine imposed on a legal person may be divided over a period of up to five years, when its amount demonstrably endangers the survival of the latter or the maintenance of existing jobs therein, or when the general interest so advises. If the convicted legal person does not satisfy, voluntarily or by way of enforcement, the fine imposed within the period that has been indicated, the Court may order its intervention until the total payment thereof.

Section 5 On accessory penalties

Article 54

Penalties of disqualification are accessory in cases in which, without imposing them specifically, the Law declares that other penalties carry them with them.

Article 55

The penalty of imprisonment equal to or exceeding ten years shall carry with it absolute disqualification for the duration of the sentence, unless this is already provided as a principal penalty for the case in question. The Judge may also order special disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or fostering, or else the deprivation of parental authority, when these rights have had a direct relationship with the offence committed. This connection must be expressly determined in the judgment.

Article 56

1.	In penalties of imprisonment of less than ten years, the judges or courts shall impose, having regard to the gravity of the offence, as accessory penalties, one or more of the following:
1.	º Suspension from public employment or office.

2.	º Special disqualification for the right of passive suffrage for the duration of the sentence.
3.	º Special disqualification for public employment or office, profession, trade, industry, commerce, exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or fostering or any other right, the deprivation of parental authority, if these rights have had a direct relationship with the offence committed, this connection having to be expressly determined in the judgment, without prejudice to the application of the provisions of Article 579 of this Code.
2.	The provisions of this article are understood without prejudice to the application of the provisions of other precepts of this Code regarding the imposition of these penalties.

Article 57

1. The judicial authorities, in crimes of homicide, abortion, injuries, against freedom, torture and against moral integrity, trafficking in human beings, against sexual freedom and indemnity, privacy, the right to one's own image and the inviolability of the home, honour, property, the socio-economic order and family relations, having regard to the seriousness of the facts or the danger represented by the offender, may order in their judgments the imposition of one or more of the prohibitions contemplated in Article 48, for a time that shall not exceed ten years if the crime were serious, or five if it were less serious.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the convicted person were sentenced to imprisonment and the Judge or Court were to order the imposition of one or more of said prohibitions, it shall do so for a time exceeding by between one and ten years the duration of the prison sentence imposed in the judgment, if the crime were serious, and between one and five years, if it were less serious. In this case, the prison sentence and the aforementioned prohibitions shall necessarily be served by the convicted person simultaneously.
2. In the cases of the crimes mentioned in the first paragraph of section 1 of this article committed against a person who is or has been the spouse, or against a person who is or has been linked to the convicted person by an analogous relationship of affection even without cohabitation, or against descendants, ascendants or siblings by nature, adoption or affinity, of the convicted person or of the spouse or cohabitant, or against minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection who live with him or who are subject to the authority, guardianship, curatorship, foster care or de facto custody of the spouse or cohabitant, or against a person protected in any other relationship by which he or she is integrated into the nucleus of his family cohabitation, as well as against persons who by reason of their special vulnerability are subject to his custody or care in public or private centres, the application of the penalty provided for in section 2 of Article 48 shall be ordered in all cases, for a time that shall not exceed ten years if the crime were serious, or five if it were less serious, without prejudice to the provisions of the second paragraph of the preceding section.
3. The prohibitions established in Article 48 may also be imposed, for a period of time that shall not exceed six months, for the commission of the crimes mentioned in the first paragraph of section 1 of this article that have the consideration of minor crimes.

Section 6 Common provisions

Article 58

1. The time of deprivation of liberty suffered provisionally shall be credited in its entirety by the sentencing Judge or Court for the fulfilment of the sentence or sentences imposed in the case in which said deprivation was ordered, except insofar as it has coincided with any deprivation of liberty imposed on the convicted person in another case, which has been credited or is creditable to him in that case. In no case may the same period of deprivation of liberty be credited in more than one case.
2. The crediting of provisional imprisonment in a case other than that in which it was ordered shall be agreed ex officio or at the request of the convicted person and after verification that it has not been credited in another case, by the Penitentiary Supervision Judge of the jurisdiction on which the penitentiary centre in which the convicted person is located depends, after hearing the public prosecutor.

3. The crediting of provisional imprisonment suffered in another case shall only proceed when said precautionary measure is subsequent to the criminal facts that motivated the sentence to which it is intended to be credited.
4. The foregoing rules shall also apply with respect to deprivations of rights ordered as a precautionary measure.

Article 59

When the precautionary measures suffered and the sentence imposed are of a different nature, the Judge or Court shall order that the sentence imposed be deemed executed in that part which it considers compensated.

Article 60

1. When, after a final judgment has been pronounced, a lasting situation of serious mental disorder is found in the convicted person that prevents him from understanding the meaning of the sentence, the Penitentiary Supervision Judge shall suspend the execution of the custodial sentence that has been imposed on him, ensuring that he receives the necessary medical assistance, for which purpose he may order the imposition of a custodial security measure of those provided for in this Code which may not, in any case, be more onerous than the sentence replaced. If it were a sentence of a different nature, the Penitentiary Supervision Judge shall assess whether the situation of the convicted person allows him to understand the meaning of the sentence and, where appropriate, shall suspend the execution by imposing the security measures he deems necessary.
The Supervision Judge shall communicate to the public prosecutor, with sufficient advance notice, the imminent expiry of the sentence or security measure imposed, for the purposes of the provisions of the first additional provision of this Code.
2. Once the mental health of the convicted person is restored, he shall serve the sentence if the sentence has not prescribed, without prejudice to the Judge or Court being able, for reasons of equity, to consider the sentence extinguished or reduce its duration, to the extent that the fulfilment of the sentence proves unnecessary or counterproductive.

CHAPTER II

On the application of sentences

Section 1 General rules for the application of sentences

Article 61

When the Law establishes a sentence, it is understood to impose it on the perpetrators of the completed offence.

Article 62

The perpetrators of an attempted crime shall be imposed the sentence lower by one or two degrees than that indicated by the Law for the completed crime, to the extent deemed appropriate, having regard to the danger inherent in the attempt and the degree of execution achieved.

Article 63

Accomplices to a completed or attempted crime shall be imposed the sentence lower by one degree than that fixed by the Law for the perpetrators of the same crime.

Article 64

The foregoing rules shall not apply in cases where the attempt and complicity are specially punished by the Law.

Article 65

1. Aggravating or mitigating circumstances that consist of any cause of a personal nature shall aggravate or mitigate the liability only of those in whom they are present.
2. Those that consist of the material execution of the act or the means employed to carry it out shall serve only to aggravate or mitigate the liability of those who have had knowledge of them at the time of the action or of their cooperation in the crime.
3. When the conditions, qualities or personal relationships that ground the culpability of the perpetrator are not present in the inducer or necessary cooperator, the judges or courts may impose the sentence lower by one degree than that indicated by the law for the offence in question.

Article 66

1. In the application of the penalty, in the case of intentional offences, judges or courts shall observe, depending on whether or not there are mitigating or aggravating circumstances, the following rules:
1. When only one mitigating circumstance is present, they shall apply the penalty in the lower half of that established by law for the offence.
2. When two or more mitigating circumstances are present, or one or more highly qualified ones, and no aggravating circumstance is present, they shall apply the penalty one or two degrees lower than that established by law, having regard to the number and nature of such mitigating circumstances.
3. When only one or two aggravating circumstances are present, they shall apply the penalty in the upper half of that established by law for the offence.
4. When more than two aggravating circumstances are present and no mitigating circumstance is present, they may apply the penalty one degree higher than that established by law, in its lower half.
5. When the aggravating circumstance of recidivism is present with the qualification that the offender, when committing the offence, had been finally convicted of at least three offences included in the same title of this Code, provided they are of the same nature, they may apply the penalty one degree higher than that provided by law for the offence in question, taking into account the previous convictions, as well as the seriousness of the new offence committed.
For the purposes of this rule, criminal records that have been cancelled or should be cancelled shall not be counted.
6. When neither mitigating nor aggravating circumstances are present, they shall apply the penalty established by law for the offence committed, to the extent they deem appropriate, having regard to the personal circumstances of the offender and the greater or lesser seriousness of the act.
7. When mitigating and aggravating circumstances are present, they shall assess and rationally balance them for the individualisation of the penalty. In the event that a qualified basis for mitigation persists, they shall apply the penalty one degree lower. If a qualified basis for aggravation remains, they shall apply the penalty in its upper half.
8. When judges or courts apply the penalty more than one degree lower, they may do so to its full extent.
2. In minor offences and in negligent offences, judges or courts shall apply the penalties at their prudent discretion, without being subject to the rules prescribed in the preceding paragraph.

Article 66 bis

In the application of penalties imposed on legal persons, the provisions of rules 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7 and 8 of the first paragraph of Article 66 shall apply, as well as the following:
1. In cases where they are established by the provisions of Book II, in deciding on the imposition and extent of the penalties provided for in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33, the following shall be taken into account:
a) Their necessity to prevent the continuity of the criminal activity or its effects.
b) Their economic and social consequences, and especially the effects on workers.

c) The position in the structure of the legal person occupied by the natural person or body that breached the duty of control.
2. When the penalties provided for in letters c) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33 are imposed for a limited duration, it may not exceed the maximum duration of the custodial penalty provided for in the event that the offence was committed by a natural person.
For the imposition of the sanctions provided for in letters c) to g) for a period exceeding two years, one of the following two circumstances must be present:
a) That the legal person is a repeat offender.
b) That the legal person is used instrumentally for the commission of criminal offences. It shall be understood that this latter case exists whenever the legal activity of the legal person is less relevant than its illegal activity.
When the liability of the legal person, in the cases provided for in letter b) of paragraph 1 of Article 31 bis, derives from a breach of the duties of supervision, monitoring and control that is not of a serious nature, these penalties shall in any case have a maximum duration of two years.
For the permanent imposition of the sanctions provided for in letters b) and e), and for the imposition for a period exceeding five years of those provided for in letters e) and
f) of paragraph 7 of Article 33, one of the following two circumstances must be present:
a) That the factual situation provided for in rule 5 of paragraph 1 of Article 66 exists.
b) That the legal person is used instrumentally for the commission of criminal offences. It shall be understood that this latter case exists whenever the legal activity of the legal person is less relevant than its illegal activity.

Article 67

The rules of the preceding article shall not apply to aggravating or mitigating circumstances that the law has taken into account in describing or sanctioning an offence, nor to those that are so inherent to the offence that without their presence it could not be committed.

Article 68

In the cases provided for in the first circumstance of Article 21, judges or courts shall impose the penalty one or two degrees lower than that indicated by law, having regard to the number and nature of the requirements that are lacking or present, and the personal circumstances of its author, without prejudice to the application of Article 66 of this Code.

Article 69

To a person over eighteen years of age and under twenty-one who commits a criminal act, the provisions of the law regulating the criminal liability of minors may be applied in the cases and with the requirements provided therein.

Article 70

1. The penalty one degree higher or lower than that provided by law for any offence shall have the extent resulting from the application of the following rules:
1. The penalty one degree higher shall be formed starting from the maximum figure indicated by law for the offence in question and adding to it half of its amount, the resulting sum constituting its maximum limit. The minimum limit of the penalty one degree higher shall be the maximum of the penalty indicated by law for the offence in question, increased by one day or one day-fine according to the nature of the penalty to be imposed.
2. The penalty one degree lower shall be formed starting from the minimum figure indicated for the offence in question and deducting from it half of its amount, the result of such deduction constituting its minimum limit. The maximum limit of the penalty one degree lower shall be the

minimum of the penalty indicated by law for the offence in question, reduced by one day or one day-fine according to the nature of the penalty to be imposed.
2. For the purposes of determining the upper or lower half of the penalty or of specifying the penalty one degree lower or higher, the day or day-fine shall be considered indivisible and shall act as penological units of more or less, as the case may be.
3. When, in the application of rule 1 of paragraph 1 of this article, the penalty one degree higher exceeds the maximum limits fixed for each penalty in this Code, the following shall be considered as immediately higher:
1. If the penalty determined were imprisonment, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 30 years.
2. If it were absolute or special disqualification, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 30 years.
3. If it were suspension from employment or public office, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be eight years.
4. In the case of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 15 years.
5. In the case of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.
6. In the case of deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or to go to them, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.
7. In the case of prohibition from approaching the victim or those of his or her relatives or other persons determined by the judge or court, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.
