8. In the case of prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of his or her relatives or other persons determined by the judge or court, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.
9. If it were a fine, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 30 months.
4. The penalty one degree lower than permanent imprisonment is the penalty of imprisonment from twenty to thirty years.

Article 71

1. In determining the penalty one degree lower, judges or courts shall not be limited by the minimum amounts indicated by law for each class of penalty, but may reduce them in the manner resulting from the application of the corresponding rule.
2. Notwithstanding, when by application of the preceding rules it is appropriate to impose a penalty of imprisonment of less than three months, this shall in all cases be replaced by a fine, community service, or permanent location, even if the law does not provide for these penalties for the offence in question, replacing each day of imprisonment with two fine units or one day of work or one day of permanent location.

Article 72

Judges or courts, in the application of the penalty, in accordance with the rules contained in this chapter, shall give reasons in the judgment for the degree and specific extent of that imposed.

Section 2 Special rules for the application of penalties

Article 73

The person responsible for two or more offences shall be imposed all the penalties corresponding to the various infractions for their simultaneous enforcement, if possible, by the nature and effects thereof.

Article 74

1. Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding article, a person who, in execution of a preconceived plan or taking advantage of an identical occasion, carries out a plurality of actions or

omissions that offend one or more subjects and infringe the same criminal provision or provisions of the same or similar nature, shall be punished as the author of a continuing offence with the penalty indicated for the most serious infraction, which shall be imposed in its upper half, and may reach up to the lower half of the penalty one degree higher.
2. If they were infractions against property, the penalty shall be imposed taking into account the total damage caused. In these infractions the Judge or Court shall impose, with reasons given, the penalty one or two degrees higher, to the extent it deems appropriate, if the act were of notorious gravity and had harmed a generality of persons.
3. Excluded from the provisions of the preceding paragraphs are offences against eminently personal interests, except those constituting infractions against honour and sexual freedom and indemnity that affect the same victim. In these cases, the nature of the act and of the provision infringed shall be taken into account in order to apply or not apply criminal continuity.

Article 75

When all or some of the penalties corresponding to the various infractions cannot be served simultaneously by the convicted person, the order of their respective gravity shall be followed for their successive enforcement, insofar as possible.

Article 76

1. Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding article, the maximum effective enforcement of the sentence of the guilty party may not exceed triple the time for which the most serious of the penalties incurred is imposed, declaring extinguished those that proceed from the time when those already imposed cover said maximum, which may not exceed 20 years. Exceptionally, this maximum limit shall be:
a) 25 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offences and one of them is punishable by law with a penalty of imprisonment of up to 20 years.
b) 30 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offences and one of them is punishable by law with a penalty of imprisonment exceeding 20 years.
c) 40 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offences and at least two of them are punishable by law with a penalty of imprisonment exceeding 20 years.
d) 40 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offences relating to terrorist organisations and groups and offences of terrorism under Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code and one of them is punishable by law with a penalty of imprisonment exceeding 20 years.
e) When the subject has been convicted of two or more offences and at least one of them is punishable by law with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment, the provisions of articles 92 and 78 bis shall apply.
2. The limitation shall apply even if the penalties have been imposed in different proceedings when they have been imposed for acts committed before the date on which those that, being the subject of accumulation, had been tried first were tried.

Article 77

1.	The provisions of the two preceding articles are not applicable in the case where a single act constitutes two or more offences, or when one of them is a necessary means to commit the other.
2.	In the first case, the penalty provided for the most serious infringement shall be applied in its upper half, without exceeding that which represents the sum of those that would correspond if the infractions were punished separately. When the penalty thus computed exceeds this limit, the infractions shall be punished separately.
3.	In the second case, a penalty greater than that which would have corresponded, in the specific case, for the most serious infringement shall be imposed, and which may not exceed the sum of the specific penalties that would have been imposed separately for each of the offences. Within these limits, the judge or court shall individualize the penalty in accordance with the criteria expressed

in article 66. In any case, the penalty imposed may not exceed the duration limit provided for in the preceding article.

Article 78

1.	If as a consequence of the limitations established in paragraph 1 of article 76 the penalty to be served were to be less than half of the total sum of those imposed, the sentencing judge or court may agree that penitentiary benefits, exit permits, classification in third grade and the computation of time for conditional release refer to the totality of the penalties imposed in the sentences.
2.	In these cases, the supervisory judge, following an individualized and favorable prognosis of social reintegration and assessing, where appropriate, the personal circumstances of the offender and the evolution of the re-educational treatment, may reasonably agree, having heard the Public Prosecutor's Office, Penitentiary Institutions and the other parties, the application of the general regime of compliance.
If these were offences relating to terrorist organizations and groups and terrorism offences of Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, or committed within criminal organizations, and taking into account the total sum of the penalties imposed, the foregoing possibility shall only be applicable:
a)	To third grade penitentiary status, when one fifth of the maximum limit of compliance of the sentence remains to be served.
b)	To conditional release, when one eighth of the maximum limit of compliance of the sentence remains to be served.

Article 78 bis

1.	When the subject has been convicted of two or more offences and at least one of them is punishable by law with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment, progression to third grade shall require compliance with:
a)	a minimum of eighteen years of imprisonment, when the convicted person has been convicted of several offences, one of them is punishable with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment and the rest of the penalties imposed total more than five years.
b)	a minimum of twenty years of imprisonment, when the convicted person has been convicted of several offences, one of them is punishable with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment and the rest of the penalties imposed total more than fifteen years.
c)	a minimum of twenty-two years of imprisonment, when the convicted person has been convicted of several offences and two or more of them are punishable with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment, or one of them is punishable with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment and the rest of the penalties imposed total twenty-five years or more.
2.	In these cases, the suspension of execution of the remainder of the penalty shall require that the convicted person has served:
a)	A minimum of twenty-five years of imprisonment, in the cases referred to in letters a) and b) of the preceding paragraph.
b)	A minimum of thirty years of imprisonment in that of letter c) of the preceding paragraph.
3.	If these were offences relating to terrorist organizations and groups and terrorism offences of Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, or committed within criminal organizations, the minimum limits of compliance for access to third grade classification shall be twenty-four years of imprisonment, in the cases referred to in letters a) and b) of the first paragraph, and thirty-two years of imprisonment in that of letter c) of the first paragraph.
In these cases, the suspension of execution of the remainder of the penalty shall require that the convicted person has served a minimum of twenty-eight years of imprisonment, in the cases referred to in letters a) and b) of the first paragraph, and thirty-five years of imprisonment in that of letter b)(*) of the first paragraph.

(*) We understand that this refers to letter c) of the first paragraph.

Article 79

Whenever Judges or Courts impose a penalty that carries other accessory penalties, they shall also expressly condemn the offender to the latter.

CHAPTER III

On substitute forms of execution of custodial penalties and conditional release.

Section 1 On the suspension of the execution of custodial sentences

Article 80

1.	Judges or courts, by reasoned decision, may suspend the execution of custodial sentences not exceeding two years when it is reasonable to expect that the execution of the sentence will not be necessary to prevent the convicted person from committing future crimes.
To adopt this decision, the judge or court shall assess the circumstances of the crime committed, the personal circumstances of the convicted person, their record, their conduct after the event, in particular their effort to repair the damage caused, their family and social circumstances, and the effects that may be expected from the suspension of execution itself and from compliance with the measures that may be imposed.
2.	The following shall be necessary conditions for suspending the execution of the sentence:
1.	ª That the convicted person has offended for the first time. For this purpose, previous convictions for negligent crimes or for minor crimes shall not be taken into account, nor shall criminal records that have been cancelled, or should be cancelled in accordance with the provisions of article 136. Nor shall criminal records corresponding to crimes that, by their nature or circumstances, lack relevance for assessing the probability of committing future crimes be taken into account.
2.	ª That the sentence or the sum of those imposed does not exceed two years, not including in such calculation that derived from non-payment of the fine.
3.	ª That the civil liabilities that may have arisen have been satisfied and that the confiscation ordered in the judgment pursuant to article 127 has been made effective.
This requirement shall be deemed fulfilled when the convicted person assumes the commitment to satisfy the civil liabilities according to their economic capacity and to facilitate the agreed confiscation, and it is reasonable to expect that it will be fulfilled within the prudent period determined by the judge or court. The judge or court, having regard to the scope of the civil liability and the social impact of the crime, may request the guarantees it considers appropriate to ensure its fulfilment.
3.	Exceptionally, even if conditions 1.ª and 2.ª of the preceding paragraph do not concur, and provided that they are not habitual offenders, the suspension of prison sentences that individually do not exceed two years may be agreed when the personal circumstances of the offender, the nature of the act, their conduct and, in particular, the effort to repair the damage caused, so advise.
In these cases, the suspension shall always be conditioned on the effective reparation of the damage
or compensation for the harm caused according to their physical and economic possibilities,
or on compliance with the agreement referred to in measure 1.ª of article 84. Likewise, one of the measures referred to in numerals 2.ª or 3.ª of the same provision shall always be imposed, with an extent that may not be less than that resulting from applying the conversion criteria set out therein on one-fifth of the sentence imposed.
4.	Judges and courts may grant the suspension of any sentence imposed without being subject to any requirement in the event that the convicted person suffers from a very serious illness with incurable suffering, unless at the time of committing the crime they already had another sentence suspended for the same reason.
5.	Even when conditions 1.ª and 2.ª provided for in paragraph 2 of this article do not concur, the judge or court may agree to suspend the execution of custodial sentences

not exceeding five years for convicted persons who have committed the criminal act because of their dependence on the substances indicated in numeral 2.º of article 20, provided that it is sufficiently certified, by a duly accredited or approved public or private centre or service, that the convicted person is rehabilitated or undergoing treatment for that purpose at the time of deciding on the suspension.
The judge or court may order the necessary verifications to be carried out to verify compliance with the above requirements.
In the event that the convicted person is undergoing rehabilitation treatment, the suspension of the execution of the sentence shall also be conditioned on their not abandoning the treatment until its completion. Relapses in treatment shall not be understood as abandonment if they do not evidence a definitive abandonment of the rehabilitation treatment.
6.	In crimes that can only be prosecuted following a complaint or criminal charge by the victim, judges and courts shall hear the victim and, where appropriate, their representative, before granting the benefits of suspension of execution of the sentence.

Article 81

The suspension period shall be from two to five years for custodial sentences not exceeding two years, and from three months to one year for minor sentences, and shall be set by the judge or court, taking into account the criteria expressed in the second paragraph of paragraph 1 of article 80.
In the event that the suspension has been agreed in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 5 of the preceding article, the suspension period shall be from three to five years.

Article 82

1.	The judge or court shall decide in the judgment on the suspension of the execution of the sentence whenever possible. In other cases, once the judgment has become final, it shall rule with the greatest urgency, after hearing the parties, on whether or not to grant the suspension of the execution of the sentence.
2.	The suspension period shall be calculated from the date of the decision granting it. If the suspension has been agreed in the judgment, the suspension period shall be calculated from the date on which it became final.
The period during which the convicted person has remained in a situation of absconding shall not be counted as a suspension period.

Article 83

1.	The judge or court may condition the suspension on compliance with the following prohibitions and duties when this is necessary to avoid the danger of commission of new offences, provided that duties and obligations that are excessive and disproportionate may not be imposed:
1.	ª Prohibition on approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, their homes, their places of work or other places habitually frequented by them, or on communicating with them by any means. The imposition of this prohibition shall always be communicated to the persons in relation to whom it is agreed.
2.	ª Prohibition on establishing contact with certain persons or with members of a certain group, when there are indications that allow a well-founded supposition that such subjects may provide them with the opportunity to commit new offences or incite them to do so.
3.	ª Maintaining their place of residence in a certain place with prohibition on leaving it or being temporarily absent without authorisation from the judge or court.
4.	ª Prohibition on residing in a certain place or going to it, when in those places they may find the opportunity or motive to commit new offences.
5.	ª Appearing personally with the frequency determined before the judge or court, police units or administrative service determined, to report on their activities and justify them.

6.	ª Participating in training, employment, cultural, road safety education, sexual education, environmental protection, animal protection, equal treatment and non-discrimination, peaceful conflict resolution, positive parenting and other similar programmes.
7.	ª Participating in programmes for breaking addiction to alcohol, toxic drugs or narcotic substances, or for treatment of other addictive behaviours.
8.	ª Prohibition on driving motor vehicles that do not have technological devices that condition their ignition or operation on prior verification of the physical conditions of the driver, when the subject has been convicted of an offence against road safety and the measure is necessary to prevent the possible commission of new offences.
9.	ª Complying with other duties that the judge or court deems appropriate for the social rehabilitation of the convicted person, with their prior consent, provided that they do not violate their dignity as a person.
2.	When dealing with offences committed against a woman by whoever is or has been her spouse, or by whoever is or has been linked to her by a similar relationship of affection, even without cohabitation, the prohibitions and duties indicated in rules 1.ª, 4.ª and 6.ª of the preceding paragraph shall always be imposed.
The foregoing prohibitions and duties shall also be imposed when dealing with offences against sexual freedom, forced marriage, female genital mutilation and trafficking in human beings.
3.	The imposition of any of the prohibitions or duties of rules 1.ª, 2.ª, 3.ª, or
4.	ª of paragraph 1 of this article shall be communicated to the State Security Forces and Corps, which shall ensure their compliance. Any possible breach or circumstance relevant to assessing the dangerousness of the convicted person and the possibility of future commission of new offences shall be immediately communicated to the Public Prosecutor's Office and to the enforcement judge or court.
4.	Control of compliance with the duties referred to in rules 6.ª, 7.ª and 8.ª of paragraph 1 of this article shall correspond to the alternative sentence and measure management services of the Prison Administration. These services shall report to the enforcement judge or court on compliance at least quarterly, in the case of rules 6.ª and 8.ª, and half-yearly, in the case of 7.ª and, in any case, upon its conclusion.
They shall also immediately report any circumstance relevant to assessing the dangerousness of the convicted person and the possibility of future commission of new offences, as well as breaches of the obligation imposed or its effective compliance.

Article 84

1.	The judge or court may also condition the suspension of execution of the sentence on compliance with one or more of the following services or measures:
1.	ª Compliance with the agreement reached by the parties by virtue of mediation.
2.	ª Payment of a fine, the extent of which shall be determined by the judge or court in view of the circumstances of the case, which may not exceed that resulting from applying two fine units for each day of imprisonment on a maximum limit of two thirds of its duration.
3.	ª Performance of community service, especially when it is appropriate as a form of symbolic reparation in view of the circumstances of the act and the perpetrator. The duration of this service shall be determined by the judge or court in view of the circumstances of the case, without exceeding that resulting from computing one day of work for each day of imprisonment on a maximum limit of two thirds of its duration.
2.	If it had been an offence committed against a woman by whoever is or has been her spouse, or by whoever is or has been linked to her by a similar relationship of affection, even without cohabitation, or against descendants, ascendants or siblings by nature, adoption or affinity of their own or of the spouse or cohabitant, or against minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection who live with them or who are subject to the authority, guardianship, curatorship, fostering or de facto care of the spouse or cohabitant, payment of the fine referred to in measure 2.ª of the preceding paragraph

may only be imposed when it is proven that between them there are no economic relations derived from a marital relationship, cohabitation or filiation, or from the existence of common descendants.

Article 85

During the time of suspension of the sentence, and in view of the possible modification of the circumstances assessed, the judge or court may modify the decision previously adopted in accordance with articles 83 and 84, and agree to the lifting of all or some of the prohibitions, duties or services that had been agreed, their modification or substitution by others that are less onerous.

Article 86

1.	The judge or court shall revoke the suspension and order the execution of the sentence when the convicted person:
a)	Is convicted of an offence committed during the suspension period and this reveals that the expectation on which the decision to suspend was based can no longer be maintained.
b)	Seriously or repeatedly fails to comply with the prohibitions and duties that have been imposed pursuant to Article 83, or evades the control of the alternative sentence and measure management services of the Prison Administration.
c)	Seriously or repeatedly fails to comply with the conditions that have been imposed for the suspension pursuant to Article 84.
d)	Provides inaccurate or insufficient information about the whereabouts of assets or objects whose confiscation has been ordered; fails to fulfil the commitment to pay the civil liabilities for which he or she has been convicted, unless he or she lacks the economic capacity to do so; or provides inaccurate or insufficient information about his or her assets, failing to comply with the obligation imposed in Article 589 of the Civil Procedure Act.
2.	If the failure to comply with the prohibitions, duties or conditions has not been serious or repeated, the judge or court may:
a)	Impose new prohibitions, duties or conditions on the convicted person, or modify those already imposed.
b)	Extend the suspension period, provided that in no case may it exceed half the duration of that which was initially fixed.
3.	In the case of revocation of the suspension, the expenses that the convicted person has incurred to repair the damage caused by the offence pursuant to paragraph 1 of Article 84 shall not be reimbursed. However, the judge or court shall credit against the sentence the payments and the provision of work that have been made or performed pursuant to measures 2 and 3.
4.	In all the above cases, the judge or court shall rule after having heard the Prosecutor and the other parties. However, he or she may revoke the suspension of the execution of the sentence and order the immediate imprisonment of the convicted person when it is essential to avoid the risk of repeat offending, the risk of the convicted person absconding or to ensure the protection of the victim.
The judge or court may order the carrying out of such verification proceedings as may be necessary and order the holding of an oral hearing when he or she considers it necessary in order to rule.

Article 87

1.	Once the fixed suspension period has elapsed without the subject having committed an offence that reveals that the expectation on which the decision to suspend was based can no longer be maintained, and the rules of conduct fixed by the judge or court having been sufficiently complied with, the latter shall order the remission of the sentence.
2.	Nevertheless, in order to grant the remission of the sentence that has been suspended pursuant to paragraph 5 of Article 80, proof must be provided of the subject's rehabilitation or the continuation of treatment. Otherwise, the judge or court shall order its enforcement,

unless, having heard the relevant reports, he or she considers the continuation of treatment necessary; in such case he or she may grant, with reasons, an extension of the suspension period for a time not exceeding two years.

Section 2 On the substitution of custodial sentences

Article 88

(Deleted).

Article 89

1. Prison sentences of more than one year imposed on a foreign national shall be replaced by expulsion from Spanish territory. Exceptionally, when necessary to ensure the defence of the legal order and restore confidence in the validity of the rule infringed by the offence, the judge or court may order the execution of a part of the sentence that may not exceed two thirds of its length, and the substitution of the remainder by the expulsion of the convicted person from Spanish territory. In all cases, the remainder of the sentence shall be replaced by the expulsion of the convicted person from Spanish territory when he or she accesses the third grade or is granted conditional release.
2. When a prison sentence of more than five years has been imposed, or several sentences exceeding that duration, the judge or court shall order the execution of all or part of the sentence, to the extent necessary to ensure the defence of the legal order and restore confidence in the validity of the rule infringed by the offence. In these cases, the execution of the remainder of the sentence shall be replaced by the expulsion of the convicted person from Spanish territory, when the convicted person serves the part of the sentence that has been determined, accesses the third grade or is granted conditional release.
3. The judge or court shall rule in the judgment on the substitution of the execution of the sentence whenever possible. In other cases, once the judgment becomes final, it shall rule with the greatest urgency, after hearing the Public Prosecutor and the other parties, on whether or not to grant the substitution of the execution of the sentence.
4. Substitution shall not proceed when, in view of the circumstances of the act and the personal circumstances of the perpetrator, in particular his or her roots in Spain, expulsion would be disproportionate.
The expulsion of a citizen of the European Union shall only proceed when it represents a serious threat to public order or public security having regard to the nature, circumstances and seriousness of the offence committed, his or her background and personal circumstances.
If he or she has resided in Spain for the previous ten years, expulsion shall proceed when in addition:
a) He or she has been convicted of one or more offences against life, liberty, physical integrity and sexual liberty and indemnity punishable by a maximum prison sentence of more than five years and there is a well-founded assessment of a serious risk that he or she may commit offences of the same nature.
b) He or she has been convicted of one or more offences of terrorism or other offences committed within a group or criminal organisation.
In these cases, the provisions of paragraph 2 of this article shall in all cases apply.
5. The foreign national may not return to Spain for a period of five to ten years, counted from the date of his or her expulsion, having regard to the duration of the substituted sentence and the personal circumstances of the convicted person.
6. Expulsion shall entail the closure of any administrative procedure whose purpose was authorisation to reside or work in Spain.
7. If the expelled foreign national returns to Spain before the judicially established period of time has elapsed, he or she shall serve the sentences that were substituted, unless, exceptionally, the judge or court reduces their duration when their service proves unnecessary to ensure the defence of the legal order and restore confidence in the

legal rule infringed by the offence, having regard to the time elapsed since the expulsion and the circumstances in which its breach has occurred.
However, if he or she is caught at the border, he or she shall be expelled directly by the governmental authority, and the period of prohibition of entry shall begin to run again in its entirety.
8. When, upon ordering expulsion in any of the cases provided for in this article, the foreign national is not or does not remain effectively deprived of liberty in execution of the sentence imposed, the judge or court may order, in order to ensure expulsion, his or her admission to a foreign national detention centre, under the terms and with the limits and guarantees provided for in the law for governmental expulsion.
In all cases, if having ordered the substitution of the custodial sentence by expulsion, the latter cannot be carried out, the originally imposed sentence or the pending period of sentence shall be executed, or the suspension of its execution shall be applied, as appropriate.
9. Sentences imposed for the commission of the offences referred to in articles 177 bis, 312, 313 and 318 bis shall not be substituted.

Section 3 On conditional release

Article 90

1.	The prison supervision judge shall order the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the prison sentence and grant conditional release to the convicted person who meets the following requirements:
a)	That they are classified in third grade.
b)	That they have served three-quarters of the sentence imposed.
c)	That they have observed good conduct.
In order to decide on the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and the granting of conditional release, the prison supervision judge shall assess the personality of the convicted person, their background, the circumstances of the offence committed, the importance of the legal interests that could be affected by a repetition of the offence, their conduct during the serving of the sentence, their family and social circumstances and the effects that may be expected from the suspension of the execution itself and from compliance with the measures that may be imposed.
Suspension shall not be granted if the convicted person has not satisfied the civil liability arising from the offence in the cases and in accordance with the criteria established by paragraphs 5 and 6 of Article 72 of Organic Law 1/1979, of 26 September, General Penitentiary Law.
2.	The suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence may also be ordered and conditional release granted to convicted persons who meet the following requirements:
a)	That they have served two-thirds of their sentence.
b)	That during the serving of their sentence they have carried out work, cultural or occupational activities, either continuously or with such benefit as has resulted in a relevant and favourable modification of those of their personal circumstances related to their previous criminal activity.
c)	That they prove compliance with the requirements referred to in the preceding paragraph, except for having served three-quarters of their sentence.
At the proposal of Penitentiary Institutions and following a report from the Public Prosecutor's Office and the other parties, once the circumstances of letters a) and c) of the preceding paragraph have been met, the prison supervision judge may bring forward, once half of the sentence has been served, the granting of conditional release in relation to the period provided for in the preceding paragraph, up to a maximum of ninety days for each year elapsed of actual serving of the sentence. This measure shall require that the convicted person has continuously carried out the activities indicated in letter b) of this paragraph and that they also prove effective and favourable participation in victim reparation programmes or treatment or detoxification programmes, as the case may be.

3.	Exceptionally, the prison supervision judge may order the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and grant conditional release to convicted persons in whom the following requirements are met:
a)	That they are serving their first prison sentence and that it does not exceed three years in duration.
b)	That they have served half of their sentence.
c)	That they prove compliance with the requirements referred to in paragraph 1, except for having served three-quarters of their sentence, as well as that regulated in letter b) of the preceding paragraph.
This regime shall not be applicable to persons convicted of the commission of an offence against sexual freedom and integrity.
4.	The prison supervision judge may refuse the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence when the convicted person has given inaccurate or insufficient information about the whereabouts of assets or objects whose confiscation has been ordered; does not comply according to their capacity with the commitment to pay the civil liabilities to which they have been sentenced; or provides inaccurate or insufficient information about their assets, failing to comply with the obligation imposed in Article 589 of the Civil Procedure Act.
The suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence imposed for any of the offences provided for in Title XIX of Book II of this Code may also be refused when the convicted person has evaded compliance with the pecuniary liabilities or the reparation of the economic damage caused to the Administration to which they have been sentenced.
5.	In cases of suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and granting of conditional release, the rules contained in Articles 83, 86 and 87 shall apply.
The prison supervision judge, in view of the possible modification of the circumstances assessed, may modify the decision previously adopted in accordance with Article 83, and order the imposition of new prohibitions, duties or obligations, the modification of those already agreed or the lifting thereof.
Likewise, the prison supervision judge shall revoke the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and the conditional release granted when a change in the circumstances that gave rise to the suspension becomes apparent that no longer allows the prognosis of lack of dangerousness on which the decision adopted was based to be maintained.
The period of suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence shall be from two to five years. In any case, the period of suspension of execution and conditional release may not be less than the duration of the part of the sentence pending compliance. The period of suspension and conditional release shall be computed from the date of release of the convicted person.
6.	The revocation of the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and conditional release shall give rise to the execution of the part of the sentence pending compliance. The time spent on conditional release shall not be counted as time served of the sentence.
7.	The prison supervision judge shall rule ex officio on the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and granting of conditional release at the request of the convicted person. In the event that the request is not granted, the judge or court may set a period of six months, which may be extended on reasoned grounds to one year, until the claim may be raised again.
8.	In the case of persons convicted of offences committed within criminal organisations or of any of the offences regulated in Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence imposed and granting of conditional release requires that the convicted person show unequivocal signs of having abandoned the aims and means of terrorist activity and has actively collaborated with the authorities, either to prevent the commission of other offences by the terrorist organisation or group, or to mitigate the effects of their offence, or for the identification, capture and prosecution of those responsible for terrorist offences, to obtain evidence or to prevent the action or development of the organisations or associations to which they have belonged or with which they have collaborated, which may be proven by an express declaration of repudiation of their criminal activities and abandonment of violence and an express request for forgiveness from the victims of their offence, as well as by

technical reports proving that the prisoner is truly disassociated from the terrorist organisation and from the environment and activities of illegal associations and groups that surround it and their collaboration with the authorities.
Paragraphs 2 and 3 shall not be applicable to persons convicted of the commission of any of the offences regulated in Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code or of offences committed within criminal organisations.

Article 91

1. Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding article, convicted persons who have reached the age of seventy years, or reach it during the serving of the sentence, and meet the requirements set out in the preceding article, except that of having served three-quarters thereof, two-thirds or, as the case may be, half of the sentence, may obtain suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and the granting of conditional release.
The same criterion shall apply in the case of very seriously ill persons with incurable ailments, provided this is established following the medical reports that, in the opinion of the prison supervision judge, are deemed necessary.
2. When the prison administration is aware that the inmate is in any of the cases provided for in the preceding paragraphs, it shall submit the conditional release file, with the urgency required by the case, to the prison supervision judge, who, when deciding on it, shall assess together with the personal circumstances the difficulty in committing crimes and the low dangerousness of the subject.
3. If the danger to the life of the inmate, due to his illness or advanced age, is patent, as established by the opinion of the forensic doctor and the medical services of the prison establishment, the judge or court may, without the need to prove compliance with any other requirement and having assessed the lack of relevant dangerousness of the convicted person, order the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and grant him conditional release without further procedure than requesting from the prison centre the final prognosis report in order to be able to make the assessment referred to in the preceding paragraph.
In this case, the convicted person shall be obliged to provide the prison medical service, the forensic doctor, or any other person determined by the judge or court, with the information necessary to assess the evolution of his illness.
Failure to comply with this obligation may give rise to the revocation of the suspension of execution and conditional release.
4. The provisions contained in paragraphs 4, 5 and 6 of the preceding article are applicable to the case regulated in this article.

Article 92

1. The court shall order the suspension of the execution of the sentence of reviewable permanent imprisonment when the following requirements are met:
a) That the convicted person has served twenty-five years of his sentence, without prejudice to the provisions of Article 78 bis for the cases regulated therein.
b) That he is classified in third grade.
c) That the court, in view of the personality of the convicted person, his background, the circumstances of the crime committed, the relevance of the legal interests that could be affected by a repetition of the crime, his conduct during the serving of the sentence, his family and social circumstances, and the effects that may be expected from the suspension of execution itself and from compliance with the measures that may be imposed, can establish, following assessment of the progress reports sent by the prison centre and by those specialists that the court itself determines, the existence of a favourable prognosis for social reintegration.
In the event that the convicted person has been convicted of several crimes, the examination of the requirements referred to in letter c) shall be carried out by assessing all the crimes committed as a whole.

The court shall rule on the suspension of the sentence of reviewable permanent imprisonment following an adversarial oral procedure in which the Public Prosecutor and the convicted person, assisted by his lawyer, shall participate.
2. If the crimes relate to terrorist organisations and groups and terrorism offences under Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, it shall also be necessary for the convicted person to show unequivocal signs of having abandoned the aims and means of terrorist activity and to have actively collaborated with the authorities, either to prevent the commission of other crimes by the terrorist organisation or group, or to mitigate the effects of his crime, or for the identification, capture and prosecution of those responsible for terrorist crimes, to obtain evidence or to prevent the action or development of the organisations or associations to which he has belonged or with which he has collaborated, which may be proven by an express declaration of repudiation of his criminal activities and abandonment of violence and an express request for forgiveness from the victims of his crime, as well as by technical reports proving that the prisoner is truly disassociated from the terrorist organisation and from the environment and activities of illegal associations and groups surrounding it and his collaboration with the authorities.
3. The suspension of execution shall last from five to ten years. The period of suspension and conditional release shall be computed from the date of release of the convicted person. The rules contained in the second paragraph of paragraph 1 of Article 80 and in Articles 83, 86, 87 and 91 are applicable.
The judge or court, in view of the possible modification of the circumstances assessed, may modify the decision previously adopted pursuant to Article 83, and order the imposition of new prohibitions, duties or obligations, the modification of those already agreed, or the lifting thereof.
Likewise, the prison supervision judge shall revoke the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and the conditional release granted when a change in the circumstances that gave rise to the suspension becomes apparent that no longer allows the prognosis of lack of dangerousness on which the decision adopted was based to be maintained.
4. Once the part of the sentence referred to in letter a) of paragraph 1 of this article or, as the case may be, in Article 78 bis has been served, the court must verify, at least every two years, compliance with the remaining requirements for conditional release. The court shall also rule on the convicted person's applications for the granting of conditional release, but may set a period of up to one year within which, after an application has been rejected, no further applications will be processed.

Article 93

(Deleted).

Section 4 Common provisions

Article 94

For the purposes provided for in Section 2 of this chapter, habitual offenders are considered to be those who have committed three or more crimes included in the same chapter, within a period not exceeding five years, and have been convicted thereof.
To make this calculation, on the one hand, the moment of possible suspension or substitution of the sentence pursuant to Article 88 shall be considered and, on the other hand, the date of commission of those crimes that form the basis for the finding of habituality.

Article 94 bis

For the purposes provided for in this Chapter, final convictions by judges or courts imposed in other States of the European Union shall have the same value as those imposed by Spanish judges or courts unless their criminal records have been cancelled, or could be cancelled under Spanish law.

TITLE IV

Security measures

CHAPTER I

Security measures in general

Article 95

1. Security measures shall be applied by the Judge or Court, following such reports as it deems appropriate, to persons who find themselves in the situations provided for in the following chapter of this Code, provided that the following circumstances are present:
1. That the subject has committed an act provided for as a crime.
2. That from the act and the personal circumstances of the subject a prognosis of future behaviour may be deduced that reveals the probability of commission of new crimes.
2. When the penalty that could have been imposed for the crime committed is not custodial, the sentencing judge or court may only order one or some of the measures provided for in article 96.3.

Article 96

1. The security measures that may be imposed in accordance with this Code are custodial and non-custodial.
2. Custodial measures are:
1. Confinement in a psychiatric centre.
2. Confinement in a rehabilitation centre.
3. Confinement in a special educational centre.
3. Non-custodial measures are:
1. Professional disqualification.
2. Expulsion from national territory of foreigners not legally resident in Spain.
3. Supervised release
4. Family custody. The person subject to this measure shall be subject to the care and supervision of the designated family member who accepts custody, who shall exercise it in relation to the Penitentiary Supervision Judge and without prejudice to the educational or employment activities of the person in custody.
5. Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds.
6. Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons.

Article 97

During the execution of the sentence, the sentencing Judge or Court shall adopt, by the procedure established in the following article, one of the following decisions:
a) Maintain the execution of the imposed security measure.
b) Order the cessation of any imposed security measure as soon as the criminal dangerousness of the subject disappears.
c) Substitute a security measure for another that it deems more appropriate, from among those provided for the situation in question. In the event that substitution is ordered and

the subject evolves unfavourably, the substitution shall be rendered without effect, and the substituted measure shall be applied again.
d) Suspend the execution of the measure in view of the result already obtained with its application, for a period not exceeding that remaining until the maximum specified in the sentence that imposed it. The suspension shall be conditional upon the subject not committing an offence during the fixed period, and may be rendered without effect if any of the circumstances provided for in article 95 of this Code is again established.

Article 98

1. For the purposes of the preceding article, when it concerns a custodial security measure or a supervised release measure that must be executed after completion of a custodial sentence, the Penitentiary Supervision Judge shall be obliged to submit at least annually a proposal for maintenance, cessation, substitution or suspension thereof. To formulate such proposal the Penitentiary Supervision Judge must assess the reports issued by the specialists and professionals assisting the person subject to the security measure or by the competent Public Administrations and, where appropriate, the result of other actions ordered for this purpose.
2. When it concerns any other non-custodial measure, the sentencing Judge or Court shall request directly from the Administrations, specialists and professionals referred to in the preceding paragraph, the appropriate reports concerning the situation and evolution of the convicted person, their degree of rehabilitation and the prognosis of recidivism or repeat offending.
3. In all cases, the sentencing Judge or Court shall rule with reasons in view of the proposal or reports referred to respectively in the two preceding paragraphs, having heard the person subject to the measure, as well as the Public Prosecutor and the other parties. Victims of the crime who have not appeared as parties shall also be heard when they have so requested at the beginning or at any time during the execution of the sentence and remain locatable for that purpose.

Article 99

In the case of concurrence of custodial sentences and security measures, the judge or court shall order the completion of the measure, which shall be credited towards that of the sentence. Once the security measure is lifted, the judge or court may, if execution of the sentence would endanger the effects achieved through the measure, suspend completion of the remainder of the sentence for a period not exceeding its duration, or apply any of the measures provided for in article 96.3.

Article 100

1. The breach of a confinement security measure shall result in the judge or court ordering the re-entry of the subject into the same centre from which they had escaped or into another corresponding to their condition.
2. If it concerns other measures, the judge or court may order the substitution of the breached measure for that of confinement if the latter is provided for the situation in question and if the breach demonstrates its necessity.
3. In both cases the Judge or Court shall extract testimony for the breach. For these purposes, the subject's refusal to undergo medical treatment or to continue initially consented medical treatment shall not be considered a breach of the measure. Nevertheless, the Judge or Court may order the substitution of the treatment initially or subsequently rejected for another measure from among those applicable to the situation in question.

CHAPTER II

On the application of security measures

Section 1 On custodial measures

Article 101

1. To the subject who is declared exempt from criminal liability pursuant to number
1. of article 20, there may be applied, if necessary, the measure of confinement for medical treatment or special education in an establishment appropriate to the type of anomaly or mental alteration found, or any other of the measures provided for in paragraph 3 of article 96. The confinement may not exceed the time that the custodial sentence would have lasted, had the subject been declared liable, and for that purpose the Judge or Court shall fix that maximum limit in the sentence.
2. The person subject to this measure may not leave the establishment without authorisation from the sentencing Judge or Court, in accordance with the provisions of article 97 of this Code.

Article 102

1. To those exempt from criminal liability pursuant to number 2 of article 20 there shall be applied, if necessary, the measure of confinement in a public rehabilitation centre, or a duly accredited or approved private one, or any other of the measures provided for in paragraph 3 of article 96. The confinement may not exceed the time that the custodial sentence would have lasted, had the subject been declared liable, and for that purpose the Judge or Court shall fix that maximum limit in the sentence.
2. The person subject to this measure may not leave the establishment without authorisation from the sentencing Judge or Court in accordance with the provisions of article 97 of this Code.

Article 103

1. Those who are declared exempt from liability pursuant to number 3 of Article 20 may be subject, if necessary, to the measure of confinement in a special educational centre or any other of the measures provided for in the third paragraph of Article 96. The confinement may not exceed the time that the custodial sentence would have lasted, had the subject been declared liable and, for that purpose, the Judge or Court shall set that maximum limit in the judgment.
2. The person subject to this measure may not leave the establishment without authorisation from the sentencing Judge or Court in accordance with the provisions of Article 97 of this Code.
3. In this case, the proposal referred to in Article 98 of this Code must be made at the end of each course or grade of education.

Article 104

1. In cases of incomplete exemption in relation to numbers 1, 2 and 3 of Article 20, the Judge or Court may impose, in addition to the corresponding sentence, the measures provided for in Articles 101, 102 and 103. However, the measure of confinement shall only be applicable when the sentence imposed is custodial and its duration may not exceed that of the sentence provided for by the Code for the offence. For its application, the provisions of Article 99 shall be observed.
2. When a measure of confinement of those provided for in the preceding paragraph or in Articles 101, 102 and 103 is applied, the sentencing judge or court shall notify the public prosecutor, with sufficient advance notice, of the proximity of its expiry, for the purposes of the provisions of the first additional provision of this Code.

Section 2 Non-custodial measures

Article 105

In the cases provided for in Articles 101 to 104, when imposing the custodial measure or during the execution thereof, the Judge or Court may reasonably impose one or more measures listed below. It must also impose one or more of those measures in the other cases expressly provided for in this Code.
1. For a period not exceeding five years:
a) Supervised release.
b) Family custody. The person subject to this measure shall be subject to the care and supervision of the designated family member who accepts the custody, who shall exercise it in relation to the Supervisory Judge and without prejudice to the educational or employment activities of the person in custody.
2. For a period of up to ten years:
a) Supervised release, when expressly provided for by this Code.
b) Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons.
c) Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds.
In order to decree the obligation to observe one or more of the measures provided for in this article, as well as to specify that obligation when required by law to impose them, the sentencing Judge or Court must assess the reports issued by the specialists and professionals responsible for assisting the person subject to the security measure.
The Prison Supervisory Judge or the services of the corresponding Administration shall inform the sentencing Judge or Court.
In the cases provided for in this article, the sentencing Judge or Court shall order that the competent social assistance services provide the help or care required and legally due to the person subject to non-custodial security measures.

Article 106

1. Supervised release shall consist of the subjection of the convicted person to judicial control through compliance on their part with one or more of the following measures:
a) The obligation to be always locatable by means of electronic devices that allow their permanent tracking.
b) The obligation to appear periodically at the place established by the Judge or Court.
c) The obligation to communicate immediately, within the maximum period and by the means indicated by the Judge or Court for that purpose, each change of place of residence or place or position of work.
d) The prohibition on leaving the place where they reside or a certain territory without authorisation from the Judge or Court.
e) The prohibition on approaching the victim, or those of their family members or other persons determined by the Judge or Court.
f) The prohibition on communicating with the victim, or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the Judge or Court.
g) The prohibition on going to certain territories, places or establishments.
h) The prohibition on residing in certain places.
i) The prohibition on carrying out certain activities that may offer or facilitate the opportunity to commit criminal acts of a similar nature.
j) The obligation to participate in training, employment, cultural, sex education or other similar programmes.
k) The obligation to follow external medical treatment, or to undergo periodic medical monitoring.
2. Without prejudice to the provisions of Article 105, the Judge or Court must impose in the judgment the measure of supervised release for compliance subsequent to the custodial sentence imposed whenever this Code expressly so provides.

In these cases, at least two months before the expiry of the custodial sentence, so that the measure of supervised release may begin at that same moment, the Prison Supervisory Judge, by the procedure provided for in Article 98, shall submit the appropriate proposal to the sentencing Judge or Court, which, in accordance with that procedure, shall specify, without prejudice to the provisions of Article 97, the content of the measure by setting the obligations or prohibitions listed in paragraph 1 of this article that the convicted person must observe.
If the person has been sentenced to several custodial sentences to be served successively, the provisions of the preceding paragraph shall be understood to refer to the moment when the service of all of them is completed.
Likewise, the convicted person on whom as many measures of supervised release have been imposed for various offences which, given the content of the obligations or prohibitions established, could not be executed simultaneously, shall serve them successively, without prejudice to the Judge or Court being able to exercise the powers conferred by the following paragraph.
3. By the same procedure of Article 98, the Judge or Court may:
a) Modify in the future the obligations and prohibitions imposed.
b) Reduce the duration of the supervised release or even terminate it in view of the positive prognosis of reintegration that considers the continuity of the obligations or prohibitions imposed unnecessary or counterproductive.
c) Render the measure without effect when the circumstance described in the preceding letter occurs at the time of specification of the measures regulated in number 2 of this article.
4. In case of breach of one or more obligations, the Judge or Court, in view of the concurrent circumstances and by the same procedure indicated in the preceding numbers, may modify the obligations or prohibitions imposed. If the breach were repeated or serious, revealing the will not to submit to the obligations or prohibitions imposed, the Judge shall also draw up testimony for a presumed offence under Article 468 of this Code.

Article 107

The judicial authority may order, with reasoning, the measure of disqualification from the exercise of a particular right, profession, trade, industry or commerce, position or employment or other activities, whether or not remunerated, for a period of one to five years, when the person has committed, with abuse of such exercise, or in relation to it, a criminal act, and when from the assessment of the concurrent circumstances the danger may be deduced that he or she will commit the same offence or similar ones again, provided that it is not possible to impose the corresponding penalty because he or she is in one of the situations provided for in numbers 1, 2 and 3 of Article 20.

Article 108

1. If the subject were a foreigner not legally resident in Spain, the judge or court shall order in the judgment, after hearing him or her, expulsion from the national territory as a substitute for the security measures applicable to him or her, unless the judge or court, after hearing the Public Prosecutor, exceptionally and with reasoning, considers that the nature of the offence justifies compliance in Spain.
The expulsion so ordered shall entail the closure of any administrative procedure whose purpose was authorisation to reside or work in Spain.
In the event that, once the substitution of the security measure by expulsion has been ordered, it could not be carried out, the originally imposed security measure shall be complied with.
2. The foreigner may not return to Spain for a period of 10 years, counted from the date of his or her expulsion.
3. The foreigner who attempts to breach a judicial decision of expulsion and prohibition of entry referred to in the preceding paragraphs shall be returned by the governmental authority, and the period of prohibition of entry shall begin to run again in its entirety.

TITLE V

On civil liability arising from offences and procedural costs

CHAPTER I

On civil liability and its extent

Article 109

1. The execution of an act described by law as an offence obliges to repair, in the terms provided in the laws, the damage and harm caused by it.
2. The injured party may opt, in any case, to demand civil liability before the Civil Jurisdiction.

Article 110

The liability established in the preceding article comprises:
1. Restitution.
2. Reparation of the damage.
3. Compensation for material and moral harm.

Article 111

1. The same property must be restored, whenever possible, with payment for the deterioration and impairment that the judge or court determines. Restitution shall take place even if the property is in the possession of a third party and the latter has acquired it legally and in good faith, leaving intact his or her right of recourse against the appropriate person and, where applicable, the right to be compensated by the person civilly liable for the offence.
2. This provision is not applicable when the third party has acquired the property in the form and with the requirements established by the Laws to make it non-recoverable.

Article 112

Reparation of the damage may consist of obligations to give, to do or not to do that the Judge or Court shall establish having regard to the nature of the damage and the personal and financial circumstances of the guilty party, determining whether they must be fulfilled by him or her personally or may be executed at his or her expense.

Article 113

Compensation for material and moral harm shall comprise not only that which has been caused to the aggrieved party, but also that which has been inflicted on his or her family members or third parties.

Article 114

If the victim has contributed by his or her conduct to the production of the damage or harm suffered, the Judges or Courts may moderate the amount of his or her reparation or compensation.

Article 115

The Judges and Courts, when declaring the existence of civil liability, shall establish with reasoning, in their decisions, the bases on which they found the amount of damages and compensation, and may fix it in the decision itself or at the time of its execution.

CHAPTER II

On persons civilly liable

Article 116

1. Every person criminally liable for an offence is also civilly liable if damage or harm arises from the act. If there are two or more persons liable for an offence, the judges or courts shall indicate the share for which each must be liable.
2. The principals and the accomplices, each within their respective class, shall be jointly and severally liable among themselves for their shares, and subsidiarily for those corresponding to the other liable persons.
Subsidiary liability shall be enforced: first, against the property of the principals, and then against that of the accomplices.
Both in cases where joint and several liability is enforced and subsidiary liability, the right of recourse of the person who has paid against the others for the shares corresponding to each shall remain intact.
3. The criminal liability of a legal person shall entail its civil liability in the terms established in Article 110 of this Code jointly and severally with the natural persons who are convicted for the same acts.

Article 117

Insurers who have assumed the risk of pecuniary liabilities arising from the use or exploitation of any property, enterprise, industry or activity, when, as a consequence of an act provided for in this Code, the event that determines the insured risk occurs, shall be directly civilly liable up to the limit of the legally established or contractually agreed compensation, without prejudice to the right of recourse against the appropriate person.

Article 118

1. The exemption from criminal liability declared in numbers 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 of Article 20, does not include exemption from civil liability, which shall be enforced in accordance with the following rules:
1. In the cases of numbers 1 and 3, also liable for the acts executed by those declared exempt from criminal liability are those who exercise their legal or de facto support, provided that there has been fault or negligence on their part and without prejudice to the direct civil liability that may correspond to the non-imputable persons.
The Judges or Courts shall graduate equitably the extent to which each of those subjects must be liable with their property.
2. The intoxicated person and the person under the influence of drugs are equally liable in the case of number 2.
3. In the case of number 5, the persons for whose benefit the harm has been averted shall be directly civilly liable, in proportion to the harm that has been avoided for them, if it is assessable or, otherwise, in the proportion that the Judge or Court establishes according to his or her prudent discretion. When the shares for which the interested party must be liable are not equitably assignable by the Judge or Court, not even by approximation, or when liability extends to the Public Administrations or to the majority of a population and, in any case, whenever the damage has been caused with the consent of the authority or its agents, compensation shall be ordered, where appropriate, in the form established by special laws and regulations.
4. In the case of number 6, those who have caused the fear shall be primarily liable, and in their absence, those who have executed the act.
2. In the case of Article 14, the authors of the act shall be civilly liable.

Article 119

In all the cases of the preceding article, the Judge or Court that issues an acquittal judgment on the grounds of finding the concurrence of any of the exemption causes cited shall proceed to fix civil liabilities unless express reservation of the actions to claim them in the appropriate forum has been made.

Article 120

The following are also civilly liable, in default of those who are criminally liable:
1. Guardians with full powers of representation who live with the person to whom they provide support, provided there is fault or negligence on their part.
2. Natural or legal persons who own publishing houses, newspapers, magazines, radio or television stations or any other means of written, spoken or visual dissemination, for offences committed using the means they own, without prejudice to the provisions of Article 212.
3. Natural or legal persons, in cases of offences committed in establishments they own, when those who manage or administer them, or their employees or staff, have infringed police regulations or provisions of the authority that are related to the punishable act committed, such that it would not have occurred without such infringement.
4. Natural or legal persons engaged in any type of industry or commerce, for offences committed by their employees or staff, representatives or agents in the performance of their duties or services.
5. Natural or legal persons who own vehicles capable of creating risks for third parties, for offences committed in the use of such vehicles by their employees or representatives or authorised persons.

Article 121

The State, the Autonomous Community, the province, the island, the municipality and other public entities, as the case may be, are subsidiarily liable for damage caused by those criminally responsible for intentional or negligent offences, when they are authorities, agents and contractors thereof or public officials in the exercise of their offices or functions, provided that the injury is a direct consequence of the operation of the public services entrusted to them, without prejudice to the patrimonial liability arising from the normal or abnormal operation of such services that may be claimed in accordance with the rules of administrative procedure, and provided that in no case may there be duplicate compensation.
If civil liability of the authority, agents and contractors thereof or public officials is claimed in criminal proceedings, the claim must be brought simultaneously against the Administration or public entity allegedly liable as subsidiary civil party.

Article 122

Any person who has benefited from the proceeds of an offence by gratuitous title is obliged to restore the thing or compensate for the damage up to the amount of his participation.

CHAPTER III

On procedural costs

Article 123

Procedural costs are deemed to be imposed by law on those criminally responsible for any offence.

Article 124

Costs shall comprise the fees and compensation incurred in judicial proceedings and shall always include the fees of the private prosecution in offences that can only be prosecuted upon complaint by a party.

CHAPTER IV

On the enforcement of civil liability and other pecuniary liabilities

Article 125

When the assets of the civilly liable party are not sufficient to satisfy all pecuniary liabilities at once, the Judge or Court, after hearing the injured party, may divide payment into instalments, determining, according to its prudent discretion and taking into account the needs of the injured party and the economic possibilities of the liable party, the period and amount of the instalments.

Article 126

1. Payments made by the convicted person or the subsidiary civilly liable party shall be allocated in the following order:
1. To the reparation of the damage caused and compensation for losses.
2. To compensation to the State for the amount of expenses incurred on its account in the proceedings.
3. To the costs of the private or particular prosecutor when their payment is imposed in the judgment.
4. To other procedural costs, including those of the defence of the accused, without preference among the interested parties.
5. To the fine.
2. When the offence is one that can only be prosecuted upon complaint by a party, the costs of the private prosecutor shall be satisfied in preference to compensation to the State. Payment of procedural costs incurred by the victim in the cases referred to in Article 14 of the Victim of Crime Statute Act shall have the same preference.

TITLE VI

On accessory consequences

Article 127

1. Any penalty imposed for an intentional offence shall entail the forfeiture of the effects deriving from it and of the property, means or instruments with which it was prepared or executed, as well as of the profits deriving from the offence, whatever transformations they may have undergone.
2. In cases where the law provides for the imposition of a custodial sentence exceeding one year for the commission of a negligent offence, the judge or court may order the forfeiture of the effects deriving from it and of the property, means or instruments with which it was prepared or executed, as well as of the profits deriving from the offence, whatever transformations they may have undergone.
3. If for any reason it is not possible to confiscate the property referred to in the preceding paragraphs of this article, confiscation of other property shall be ordered for an amount corresponding to the economic value thereof, and to that of the profits obtained from them. The same procedure shall apply when confiscation of specific property, effects or profits is ordered, but their value is less than they had at the time of their acquisition.

Article 127 bis

1.	The judge or court shall also order the confiscation of assets, effects and gains belonging to a person convicted of any of the following offences when it determines, based on well-founded objective indications, that the assets or effects originate from criminal activity, and their lawful origin is not proven:
a)	Offences of trafficking in human beings.

a bis) Offences of trafficking in organs.
b)	Offences relating to prostitution and sexual exploitation and corruption of minors and offences of sexual abuse and assault on persons under sixteen years of age.
c)	Computer offences under paragraphs 2 and 3 of article 197 and article 264.
d)	Offences against property and against the socio-economic order in cases of continuing criminal activity and recidivism.
e)	Offences relating to punishable insolvencies.
f)	Offences against intellectual or industrial property.
g)	Offences of corruption in business.
h)	Offences of receiving stolen goods under paragraph 2 of article 298.
i)	Offences of money laundering.
j)	Offences against the Public Treasury and Social Security.
k)	Offences against the rights of workers under articles 311 to 313.
l)	Offences against the rights of foreign nationals.
m)	Offences against public health under articles 368 to 373.
n)	Offences of counterfeiting currency.
o)	Offences of bribery.
p)	Offences of embezzlement.
q)	Offences of terrorism.
r)	Offences committed within a criminal organisation or group.
2.	For the purposes of the provisions of paragraph 1 of this article, the following indications shall be assessed, in particular, among others:
1.	º The disproportion between the value of the assets and effects in question and the income from lawful sources of the convicted person.
2.	º The concealment of ownership or any power of disposal over the assets or effects through the use of interposed natural or legal persons or entities without legal personality, or tax havens or zero-taxation territories that conceal or hinder the determination of the true ownership of the assets.
3.	º The transfer of the assets or effects through operations that hinder or prevent their location or destination and that lack valid legal or economic justification.
3.	In these cases, the provisions of paragraph 3 of the preceding article shall also apply.
4.	If subsequently the convicted person is convicted of similar criminal acts committed previously, the judge or court shall assess the scope of the previous confiscation ordered when deciding on the confiscation in the new proceedings.
5.	The confiscation referred to in this article shall not be ordered when the criminal activities from which the assets or effects originated have prescribed or have already been the subject of criminal proceedings resolved by an acquittal or dismissal order with res judicata effect.

Article 127 ter

1.	The judge or court may order the confiscation provided for in the preceding articles even without a conviction, when the unlawful patrimonial situation is proven in adversarial proceedings and it concerns any of the following cases:
a)	That the subject has died or suffers from a chronic illness that prevents his or her prosecution and there is a risk that the facts may become time-barred,
b)	is in absentia and this prevents the facts from being tried within a reasonable time, or
c)	is not sentenced because he or she is exempt from criminal liability or because it has been extinguished.
2.	The confiscation referred to in this article may only be directed against a person who has been formally accused or against the suspect in relation to whom there are rational indications of criminality when the situations referred to in the preceding paragraph have prevented the continuation of the criminal proceedings.

Article 127 quater

1.	Judges and courts may also order the confiscation of the assets, effects and gains referred to in the preceding articles that have been transferred to third parties, or of a value equivalent thereto, in the following cases:
a)	In the case of effects and gains, when they have been acquired with knowledge that they originate from an unlawful activity or when a diligent person would have had reasons to suspect, in the circumstances of the case, their unlawful origin.
b)	In the case of other assets, when they have been acquired with knowledge that in this way their confiscation was being hindered or when a diligent person would have had reasons to suspect, in the circumstances of the case, that in that way their confiscation was being hindered.
2.	It shall be presumed, unless proven otherwise, that the third party has known or has had reasons to suspect that the assets originated from an unlawful activity or that they were transferred to avoid their confiscation, when the assets or effects have been transferred to him or her free of charge or for a price lower than the actual market price.

Article 127 quinquies

1.	Judges and courts may also order the confiscation of assets, effects and gains originating from the prior criminal activity of the convicted person, when the following requirements are cumulatively met:
a)	That the subject is or has been convicted of any of the offences referred to in article 127 bis.1 of the Criminal Code.
b)	That the offence has been committed in the context of a prior continuing criminal activity.
c)	That there are well-founded indications that a relevant part of the convicted person's assets originates from prior criminal activity.
Relevant indications are:
1.	º The disproportion between the value of the assets and effects in question and the income from lawful sources of the convicted person.
2.	º The concealment of ownership or any power of disposal over the assets or effects through the use of interposed natural or legal persons or entities without legal personality, or tax havens or zero-taxation territories that conceal or hinder the determination of the true ownership of the assets.
3.	º The transfer of the assets or effects through operations that hinder or prevent their location or destination and that lack valid legal or economic justification.
The provisions of the preceding paragraph shall only apply when there are well-founded indications that the subject has obtained, from his or her criminal activity, a benefit exceeding 6,000 euros.
2.	For the purposes of the preceding paragraph, it shall be understood that the offence has been committed in the context of a continuing criminal activity whenever:
a)	The subject is convicted or has been convicted in the same proceedings of three or more offences from which the obtaining of a direct or indirect economic benefit has been derived, or of a continuing offence that includes at least three criminal infractions from which a direct or indirect economic benefit has been derived.
b)	Or in the period of six years prior to the moment when the proceedings were initiated in which he or she has been convicted of any of the offences referred to in article 127 bis of the Criminal Code, he or she had been convicted of two or more offences from which the obtaining of an economic benefit had been derived, or of a continuing offence that includes at least two criminal infractions from which the obtaining of an economic benefit has been derived.

Artículo 127 sexies

For the purposes of the provisions of the preceding article, the following presumptions shall apply:
