1. º It shall be presumed that all assets acquired by the convicted person within the period of time beginning six years before the date of opening of the criminal proceedings, derive from his or her criminal activity.
For these purposes, it is understood that the assets were acquired on the earliest date on which it is established that the subject has had them at his or her disposal.
2. º It shall be presumed that all expenses incurred by the convicted person during the period of time referred to in the first paragraph of the preceding number, were paid with funds deriving from his or her criminal activity.
3. º It shall be presumed that all assets referred to in number 1 were acquired free of encumbrances.
The judge or court may order that the foregoing presumptions not be applied in relation to certain assets, effects or gains, when, in the specific circumstances of the case, they prove to be incorrect or disproportionate.

Artículo 127 septies

If the execution of the confiscation could not be carried out, in whole or in part, due to the nature or location of the assets, effects or gains in question, or for any other circumstance, the judge or court may, by order, agree to the confiscation of other assets, even of lawful origin, belonging to those criminally responsible for the act for a value equivalent to that of the unexecuted part of the confiscation initially ordered.
The same procedure shall be followed when the confiscation of specific assets, effects or gains is ordered, but their value is lower than that which they had at the time of their acquisition.

Artículo 127 octies

1. In order to guarantee the effectiveness of the confiscation, the assets, means, instruments and gains may be seized or attached and placed in deposit by the judicial authority from the moment of the first proceedings.
2. It shall be for the judge or court to decide, in accordance with the provisions of the Criminal Procedure Act, on the early realisation or provisional use of the assets and effects seized.
3. Assets, instruments and gains confiscated by final decision, unless they must be allocated to the payment of compensation to victims, shall be awarded to the State, which shall give them the destination provided for by law or regulation.

Artículo 128

When the said effects and instruments are of lawful commerce and their value is not proportionate to the nature or seriousness of the criminal offence, or the civil liabilities have been fully satisfied, the Judge or Court may not order confiscation, or order it partially.

Artículo 129

1. In the case of offences committed within, with the collaboration of, through or by means of companies, organisations, groups or any other class of entities or groupings of persons which, by lacking legal personality, are not covered by Article 31 bis, the judge or court may impose with reasoning on such companies, organisations, groups, entities or groupings one or more accessory consequences to the penalty corresponding to the perpetrator of the offence, with the content provided for in letters c) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33. It may also order the definitive prohibition of carrying out any activity, even if lawful.
2. The accessory consequences referred to in the preceding paragraph may only be applied to the companies, organisations, groups or entities or groupings mentioned therein when this Code expressly provides for it, or when it concerns any of the offences for which it permits criminal liability to be demanded of legal persons.

3. The temporary closure of premises or establishments, the suspension of social activities and judicial intervention may also be ordered by the Investigating Judge as a precautionary measure during the investigation of the case for the purposes established in this article and with the limits set out in Article 33.7.

Artículo 129 bis

If it concerns persons convicted of committing a serious offence against life, the integrity of persons, liberty, sexual liberty or indemnity, terrorism, or any other serious offence that entails a serious risk to the life, health or physical integrity of persons, when from the circumstances of the act, antecedents, assessment of his or her personality, or other available information it can be assessed that there exists a relevant danger of criminal recidivism, the judge or court may order the taking of biological samples from his or her person and the carrying out of analyses to obtain DNA identifiers and their registration in the police database. Only those analyses necessary to obtain identifiers that provide exclusively genetic information revealing the identity of the person and his or her sex may be carried out.
If the person concerned opposes the collection of the samples, its forced execution may be imposed through recourse to the minimum coercive measures indispensable for its execution, which must in all cases be proportionate to the circumstances of the case and respectful of his or her dignity.

TÍTULO VII

On the extinction of criminal liability and its effects

CAPÍTULO I

On the causes that extinguish criminal liability

Artículo 130

1. Criminal liability is extinguished:
1. º By the death of the offender.
2. º By the completion of the sentence.
3. º By the definitive remission of the penalty, in accordance with the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 of Article 87.
4. º By pardon.
5. º By the forgiveness of the injured person, when it concerns minor offences prosecutable at the instance of the aggrieved person or the law so provides. The forgiveness must be granted expressly before judgment has been delivered, for which purpose the sentencing judicial authority must hear the person injured by the offence before delivering it.
In offences committed against minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection that affect eminently personal legal interests, the forgiveness of the injured person does not extinguish criminal liability.
6. º By the prescription of the offence.
7. º By the prescription of the penalty or of the security measure.
2. The transformation, merger, absorption or division of a legal person does not extinguish its criminal liability, which shall be transferred to the entity or entities into which it is transformed, merged or absorbed and shall extend to the entity or entities resulting from the division. The Judge or Court may moderate the transfer of the penalty to the legal person according to the proportion that the legal person originally responsible for the offence bears to it.
The concealed or merely apparent dissolution of the legal person does not extinguish criminal liability. It shall be considered in all cases that there exists concealed or merely apparent dissolution of the legal person when its economic activity is continued and the substantial identity of clients, suppliers and employees, or of the most relevant part of all of them, is maintained.

Article 131

1.	Crimes prescribe:
After twenty years, when the maximum penalty provided for the crime is imprisonment of fifteen or more years.
After fifteen, when the maximum penalty provided by law is disqualification for more than ten years, or imprisonment for more than ten and less than fifteen years.
After ten, when the maximum penalty provided by law is imprisonment or disqualification for more than five years and not exceeding ten.
After five, all other crimes, except minor crimes and crimes of insults and slander, which prescribe after one year.
2.	When the penalty provided by law is compound, the rules contained in this article shall apply according to the one that requires the longest time for prescription.
3.	Crimes against humanity and genocide and crimes against persons and property protected in case of armed conflict, except those punished in article 614, shall not prescribe in any case.
Nor shall crimes of terrorism prescribe, if they have caused the death of a person.
4.	In cases of concurrence of offences or connected offences, the prescription period shall be that corresponding to the most serious crime.

Article 132

1.	The periods provided in the preceding article shall be computed from the day on which the punishable offence was committed. In cases of continuing crime, permanent crime, as well as in offences requiring habituality, such periods shall be computed, respectively, from the day on which the last offence was committed, from when the unlawful situation was eliminated or from when the conduct ceased.
In crimes of non-consensual abortion, injuries, against freedom, torture and against moral integrity, against privacy, the right to one's own image and the inviolability of the home, and against family relations, excluding the crimes contemplated in the following paragraph, when the victim is a person under eighteen years of age, the periods shall be computed from the day on which the victim reaches the age of majority, and if the victim dies before reaching it, from the date of death.
In crimes of attempted homicide, injuries under articles 149 and 150, in the crime of habitual abuse provided for in article 173.2, in crimes against sexual freedom and in crimes of trafficking in human beings, when the victim is a person under eighteen years of age, the periods shall be computed from when the victim reaches thirty-five years of age, and if the victim dies before reaching that age, from the date of death.
2.	Prescription shall be interrupted, rendering the elapsed time without effect, when the proceedings are directed against the person allegedly responsible for the crime, beginning to run again from when the proceedings are stayed or end without conviction in accordance with the following rules:
1.	ª Proceedings shall be understood to be directed against a specific person from the moment when, upon initiating the case or subsequently, a reasoned judicial decision is issued attributing to that person alleged participation in an act that may constitute a crime.
2.	ª Notwithstanding the foregoing, the filing of a criminal complaint or the complaint made before a judicial body, in which a specific person is attributed alleged participation in an act that may constitute a crime, shall suspend the computation of prescription for a maximum period of six months, counting from the same date of filing of the criminal complaint or making of the complaint.
If within said period one of the judicial decisions mentioned in rule 1.ª is issued against the person subject to the criminal complaint or complaint, or against any other person involved in the facts, the interruption of prescription shall be understood to have been retroactively produced, for all purposes, on the date of filing of the criminal complaint or complaint.

On the contrary, the computation of the prescription period shall continue from the date of filing of the criminal complaint or complaint if, within the six-month period, a final judicial decision is issued refusing to admit the criminal complaint or complaint or ordering not to direct the proceedings against the person subject to the criminal complaint or complaint. The continuation of the computation shall also occur if, within said period, the investigating judge does not adopt any of the decisions provided for in this article.
3.	For the purposes of this article, the person against whom the proceedings are directed must be sufficiently determined in the judicial decision, either through direct identification or through data that allow subsequent specification of said identification within the organization or group of persons to whom the act is attributed.
4.	In proceedings whose investigation has been assumed by the European Public Prosecutor's Office, prescription shall be interrupted:
a)	when the investigation is directed against a specific person, sufficiently identified, in the terms of the preceding paragraph, and this is reflected in a reasoned Decree.
b)	when a criminal complaint or complaint is filed before the European Public Prosecutor's Office in which a specific person is attributed alleged participation in an act that may constitute a crime, rule 2.ª of paragraph 2 of this article being applicable.

Article 133

1.	Penalties imposed by final judgment prescribe:
After 30 years, those of imprisonment for more than 20 years.
After 25 years, those of imprisonment of 15 or more years without exceeding 20.
After 20, those of disqualification for more than 10 years and those of imprisonment for more than 10 and less than 15.
After 15, those of disqualification for more than six years and not exceeding 10, and those of imprisonment for more than five years and not exceeding 10.
After 10, the remaining serious penalties. After five, less serious penalties. After one year, minor penalties.
2.	Penalties imposed for crimes against humanity and genocide and for crimes against persons and property protected in case of armed conflict, except those punished in article 614, shall not prescribe in any case.
Nor shall penalties imposed for crimes of terrorism prescribe, if these have caused the death of a person.

Article 134

1.	The time of prescription of the penalty shall be computed from the date of the final judgment, or from the breach of the sentence, if it had begun to be served.
2.	The prescription period of the penalty shall be suspended:
a)	During the period of suspension of the execution of the penalty.
b)	During the serving of other penalties, when the provisions of article 75 are applicable.

Article 135

1.	Security measures shall prescribe after ten years, if they are custodial measures exceeding three years, and after five years if they are custodial measures equal to or less than three years or have other content.
2.	The time of prescription shall be computed from the day on which the decision imposing the measure became final or, in case of successive enforcement, from when it should have begun to be enforced.
3.	If the enforcement of a security measure is subsequent to that of a penalty, the period shall be computed from the extinction of the latter.

CHAPTER II

On the cancellation of criminal records

Article 136

1.	Convicted persons who have extinguished their criminal liability have the right to obtain from the Ministry of Justice, ex officio or at the request of a party, the cancellation of their criminal records, when the following periods have elapsed without having reoffended:
a)	Six months for minor penalties.
b)	Two years for penalties not exceeding twelve months and those imposed for negligent offences.
c)	Three years for the remaining less serious penalties of less than three years.
d)	Five years for the remaining less serious penalties equal to or greater than three years.
e)	Ten years for serious penalties.
2.	The periods referred to in the preceding paragraph shall be counted from the day following that on which the penalty was extinguished, but if this occurred through conditional remission, the period, once definitive remission has been obtained, shall be calculated by backdating it to the day following that on which the penalty would have been served if this benefit had not been enjoyed. In this case, the day following the granting of the suspension shall be taken as the initial date for calculating the duration of the penalty.
3.	Penalties imposed on legal persons and the accessory consequences of Article 129 shall be cancelled within the corresponding period, in accordance with the rule provided in paragraph 1 of this article, unless dissolution or definitive prohibition of activities has been agreed. In these cases, the entries shall be cancelled after fifty years computed from the day following the finality of the judgment.
4.	Entries of criminal records in the different sections of the Central Register of Convicted Persons and Rebels shall not be public. During their validity, only certifications shall be issued with the limitations and guarantees provided in their specific rules and in the cases established by law. In any case, those requested by judges or courts shall be issued, whether or not they refer to cancelled entries, expressly stating this latter circumstance.
5.	In cases where, despite the requirements established in this article for cancellation being met, it has not occurred, the judge or court, having proven such circumstances, shall not take such records into account.

Article 137

Entries of security measures imposed in accordance with the provisions of this Code or other criminal laws shall be cancelled once the respective measure has been served or prescribed; in the meantime, they shall only appear in the certifications that the Register issues for judges or courts or administrative authorities, in the cases established by law.

BOOK II

Offences and their penalties

TITLE I

Homicide and its forms

Article 138

1.	Whoever kills another shall be punished, as guilty of homicide, with the penalty of imprisonment of ten to fifteen years.
2.	The acts shall be punished with the penalty one degree higher in the following cases:

a)	when any of the circumstances of paragraph 1 of Article 140 concurs in its commission, or
b)	when the acts are also constitutive of an offence of assault under Article 550.

Article 139

1.	Whoever kills another with any of the following circumstances concurring shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of fifteen to twenty-five years, as guilty of murder:
1.	ª With treachery.
2.	ª For price, reward or promise.
3.	ª With cruelty, deliberately and inhumanly increasing the pain of the victim.
4.	ª To facilitate the commission of another offence or to prevent its discovery.
2.	When more than one of the circumstances provided in the preceding paragraph concur in a murder, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half.

Article 140

1.	Murder shall be punished with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment when any of the following circumstances concurs:
1.	ª That the victim is under sixteen years of age, or is a particularly vulnerable person by reason of age, illness or disability.
2.	ª That the act was subsequent to an offence against sexual freedom that the perpetrator had committed against the victim.
3.	ª That the offence had been committed by someone belonging to a criminal group or organisation.
2.	The perpetrator of murder who has been convicted for the death of more than two persons shall be imposed a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment. In this case, the provisions of letter b) of paragraph 1 of Article 78 bis and letter b) of paragraph 2 of the same article shall apply.

Article 140 bis

1.	Persons convicted of committing one or more offences included in this title may also be imposed a measure of supervised release.
2.	If the victim and the perpetrator of the offences provided in the three preceding articles have a son or daughter in common, the judicial authority shall impose, with respect to the latter, the penalty of deprivation of parental authority.
The same penalty shall be imposed when the victim was a son or daughter of the perpetrator, with respect to other sons and daughters, if any exist.

Article 141

Incitement, conspiracy and proposal to commit the offences provided in the three preceding articles shall be punished with the penalty one or two degrees lower than that indicated in each case in the preceding articles.

Article 142

1.	Whoever through gross negligence causes the death of another shall be punished, as guilty of negligent homicide, with the penalty of imprisonment of one to four years.
If the negligent homicide had been committed using a motor vehicle or a moped, the penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for one to six years shall also be imposed. For the purposes of this paragraph, driving in which the concurrence of any of the circumstances provided in Article 379 determined the occurrence of the act shall be deemed in all cases as gross negligence.

If the negligent homicide had been committed using a firearm, the penalty of deprivation of the right to carry or possess weapons for a period of one to six years shall also be imposed.
If the homicide had been committed through professional negligence, the penalty of special disqualification for the exercise of the profession, trade or position for a period of three to six years shall also be imposed.
2.	Whoever through minor negligence causes the death of another shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of three months to eighteen months.
If the homicide had been committed using a motor vehicle or a moped, the penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for three to eighteen months shall also be imposed. Negligence not classified as gross in which the commission of any of the serious infringements of traffic rules, circulation of motor vehicles and road safety has been determinant for the occurrence of the act shall be deemed in all cases as minor negligence. The assessment of whether or not the determination exists must be appreciated in a reasoned decision.
If the homicide had been committed using a firearm, the penalty of deprivation of the right to carry or possess weapons for a period of three to eighteen months may also be imposed.
Except in cases where it occurs using a motor vehicle or a moped, the offence provided in this paragraph shall only be prosecutable upon complaint by the aggrieved person or their legal representative.

Article 142 bis

In the cases provided for in number 1 of the preceding article, the Judge or Court may impose, with reasoning, the penalty higher by one degree, to the extent it deems appropriate, if the act were of notorious gravity, having regard to the singular nature and relevance of the risk created and the normative duty of care infringed, and had caused the death of two or more persons or the death of one and injuries constituting an offence under Article 152.1.2.º or 3.º in the others, and by two degrees if the number of deceased were very high.

Article 143

1.	Whoever induces another to suicide shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from four to eight years.
2.	The penalty of imprisonment from two to five years shall be imposed on whoever cooperates with acts necessary to the suicide of a person.
3.	The penalty of imprisonment from six to ten years shall be imposed if the cooperation reached the point of executing the death.
4.	Whoever causes or actively cooperates with acts necessary and direct to the death of a person who suffered grave, chronic and disabling suffering or a grave and incurable illness, with constant and unbearable physical or psychological suffering, at the express, serious and unequivocal request of that person, shall be punished with the penalty lower by one or two degrees than those indicated in paragraphs 2 and 3.
5.	Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraph, whoever causes or actively cooperates in the death of another person in compliance with the provisions of the organic law regulating euthanasia shall not incur criminal liability.

Article 143 bis

The distribution or public dissemination through the Internet, telephone or any other information or communication technology of content specifically intended to promote, encourage or incite the suicide of minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from one to four years.
The judicial authorities shall order the adoption of the necessary measures for the removal of the content referred to in the preceding paragraph, for the interruption of services that predominantly offer such content or for the blocking of both when they are located abroad.

TITLE II

On abortion

Article 144

Whoever causes the abortion of a woman, without her consent, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from four to eight years and special disqualification from exercising any health profession, or from providing services of any kind in clinics, establishments or gynaecological consulting rooms, public or private, for a period of three to ten years.
The same penalties shall be imposed on whoever performs the abortion having obtained the woman's consent by means of violence, threat or deception.

Article 145

1.	Whoever causes the abortion of a woman, with her consent, outside the cases permitted by law shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from one to three years and special disqualification from exercising any health profession, or from providing services of any kind in clinics, establishments or gynaecological consulting rooms, public or private, for a period of one to six years. The judge may impose the penalty in its upper half when the acts described in this paragraph are carried out outside an accredited public or private centre or establishment.
2.	The woman who causes her own abortion or consents to another person causing it, outside the cases permitted by law, shall be punished with a fine of six to twenty-four months.
3.	In all cases, the judge or court shall impose the penalties respectively provided for in this article in their upper half when the conduct is carried out from the twenty-second week of gestation onwards.

Article 145 bis

1.	Whoever, within the cases contemplated by law, performs an abortion shall be punished with a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification from providing services of any kind in clinics, establishments or gynaecological consulting rooms, public or private, for a period of six months to two years:
a)	Without having the mandatory prior reports;
b)	Outside an accredited public or private centre or establishment. In this case, the judge may impose the penalty in its upper half.
2.	In all cases, the judge or court shall impose the penalties provided for in this article in their upper half when the abortion has been performed from the twenty-second week of gestation onwards.
3.	The pregnant woman shall not be punished under this provision.

Article 146

Whoever by gross negligence causes an abortion shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from three to five months or a fine of six to 10 months.
When the abortion is committed by professional negligence, the penalty of special disqualification from the exercise of the profession, trade or position for a period of one to three years shall also be imposed.
The pregnant woman shall not be punished under this provision.

TITLE III

On injuries

Article 147

1.	Whoever, by any means or procedure, causes another an injury that impairs their bodily integrity or their physical or mental health, shall be punished, as perpetrator of the

offence of injuries with a penalty of imprisonment from three months to three years or a fine of six to twelve months, provided that the injury objectively requires for its healing, in addition to initial medical assistance, medical or surgical treatment. Simple monitoring or medical follow-up of the course of the injury shall not be considered medical treatment.
2.	Whoever, by any means or procedure, causes another an injury not included in the preceding paragraph, shall be punished with a fine of one to three months.
3.	Whoever strikes or physically mistreats another without causing injury, shall be punished with a fine of one to two months.
4.	The offences provided for in the two preceding paragraphs shall only be prosecutable upon complaint by the aggrieved person or their legal representative.

Article 148

The injuries provided for in paragraph 1 of the preceding article may be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from two to five years, having regard to the result caused or risk produced:
1.	º If in the assault weapons, instruments, objects, means, methods or forms specifically dangerous to the life or health, physical or psychological, of the injured person had been used.
2.	º If there had been cruelty or treachery.
3.	º If the victim were a minor under fourteen years of age or a person with a disability in need of special protection.
4.	º If the victim were or had been the wife, or a woman who is or had been linked to the perpetrator by an analogous relationship of affection, even without cohabitation.
5.	º If the victim were a particularly vulnerable person who lives with the perpetrator.

Article 149

1.	Whoever causes another, by any means or procedure, the loss or uselessness of a principal organ or limb, or of a sense, impotence, sterility, a grave deformity, or a grave somatic or psychological illness, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from six to 12 years.
2.	Whoever causes another genital mutilation in any of its manifestations shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from six to 12 years. If the victim were a minor or a person with a disability in need of special protection, the penalty of special disqualification from the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or fostering for a period of four to 10 years shall be applicable, if the judge deems it appropriate to the interest of the minor or person with a disability in need of special protection.

Article 150

Whoever causes another the loss or uselessness of a non-principal organ or limb, or deformity, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from three to six years.

Article 151

Incitement, conspiracy and proposal to commit the offences provided for in the preceding articles of this Title shall be punished with a penalty one or two degrees lower than that of the corresponding offence.

Article 152

1.	Whoever by gross negligence causes any of the injuries provided for in the preceding articles shall be punished, having regard to the risk created and the result produced:
1.	° With the penalty of imprisonment from three to six months or a fine of six to eighteen months, if it concerns the injuries of paragraph 1 of Article 147.
2.	° With the penalty of imprisonment from one to three years, if it concerns the injuries of Article 149.
3.	° With the penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years, if it concerns the injuries of Article 150.

If the acts had been committed using a motor vehicle or a moped, the penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from one to four years shall also be imposed. For the purposes of this paragraph, driving in which the concurrence of any of the circumstances provided for in Article 379 determined the production of the act shall be deemed in all cases as gross negligence.
If the injuries had been caused using a firearm, the penalty of deprivation of the right to carry or possess weapons for a period of one to four years shall also be imposed. If the injuries had been committed by professional negligence, the penalty of special disqualification for the exercise of the profession, trade or office for a period of six months to four years shall also be imposed.
2.	Whoever by less serious negligence causes any of the injuries referred to in Article 147.1 shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of one to two months, and if the injuries referred to in Articles 149 and 150 are caused, shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of three months to twelve months.
If the acts had been committed using a motor vehicle or a moped, the penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from three to eighteen months shall also be imposed. For the purposes of this paragraph, negligence not classified as gross in which the commission of any of the serious infringements of traffic rules, vehicle circulation and road safety has been determinant for the production of the act shall be deemed in all cases as less serious negligence. The assessment of whether or not the determination exists must be appreciated in a reasoned decision.
If the injuries had been caused using a firearm, the penalty of deprivation of the right to carry or possess weapons for a period of three months to one year may also be imposed.
The offence provided for in this paragraph shall only be prosecutable upon complaint by the aggrieved person or their legal representative.

Article 152 bis

In the cases provided for in number 1 of the preceding article, the Judge or Court may impose with reasoning the penalty one degree higher, to the extent it deems appropriate, if the act were of notorious gravity, having regard to the singular nature and relevance of the risk created and the normative duty of care infringed, and had caused injuries constituting an offence under Article 152.1.2.º or 3.º to a plurality of persons, and by two degrees if the number of injured persons were very high.

Article 153

1.	Whoever by any means or procedure causes another person psychological impairment or a less serious injury of those provided for in paragraph 2 of Article 147, or strikes or physically mistreats another without causing injury, when the victim is or has been a spouse, or a woman who is or has been linked to him by an analogous relationship of affection even without cohabitation, or a particularly vulnerable person who lives with the perpetrator, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year or community service from thirty-one to eighty days and, in all cases, deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from one year and one day to three years, as well as, when the judge or court deems it appropriate to the interest of the minor or person with disability in need of special protection, disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, custody or foster care for up to five years.
2.	If the victim of the offence provided for in the preceding paragraph were any of the persons referred to in Article 173.2, except for the persons contemplated in the preceding paragraph of this article, the perpetrator shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from three months to one year or community service from thirty-one to eighty days and, in all cases, deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from one year and one day to three years, as well as, when the Judge or Court deems it appropriate to the interest of the minor or person with disability in need of special protection, disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, custody or foster care from six months to three years.
3.	The penalties provided for in paragraphs 1 and 2 shall be imposed in their upper half when the offence is perpetrated in the presence of minors, or using weapons, or takes place in the

common home or in the victim's home, or is carried out in breach of a penalty of those contemplated in Article 48 of this Code or a precautionary or security measure of the same nature.
4.	Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraphs, the Judge or Court, giving reasons in the judgment, having regard to the personal circumstances of the perpetrator and those concurrent in the commission of the act, may impose the penalty one degree lower.

Article 154

Those who fight among themselves, attacking each other tumultuously, and using means or instruments that endanger the life or physical integrity of persons, shall be punished for their participation in the fight with the penalty of imprisonment from three months to one year or a fine of six to 24 months.

Article 155

In offences of injuries, if the valid, free, spontaneous and expressly given consent of the victim has been obtained, the penalty one or two degrees lower shall be imposed.
Consent given by a minor or a person with disability in need of special protection shall not be valid.

Article 156

Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding article, valid, free, conscious and expressly given consent exempts from criminal liability in cases of organ transplants carried out in accordance with the provisions of the law, sterilisations and transsexual surgery performed by a medical practitioner, unless the consent has been obtained through duress, or by means of price or reward, or the grantor is a minor or absolutely lacks the capacity to give it, in which case that given by them or by their legal representatives shall not be valid.

Article 156 bis

1.	Those who in any way promote, favour, facilitate, advertise or execute the trafficking of human organs shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from six to twelve years in the case of an organ from a living person and imprisonment from three to six years in the case of an organ from a deceased person.
For these purposes, trafficking of human organs shall be understood as:
a)	The illicit extraction or obtaining of human organs from others. Such extraction or obtaining shall be illicit if it occurs in the presence of any of the following circumstances:
1.	ª that it has been carried out without the free, informed and express consent of the living donor in the form and with the requirements legally provided;
2.	ª that it has been carried out without the necessary authorisation required by law in the case of the deceased donor,
3.	ª that, in exchange for the extraction or obtaining, for one's own or another's benefit, a gift or remuneration of any kind is requested or received by the donor or a third party, directly or through an intermediary, or an offer or promise is accepted. Reimbursement of expenses or loss of income derived from the donation shall not be understood as a gift or remuneration.
b)	The preparation, preservation, storage, transport, transfer, receipt, import or export of illicitly extracted organs.
c)	The use of illicitly extracted organs for the purpose of transplantation or for other purposes.
2.	Those who, for their own or another's benefit, shall be punished in the same manner:
a)	request or receive, directly or through an intermediary, a gift or remuneration of any kind, or accept an offer or promise for proposing or recruiting a donor or a recipient of organs;
b)	offer or deliver, directly or through an intermediary, a gift or remuneration of any kind to medical personnel, public officials or private individuals in connection with the

exercise of their profession or position in clinics, establishments or consulting rooms, public or private, with the purpose of carrying out or facilitating the illicit extraction or obtaining or the implantation of illicitly extracted organs.
3.	If the recipient of the organ consents to the performance of the transplant knowing its illicit origin, he or she shall be punished with the same penalties provided for in paragraph 1, which may be reduced by one or two degrees taking into account the circumstances of the act and of the offender.
4.	The penalties higher in degree than those provided for in paragraph 1 shall be imposed when:
a)	the life or physical or psychological integrity of the victim of the crime has been seriously endangered.
b)	the victim is a minor or especially vulnerable by reason of age, disability, illness or situation.
If both circumstances concur, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half.
5.	The medical practitioner, public official or private individual who, in connection with the exercise of his or her profession or position, carries out in public or private centres the conduct described in paragraphs 1 and 2, or requests or receives the gift or remuneration referred to in letter b) of the latter paragraph, or accepts the offer or promise to receive it, shall incur the penalty indicated therein higher in degree and, in addition, special disqualification from public employment or position, profession or occupation, from exercising any health profession or from providing services of any kind in clinics, establishments or consulting rooms, public or private, for the duration of the sentence. If, in addition, any of the circumstances provided for in paragraph 4 concurs, the penalties shall be imposed in their upper half.
For the purposes of this article, the term medical practitioner includes doctors, nursing staff and any other person who carries out a health or socio-health activity.
6.	The penalty higher in degree than that provided for in paragraph 1 and special disqualification from profession, occupation, industry or trade for the duration of the sentence shall be imposed when the offender belongs to an organisation or criminal group dedicated to carrying out such activities. If any of the circumstances provided for in paragraph 4 concurs, the penalties shall be imposed in the upper half. If the circumstance provided for in paragraph 5 concurs, the penalties indicated therein shall be imposed in their upper half.
When it concerns the leaders, administrators or managers of such organisations or groups, the penalty shall be applied in its upper half, which may be raised to the immediately higher degree. In any case, the penalty shall be raised to the immediately higher degree if any of the circumstances provided for in paragraph 4 or the circumstance provided for in paragraph 5 concurs.
7.	When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is responsible for the crimes included in this article, it shall be imposed the penalty of a fine of triple to quintuple the benefit obtained.
Taking into account the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.
8.	Incitement, conspiracy and proposal to commit the crimes provided for in this article shall be punished with the penalty lower by one to two degrees than that corresponding, respectively, to the acts provided for in the preceding paragraphs.
9.	In any case, the penalties provided for in this article shall be imposed without prejudice to those corresponding, where appropriate, for the crime of Article 177 bis of this Code and other crimes actually committed.
10.	Convictions by foreign judges or courts for crimes of the same nature as those provided for in this article shall produce the effects of recidivism, unless the criminal record has been cancelled or can be cancelled in accordance with Spanish law.

Article 156 ter

The distribution or public dissemination through the Internet, telephone or any other information or communication technology of content specifically intended to promote, encourage or incite self-harm by minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from six months to three years.

The judicial authorities shall order the adoption of the necessary measures for the removal of the content referred to in the preceding paragraph, for the interruption of services that predominantly offer such content or for the blocking of both when they are located abroad.

Article 156 quater

Persons convicted of the commission of one or more crimes included in this Title, when the victim is any of the persons referred to in paragraph 2 of Article 173, may also be imposed a measure of supervised release.

Article 156 quinquies

Persons convicted of the commission of any of the crimes provided for in Articles 147.1, 148, 149, 150 and 153 in which the victim is a minor may be imposed, in addition to the applicable penalties, the penalty of special disqualification from any profession, occupation or other activities, whether or not remunerated, that involve regular and direct contact with minors, for a period between three and five years longer than the duration of the custodial sentence imposed in the judgment or for a period of two to five years when a prison sentence has not been imposed, in both cases taking into account proportionally the seriousness of the crime, the number of crimes committed and the circumstances present in the convicted person.

TITLE IV

Injuries to the foetus

Article 157

Whoever, by any means or procedure, causes an injury or illness to a fetus that seriously harms its normal development, or causes in it a serious physical or mental defect, shall be punished with a prison sentence of one to four years and special disqualification from exercising any health profession, or from providing services of any kind in gynecological clinics, establishments or consulting rooms, public or private, for a period of two to eight years.

Article 158

Whoever, through gross negligence, commits the acts described in the preceding article, shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to five months or a fine of six to 10 months.
When the acts described in the preceding article are committed through professional negligence, the penalty of special disqualification from exercising the profession, trade or position for a period of six months to two years shall also be imposed.
The pregnant woman shall not be punished under this provision.

TITLE V

Offenses relating to genetic manipulation

Article 159

1.	Those who, for a purpose other than the elimination or reduction of serious defects or illnesses, manipulate human genes in such a way as to alter the genotype, shall be punished with a prison sentence of two to six years and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for seven to ten years.
2.	If the alteration of the genotype is carried out through gross negligence, the penalty shall be a fine of six to fifteen months and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for one to three years.

Article 160

1.	The use of genetic engineering to produce biological or exterminating weapons of the human species shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to seven years and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of seven to 10 years.
2.	Those who fertilize human ova for any purpose other than human procreation shall be punished with a prison sentence of one to five years and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for six to 10 years.
3.	The same penalty shall be imposed for the creation of identical human beings by cloning or other procedures aimed at the selection of the race.

Article 161

1.	Whoever performs assisted reproduction on a woman without her consent shall be punished with a prison sentence of two to six years, and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of one to four years.
2.	To proceed for this offense, a complaint from the aggrieved person or their legal representative shall be required. When the aggrieved person is a minor, a person with a disability in need of special protection or a helpless person, the Public Prosecutor may also file a complaint.

Article 162

In the offenses contemplated in this title, the judicial authority may impose one or more of the consequences provided for in article 129 of this Code when the guilty party belongs to a society, organization or association, even of a temporary nature, that is dedicated to carrying out such activities.

TITLE VI

Offenses against liberty

CHAPTER I

Illegal detentions and kidnappings

Article 163

1.	A private individual who confines or detains another, depriving them of their liberty, shall be punished with a prison sentence of four to six years.
2.	If the guilty party releases the confined or detained person within the first three days of their detention, without having achieved the objective they had proposed, the penalty one degree lower shall be imposed.
3.	A prison sentence of five to eight years shall be imposed if the confinement or detention has lasted more than fifteen days.
4.	A private individual who, outside the cases permitted by law, apprehends a person to present them immediately to the authority, shall be punished with a fine of three to six months.

Article 164

The kidnapping of a person demanding some condition for their release shall be punished with a prison sentence of six to ten years. If in the kidnapping the circumstance of article 163.3 has occurred, the penalty one degree higher shall be imposed, and one degree lower if the conditions of article 163.2 are met.

Article 165

The penalties of the preceding articles shall be imposed in their upper half, in the respective cases, if the illegal detention or kidnapping has been carried out with simulation of

authority or public function, or the victim is a minor or a person with a disability in need of special protection or a public official in the exercise of their functions.

Article 166

1.	The perpetrator of illegal detention or kidnapping who does not give an account of the whereabouts of the detained person shall be punished with a prison sentence of ten to fifteen years, in the case of illegal detention, and fifteen to twenty years in the case of kidnapping.
2.	The act shall be punished with a prison sentence of fifteen to twenty years, in the case of illegal detention, and twenty to twenty-five years in prison, in the case of kidnapping, when any of the following circumstances concur:
a)	That the victim was a minor or a person with a disability in need of special protection.
b)	That the perpetrator had carried out the illegal detention or kidnapping with the intention of attacking the liberty or sexual integrity of the victim, or had subsequently acted with that purpose.

Article 167

1.	The authority or public official who, outside the cases permitted by law, and without there being cause for an offense, commits any of the acts described in this Chapter shall be punished with the penalties respectively provided therein, in their upper half, and may reach the degree higher.
2.	The same penalties shall be imposed on:
a)	The public official or authority who, whether or not there is cause for an offense, orders, carries out or prolongs the deprivation of liberty of any person and does not acknowledge said deprivation of liberty or, in any other way, conceals the situation or whereabouts of that person, depriving them of their constitutional or legal rights.
b)	The private individual who has carried out the acts with the authorization, support or acquiescence of the State or its authorities.
3.	In all cases in which the acts referred to in this article have been committed by an authority or public official, they shall also be imposed the penalty of absolute disqualification for a period of eight to twelve years.

Article 168

Incitement, conspiracy and proposal to commit the offenses provided for in this Chapter shall be punished with the penalty one or two degrees lower than that assigned to the offense in question.

CHAPTER II

Threats

Article 169

Whoever threatens another with causing them, their family or other persons with whom they are intimately linked a harm that constitutes offenses of homicide, injuries, abortion, against liberty, torture and against moral integrity, sexual freedom, privacy, honor, property and the socioeconomic order, shall be punished:
1.	º With a prison sentence of one to five years, if the threat has been made demanding an amount or imposing any other condition, even if not unlawful, and the guilty party has achieved their purpose. If they do not achieve it, a prison sentence of six months to three years shall be imposed.
The penalties indicated in the preceding paragraph shall be imposed in their upper half if the threats are made in writing, by telephone or by any means of communication or reproduction, or in the name of real or supposed entities or groups.
2.	º With a prison sentence of six months to two years, when the threat has not been conditional.

Article 170

1.	If threats of a harm that would constitute a crime were directed at terrorising the inhabitants of a town, ethnic, cultural or religious group, or social or professional collective, or any other group of persons, and had the gravity necessary to achieve this, the penalties superior in degree to those provided for in the preceding article shall be imposed respectively.
2.	Those who, with the same purpose and gravity, publicly call for the commission of violent actions by terrorist organisations or groups shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years.

Article 171

1.	Threats of a harm that does not constitute a crime shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from three months to one year or a fine of six to 24 months, having regard to the gravity and circumstances of the act, when the threat is conditional and the condition does not consist of conduct that is due. If the offender has achieved his purpose, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half.
2.	If someone demands from another a sum or reward under the threat of revealing or disseminating facts relating to his private life or family relations that are not publicly known and may affect his reputation, credit or interest, he shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from two to four years, if he has obtained delivery of all or part of what was demanded, and with that of four months to two years, if he did not obtain it.
3.	If the act described in the preceding paragraph consists of the threat to reveal or report the commission of some crime, the public prosecutor may, to facilitate the punishment of the threat, refrain from prosecuting for the crime whose revelation was threatened, unless the latter is punishable with a penalty of imprisonment exceeding two years. In the latter case, the judge or court may reduce the sanction by one or two degrees.
4.	He who in a minor way threatens a person who is or has been his wife, or a woman who is or has been linked to him by an analogous relationship of affection even without cohabitation, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year or community service from thirty-one to eighty days and, in any case, deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from one year and one day to three years, as well as, when the Judge or Court deems it appropriate to the interest of the minor or person with disability in need of special protection, special disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or fostering for up to five years.
The same penalty shall be imposed on he who in a minor way threatens a particularly vulnerable person who lives with the perpetrator.
5.	He who in a minor way threatens with weapons or other dangerous instruments any of the persons referred to in article 173.2, except those contemplated in the preceding paragraph of this article, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from three months to one year or community service from thirty-one to eighty days and, in any case, deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from one to three years, as well as, when the Judge or Court deems it appropriate to the interest of the minor or person with disability in need of special protection, special disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or fostering for a period from six months to three years.
The penalties provided for in paragraphs 4 and 5 shall be imposed in their upper half when the crime is perpetrated in the presence of minors, or takes place in the common home or in the victim's home, or is committed in breach of a penalty of those contemplated in article 48 of this Code or a precautionary or security measure of the same nature.
6.	Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 4 and 5, the Judge or Court, giving reasons in the judgment, having regard to the personal circumstances of the perpetrator and those concurrent in the commission of the act, may impose the penalty lower in degree.
7.	Outside the preceding cases, he who in a minor way threatens another shall be punished with the penalty of a fine from one to three months. This act shall only be prosecutable by complaint of the aggrieved person or his legal representative.
When the victim is any of the persons referred to in paragraph 2 of article 173, the penalty shall be permanent location from five to thirty days, always in a different home and away from that of the victim, or community service from

five to thirty days, or a fine from one to four months, the latter only in cases where the circumstances expressed in paragraph 2 of article 84 are present. In these cases the complaint referred to in the preceding paragraph shall not be required.

CHAPTER III

On coercion

Article 172

1.	He who, without being lawfully authorised, prevents another with violence from doing what the law does not prohibit, or compels him to do what he does not wish, whether just or unjust, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to three years or with a fine of 12 to 24 months, according to the gravity of the coercion or the means employed.
When the coercion exercised had as its object preventing the exercise of a fundamental right, the penalties shall be imposed in their upper half, unless the act had a greater penalty assigned in another provision of this Code.
The penalties shall also be imposed in their upper half when the coercion exercised had as its object preventing the lawful enjoyment of housing.
2.	He who in a minor way coerces a person who is or has been his wife, or a woman who is or has been linked to him by an analogous relationship of affection, even without cohabitation, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year or community service from thirty-one to eighty days and, in any case, deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from one year and one day to three years, as well as, when the Judge or Court deems it appropriate to the interest of the minor or person with disability in need of special protection, special disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or fostering for up to five years.
The same penalty shall be imposed on he who in a minor way coerces a particularly vulnerable person who lives with the perpetrator.
The penalty shall be imposed in its upper half when the crime is perpetrated in the presence of minors, or takes place in the common home or in the victim's home, or is committed in breach of a penalty of those contemplated in article 48 of this Code or a precautionary or security measure of the same nature.
Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraphs, the Judge or Court, giving reasons in the judgment, having regard to the personal circumstances of the perpetrator and those concurrent in the commission of the act, may impose the penalty lower in degree.
3.	Outside the preceding cases, he who causes another coercion of a minor nature shall be punished with the penalty of a fine from one to three months. This act shall only be prosecutable by complaint of the aggrieved person or his legal representative.
When the victim is any of the persons referred to in paragraph 2 of article 173, the penalty shall be permanent location from five to thirty days, always in a different home and away from that of the victim, or community service from five to thirty days, or a fine from one to four months, the latter only in cases where the circumstances expressed in paragraph 2 of article 84 are present. In these cases the complaint referred to in the preceding paragraph shall not be required.

Article 172 bis

1.	Whoever compels another person to contract marriage by means of serious intimidation or violence shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from six months to three years and six months or with a fine of twelve to twenty-four months, according to the seriousness of the coercion or the means employed.
2.	The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever, for the purpose of committing the acts referred to in the preceding paragraph, uses violence, serious intimidation or deceit to force another to leave Spanish territory or not to return to it.
3.	The penalties shall be imposed in their upper half when the victim is a minor.
4.	In convictions for the offence of forced marriage, in addition to the pronouncement corresponding to civil liability, those that

are appropriate shall be made regarding the declaration of nullity or dissolution of the marriage thus contracted and regarding filiation and the fixing of maintenance.

Article 172 ter

1.	Whoever harasses a person by carrying out insistently and repeatedly, and without being legitimately authorised, any of the following conducts and, in this way, alters the normal development of their daily life, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from three months to two years or a fine of six to twenty-four months:
1.	ª Watches them, pursues them or seeks their physical proximity.
2.	ª Establishes or attempts to establish contact with them through any means of communication, or through third parties.
3.	ª Through the improper use of their personal data, acquires products or goods, or contracts services, or causes third parties to contact them.
4.	ª Attacks their freedom or their property, or the freedom or property of another person close to them. When the victim is in a situation of special vulnerability by reason of their age, illness, disability or any other circumstance, the penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years shall be imposed.
2.	When the offended party is any of the persons referred to in paragraph 2 of Article 173, a penalty of imprisonment from one to two years, or community service from sixty to one hundred and twenty days, shall be imposed. In this case the complaint referred to in paragraph 4 of this Article shall not be necessary.
3.	The penalties provided for in this Article shall be imposed without prejudice to those that may correspond to the offences in which the acts of harassment have materialised.
4.	The acts described in this Article shall only be prosecutable by complaint of the aggrieved person or their legal representative.
5.	Whoever, without the consent of its holder, uses the image of a person to place advertisements or open false profiles on social networks, contact pages or any means of public dissemination, causing them a situation of harassment, hostility or humiliation, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from three months to one year or a fine of six to twelve months. If the victim of the offence is a minor or a person with a disability, the upper half of the sentence shall be applied.

Article 172 quater

1.	Whoever, in order to obstruct the exercise of the right to voluntary termination of pregnancy, harasses a woman by means of annoying, offensive, intimidating or coercive acts that undermine her freedom, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from three months to one year or community service from thirty-one to eighty days.
2.	The same penalties shall be imposed on whoever, in the manner described in the preceding paragraph, harasses workers in the health field in their professional practice or public function and the professional or managerial staff of centres authorised to terminate pregnancy with the objective of obstructing the exercise of their profession or position.
3.	Having regard to the seriousness, the personal circumstances of the perpetrator and those concurrent in the commission of the act, the court may additionally impose the prohibition from attending certain places for a period of six months to three years.
4.	The penalties provided for in this Article shall be imposed without prejudice to those that may correspond to the offences in which the acts of harassment have materialised.
5.	In the prosecution of the acts described in this Article, the complaint of the aggrieved person or their legal representation shall not be necessary.

TITLE VII

On torture and other offences against moral integrity

Article 173

1.	Whoever inflicts degrading treatment on another person, seriously undermining their moral integrity, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to two years.

The same penalty shall be imposed on those who, having knowledge of the whereabouts of a person's corpse, repeatedly conceal such information from the relatives or close ones of that person.
The same penalty shall be imposed on those who, within the scope of any employment or official relationship and taking advantage of their position of superiority, repeatedly carry out hostile or humiliating acts against another person which, without constituting degrading treatment, constitute serious harassment of the victim.
The same penalty shall also be imposed on whoever repeatedly carries out hostile or humiliating acts which, without constituting degrading treatment, are intended to prevent the legitimate enjoyment of housing.
When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis, a legal person is responsible for the offences contained in the preceding paragraphs, it shall be punished with a fine of six months to two years. Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, Judges and Courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.
2.	Whoever habitually exercises physical or psychological violence against a person who is or has been their spouse or against a person who is or has been linked to them by an analogous relationship of affection even without cohabitation, or against descendants, ascendants or siblings by nature, adoption or affinity, their own or those of the spouse or cohabitant, or against minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection who live with them or who are subject to the authority, guardianship, curatorship, foster care or de facto care of the spouse or cohabitant, or against a person protected by any other relationship by which they are integrated into the nucleus of their family cohabitation, as well as against persons who by reason of their special vulnerability are subject to custody or care in public or private centres, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to three years, deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for three to five years and, where appropriate, when the judge or court deems it appropriate to the interest of the minor or person with a disability in need of special protection, special disqualification from the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, care or foster care for a period of one to five years, without prejudice to the penalties that may correspond to the offences in which the acts of physical or psychological violence have been specified.
The penalties shall be imposed in their upper half when one or more of the acts of violence are perpetrated in the presence of minors, or using weapons, or take place in the common home or in the victim's home, or are carried out in breach of one of the penalties contemplated in Article 48 or a precautionary or security measure or prohibition of the same nature.
In the cases referred to in this paragraph, a measure of supervised release may also be imposed.
3.	In order to assess the habitual nature referred to in the preceding paragraph, regard shall be had to the number of acts of violence that are proven, as well as their temporal proximity, regardless of whether such violence has been exercised against the same or different victims of those included in this article, and whether or not the violent acts have been the subject of prosecution in previous proceedings.
4.	Whoever causes injury or unjust vexation of a minor nature, when the offended party is one of the persons referred to in paragraph 2 of Article 173, shall be punished with a penalty of permanent location for five to thirty days, always in a different home and away from that of the victim, or community service for five to thirty days, or a fine of one to four months, the latter only in cases where the circumstances expressed in paragraph 2 of Article 84 are present.
The same penalties shall be imposed on those who address another person with expressions, behaviour or propositions of a sexual nature that create for the victim an objectively humiliating, hostile or intimidating situation, without constituting other offences of greater gravity.
The offences defined in the two preceding paragraphs shall only be prosecutable upon complaint by the aggrieved person or their legal representative.

Article 174

1.	An authority or public official who, abusing their position, and with the purpose of obtaining a confession or information from any person or of punishing them for any act they have committed or are suspected of having committed, or for any reason based on some type of discrimination, subjects them to conditions or procedures which by their nature, duration or other circumstances, cause them physical or mental suffering, the suppression or diminution of their faculties of knowledge, discernment or decision or which, in any other way, violate their moral integrity, commits torture. The person guilty of torture shall be punished with a prison sentence of two to six years if the violation is serious, and imprisonment of one to three years if it is not. In addition to the penalties indicated, the penalty of absolute disqualification for eight to 12 years shall be imposed in all cases.
2.	The same penalties shall be incurred, respectively, by the authority or official of penitentiary institutions or centres for the protection or correction of minors who commits, with respect to detainees, inmates or prisoners, the acts referred to in the preceding paragraph.

Article 175

An authority or public official who, abusing their position and outside the cases covered by the preceding article, violates the moral integrity of a person shall be punished with a prison sentence of two to four years if the violation is serious, and imprisonment of six months to two years if it is not. In all cases, in addition to the penalties indicated, the author shall be subject to the penalty of special disqualification from employment or public office for two to four years.

Article 176

The penalties respectively established in the preceding articles shall be imposed on the authority or official who, failing in the duties of their position, permits other persons to execute the acts provided for therein.

Article 177

If in the offences described in the preceding articles, in addition to the violation of moral integrity, injury or harm is caused to the life, physical integrity, health, sexual freedom or property of the victim or of a third party, the acts shall be punished separately with the penalty corresponding to the offences committed, except when the former is already specially punished by law.

TITLE VII BIS

On trafficking in human beings

Article 177 bis

1. Whoever, whether in Spanish territory, from Spain, in transit or destined for it, employing violence, intimidation or deception, or abusing a situation of superiority or of necessity or of vulnerability of the national or foreign victim, or by means of the delivery or receipt of payments or benefits to obtain the consent of the person who possessed control over the victim, recruits, transports, transfers, harbours, or receives her or him, including the exchange or transfer of control over such persons, for any of the following purposes, shall be punished with a sentence of five to eight years' imprisonment as guilty of trafficking in human beings:
a) The imposition of forced labour or services, slavery or practices similar to slavery, servitude or begging.
b) Sexual exploitation, including pornography.
c) Exploitation to carry out criminal activities.
d) The removal of their bodily organs.
e) The celebration of forced marriages.

A situation of necessity or vulnerability exists when the person in question has no other alternative, real or acceptable, than to submit to the abuse.
When the victim of trafficking in human beings is a minor, the penalty of special disqualification from any profession, trade or activities, whether remunerated or not, that entails regular and direct contact with minors, for a period exceeding between six and twenty years that of the duration of the custodial sentence imposed, shall be imposed in all cases.
2. Even when none of the means set out in the preceding paragraph is used, any of the actions indicated in the preceding paragraph shall be considered trafficking in human beings when carried out in respect of minors for purposes of exploitation.
3. The consent of a victim of trafficking in human beings shall be irrelevant when any of the means indicated in the first paragraph of this article has been used.
4. The penalty higher in degree than that provided for in the first paragraph of this article shall be imposed when:
a) the life or physical or psychological integrity of the persons subject to the offence has been endangered;
b) the victim is especially vulnerable by reason of illness, pregnancy, disability or personal situation, or is a minor.
c) the victim is a person whose situation of vulnerability has been caused or aggravated by displacement resulting from an armed conflict or a humanitarian catastrophe.
If more than one circumstance is present, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half.
5. The penalty higher in degree than that provided for in paragraph 1 of this article and absolute disqualification from six to twelve years shall be imposed on those who commit the acts by taking advantage of their status as an authority, agent thereof or public official. If any of the circumstances provided for in paragraph 4 of this article is also present, the penalties shall be imposed in their upper half.
6. The penalty higher in degree than that provided for in paragraph 1 of this article and special disqualification from profession, trade, industry or commerce for the duration of the sentence shall be imposed when the offender belongs to an organisation or association of more than two persons, even of a transitory nature, engaged in carrying out such activities. If any of the circumstances provided for in paragraph 4 of this article is present, the penalties shall be imposed in the upper half. If the circumstance provided for in paragraph 5 of this article is present, the penalties indicated therein shall be imposed in their upper half.
When the leaders, administrators or managers of such organisations or associations are concerned, the penalty shall be applied in its upper half, which may be raised to the immediately higher degree. In all cases the penalty shall be raised to the immediately higher degree if any of the circumstances provided for in paragraph 4 or the circumstance provided for in paragraph 5 of this article is present.
7. When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is liable for the offences covered by this article, it shall be subject to a fine of three to five times the profit obtained. Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.
8. Incitement, conspiracy and proposal to commit the offence of trafficking in human beings shall be punished with a penalty one or two degrees lower than that for the corresponding offence.
9. In all cases, the penalties provided for in this article shall be imposed without prejudice to those that may correspond, as the case may be, for the offence under Article 318 bis of this Code and other offences actually committed, including those constituting the corresponding exploitation.
10. Convictions by foreign judges or courts for offences of the same nature as those provided for in this article shall have the effects of recidivism, unless the criminal record has been cancelled or may be cancelled under Spanish law.

11. Without prejudice to the application of the general rules of this Code, the victim of trafficking in human beings shall be exempt from penalty for criminal offences committed in the situation of exploitation suffered, provided that their participation in them was a direct consequence of the situation of violence, intimidation, deception or abuse to which they were subjected and that there is adequate proportionality between that situation and the criminal act committed.

TITLE VIII

Offences against sexual freedom

CHAPTER I

Sexual assaults

Article 178

1. Whoever commits any act that violates the sexual freedom of another person without their consent shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from one to four years, as liable for sexual assault. Consent shall only be understood to exist when it has been freely expressed by acts which, having regard to the circumstances of the case, clearly express the will of the person.
2. Acts of a sexual nature committed by employing violence, intimidation or abuse of a situation of superiority or of vulnerability of the victim, as well as those carried out on persons who are deprived of their senses or whose mental state is abused and those committed when the victim has their will annulled for any reason, shall be considered in all cases sexual assault.
3. If the assault was committed by employing violence or intimidation or on a victim whose will is annulled for any reason, the offender shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from one to five years.
4. The sentencing body, giving reasons in the judgment, and provided that violence or intimidation is not involved or that the victim did not have their will annulled for any reason or the circumstances of Article 180 are not present, may impose the sentence of imprisonment in its lower half or a fine of eighteen to twenty-four months, having regard to the lesser seriousness of the act and the personal circumstances of the offender.

Article 179

1. When the sexual assault consists of carnal access by vaginal, anal or oral means, or introduction of bodily members or objects by either of the first two means, the offender shall be punished as guilty of rape with a sentence of imprisonment from four to twelve years.
2. If the assault referred to in the preceding paragraph is committed by employing violence or intimidation or when the victim has their will annulled for any reason, a sentence of imprisonment from six to twelve years shall be imposed.

Article 180

1. The preceding conducts shall be punished, respectively, with the penalties of imprisonment from two to eight years for the assaults of article 178.1, imprisonment from five to ten years for the assaults of article 178.3, imprisonment from seven to fifteen years for the assaults of article 179.1 and imprisonment from twelve to fifteen years for those of article 179.2, when any of the following circumstances is present:
1. ª When the acts are committed through the joint action of two or more persons.
2. ª When the sexual assault is preceded or accompanied by violence of extreme gravity or by acts of a particularly degrading or humiliating nature.

3. ª When the acts are committed against a person who is in a situation of special vulnerability by reason of age, illness, disability or any other circumstance, except as provided in article 181.
4. ª When the victim is or has been the spouse or woman who is or has been linked by an analogous relationship of affection, even without cohabitation.
5. ª When, for the execution of the offence, the responsible person had taken advantage of a situation or relationship of cohabitation or kinship or a relationship of superiority with respect to the victim.
6. ª When the responsible person makes use of weapons or other equally dangerous means, capable of producing death or any of the injuries provided for in articles 149 and 150 of this Code, without prejudice to the provisions of article 194 bis.
7. ª When for the commission of these acts the responsible person has nullified the will of the victim by supplying pharmaceuticals, drugs or any other natural or chemical substance suitable for that purpose.
