The amount to be reimbursed shall be deemed to be provisionally fixed by the Administration, and shall be adjusted afterwards to what is finally decided in the criminal proceedings.
The criminal proceedings shall not paralyse the Administration's collection action either, which may initiate actions directed at collection unless the judge, ex officio or at the request of a party, has agreed to suspend enforcement actions upon provision of security. If security cannot be provided in whole or in part, exceptionally the judge may agree to suspension with total or partial waiver of security if he or she considers that enforcement could cause irreparable damage or damage that is very difficult to repair.
8.	Judges and courts may impose on the person responsible for this offence a penalty one or two degrees lower, provided that, before two months have elapsed from the judicial summons as a person under investigation, he or she carries out the reimbursement referred to in paragraph 6 and judicially acknowledges the facts. The foregoing shall be equally applicable with respect to other participants in the offence other than the person obliged to make reimbursement or the perpetrator of the offence, when they actively collaborate to obtain decisive evidence for the identification or capture of other

persons responsible, for the complete clarification of the criminal facts or for the ascertainment of the assets of the person obliged or the person responsible for the offence.

Article 308 bis

1.	The suspension of the execution of penalties imposed for any of the offences regulated in this Title shall be governed by the provisions contained in Chapter III of Title III of Book I of this Code, supplemented by the following rules:
1.	ª The suspension of the execution of the prison sentence imposed shall require, in addition to compliance with the requirements regulated in article 80, that the convicted person has paid the tax debt or the debt to Social Security, or has proceeded to reimburse the subsidies or aid improperly received or used.
This requirement shall be deemed to be met when the convicted person undertakes to satisfy the tax debt, the debt to Social Security or to proceed to reimburse the subsidies or aid improperly received or used and the civil liabilities according to his or her economic capacity and to facilitate the agreed confiscation, and it is reasonable to expect that it will be fulfilled. Suspension shall not be granted when it is established that the convicted person has provided inaccurate or insufficient information about his or her assets.
The decision by which the judge or court grants the suspension of the execution of the sentence shall be communicated to the procedural representation of the state, autonomous, local or regional Public Treasury, Social Security or the Administration that granted the subsidy or aid.
2.	ª The judge or court shall revoke the suspension and order the execution of the sentence, in addition to the cases in article 86, when the convicted person does not fulfil the commitment to pay the tax debt or the debt to Social Security, to reimburse the subsidies and aid improperly received or used, or to pay the civil liabilities, provided that he or she has the economic capacity to do so, or provides inaccurate or insufficient information about his or her assets. In these cases, the prison supervision judge may refuse to grant conditional release.
2.	In the case of article 125, the judge or court shall hear beforehand the procedural representation of the state, autonomous, local or regional Public Treasury, Social Security or the Administration that granted the subsidy or aid, for the purpose of providing an asset report on those responsible for the offence in which the real economic and asset capacity of those responsible shall be analysed and a proposal for instalment payment may be included in accordance with said capacity and with tax, Social Security or subsidy regulations.

Article 309. (Repealed)

Article 310

Anyone who, being obliged by tax law to keep mercantile accounting, books or tax registers, shall be punished with imprisonment of five to seven months:
a) Absolutely fails to comply with said obligation under the direct estimation regime for tax bases.
b) Keeps different accounting records that, referring to the same activity and fiscal year, conceal or simulate the true situation of the company.
c) Has not recorded in the mandatory books businesses, acts, operations or, in general, economic transactions, or has recorded them with figures different from the true ones.
d) Has made fictitious accounting entries in the mandatory books.
The consideration as a crime of the factual situations referred to in paragraphs
c) and d) above shall require that tax returns have been omitted or that those filed reflect false accounting and that the amount, more or less, of the omitted or falsified debits or credits exceeds, without arithmetic compensation between them,
240,000 euros for each fiscal year.

Article 310 bis

When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is responsible for the crimes included in this Title, the following penalties shall be imposed:
a) Fine of one to two times the amount defrauded or unduly obtained, if the crime committed by the natural person carries a penalty of imprisonment of more than two years.
b) Fine of two to four times the amount defrauded or unduly obtained, if the crime committed by the natural person carries a penalty of imprisonment of more than five years.
c) Fine of six months to one year, in the cases included in Article 310.
In addition to those indicated, the responsible legal person shall be subject to the loss of the possibility of obtaining subsidies or public aid and the right to enjoy tax or Social Security benefits or incentives for a period of three to six years. The prohibition to contract with Public Administrations may be imposed.
Taking into account the rules established in Article 66 bis, Judges and Courts may also impose the penalties included in letters b), c), d), e) and g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.

TITLE XV

Crimes against the rights of workers

Article 311

The following shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to six years and a fine of six to twelve months:
1. Those who, through deception or abuse of a situation of need, impose on workers in their service working conditions or Social Security conditions that harm, suppress or restrict the rights they have recognized by legal provisions, collective agreements or individual contract.
2. Those who impose illegal conditions on their workers by hiring them under formulas other than the employment contract, or maintain them against administrative requirement or sanction.
3. Those who simultaneously employ a plurality of workers without reporting their registration in the corresponding Social Security system or, where applicable, without having obtained the corresponding work authorization, provided that the number of affected workers is at least:
a) twenty-five percent, in companies or workplaces that employ more than one hundred workers,
b) fifty percent, in companies or workplaces that employ more than ten workers and not more than one hundred, or
c) all of them, in companies or workplaces that employ more than five and not more than ten workers.
4. Those who, in the case of business transfers, with knowledge of the procedures described in the preceding paragraphs, maintain the aforementioned conditions imposed by another.
5. If the conduct described in the preceding paragraphs is carried out with violence or intimidation, the penalties shall be imposed in their higher degree.

Article 311 bis

Anyone who, unless the facts are punished with a more severe penalty in another provision of this Code, shall be punished with imprisonment of three to eighteen months or a fine of twelve to thirty months:
a) Repeatedly employs or gives occupation to foreign citizens who lack a work permit, or

b) employs or gives occupation to a minor who lacks a work permit.

Article 312

1. Those who illegally traffic in labor shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of six to twelve months.
2. The same penalty shall be incurred by those who recruit persons or induce them to leave their job by offering deceptive or false employment or working conditions, and those who employ foreign nationals without a work permit under conditions that harm, suppress or restrict the rights they have recognized by legal provisions, collective agreements or individual contract.

Article 313

Anyone who induces or facilitates the emigration of any person to another country by simulating a contract or placement, or using other similar deception, shall be punished with the penalty provided in the preceding article.

Article 314

Those who produce serious discrimination in employment, public or private, against any person by reason of their ideology, religion or beliefs, their family situation, their belonging to an ethnicity, race or nation, their national origin, their sex, age, sexual orientation or gender identity, gender reasons, aporophobia or social exclusion, the illness they suffer or their disability, for holding the legal or union representation of workers, for kinship with other workers of the company or for the use of any of the official languages within the Spanish State, and do not restore the situation of equality before the law after administrative requirement or sanction, repairing the economic damages that have resulted, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years or a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.

Article 315

1. Those who, through deception or abuse of a situation of need, prevent or limit the exercise of freedom of association or the right to strike shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years or a fine of six to twelve months.
2. If the conduct described in the preceding paragraph is carried out with coercion, it shall be punished with imprisonment of one year and nine months to three years or with a fine of eighteen months to twenty-four months.

Article 316

Those who, in violation of occupational risk prevention regulations and being legally obliged, do not provide the necessary means for workers to carry out their activity with adequate safety and hygiene measures, thereby seriously endangering their life, health or physical integrity, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of six to twelve months.

Article 317

When the crime referred to in the preceding article is committed through gross negligence, it shall be punished with the penalty one degree lower.

Article 318

When the acts provided for in the articles of this title are attributed to legal persons, the penalty indicated shall be imposed on the administrators or those in charge of the service who have been responsible for them and on those who, knowing of them and being able to remedy them, have not adopted measures to do so. In these cases the judicial authority may also decree one or more of the measures provided for in Article 129 of this Code.

TITLE XV BIS

Crimes against the rights of foreign citizens

Article 318 bis

1.	Whoever intentionally assists a person who is not a national of a Member State of the European Union to enter Spanish territory or to transit through it in a manner that violates the legislation on entry or transit of foreigners, shall be punished with a fine of three to twelve months or imprisonment of three months to one year.
The acts shall not be punishable when the objective pursued by the perpetrator was solely to provide humanitarian assistance to the person in question.
If the acts had been committed for profit, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half.
2.	Whoever intentionally assists, for profit, a person who is not a national of a Member State of the European Union to remain in Spain, violating the legislation on stay of foreigners, shall be punished with a fine of three to twelve months or imprisonment of three months to one year.
3.	The acts referred to in paragraph 1 of this article shall be punished with imprisonment of four to eight years when any of the following circumstances concur:
a)	When the acts had been committed within an organization dedicated to carrying out such activities. When it concerns the leaders, administrators or managers of such organizations or associations, the penalty shall be applied in its upper half, which may be raised to the immediately higher degree.
b)	When the lives of the persons subject to the offence had been endangered, or the danger of causing serious injuries had been created.
4.	Those who commit the acts by taking advantage of their status as an authority, agent thereof or public official shall incur the same penalties as in the preceding paragraph and also that of absolute disqualification from six to twelve years.
5.	When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is responsible for the offences contained in this Title, it shall be imposed the penalty of a fine of two to five years, or that of triple to quintuple the benefit obtained if the resulting amount were higher.
Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties contained in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.
6.	The courts, taking into account the seriousness of the act and its circumstances, the conditions of the guilty party and the purpose pursued by the latter, may impose the penalty one degree lower than that respectively indicated.

TITLE XVI

On offences relating to spatial planning and urbanism, the protection of historical heritage and the environment

CHAPTER I

On offences concerning spatial planning and urbanism

Article 319

1.	The penalties of imprisonment of one year and six months to four years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months, unless the benefit obtained from the offence were greater than the resulting amount in which case the fine shall be from the equivalent to triple the amount of said benefit, and special disqualification for profession or trade for a period of one to four years, shall be imposed on promoters, builders or technical directors who carry out urbanization, construction or building works not authorizable on land designated for roads, green areas, public domain property or places that have legally or administratively recognized their

landscape, ecological, artistic, historical or cultural value, or for the same reasons have been considered of special protection.
2.	The penalty of imprisonment of one to three years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months, unless the benefit obtained from the offence were greater than the resulting amount in which case the fine shall be from the equivalent to triple the amount of said benefit, and special disqualification for profession or trade for a period of one to four years, shall be imposed on promoters, builders or technical directors who carry out urbanization, construction or building works not authorizable on non-developable land.
3.	In any case, judges or courts, with reasoning, may order, at the expense of the perpetrator of the act, the demolition of the work and the restoration to its original state of the altered physical reality, without prejudice to compensation due to third parties in good faith, and assessing the circumstances, and having heard the competent Administration, shall make the demolition temporarily conditional on the provision of guarantees that ensure the payment of those. In all cases the confiscation of the profits from the offence shall be ordered whatever transformations they may have undergone.
4.	In the cases provided for in this article, when a legal person is responsible in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis of this Code, it shall be imposed the penalty of a fine of one to three years, unless the benefit obtained from the offence were greater than the resulting amount in which case the fine shall be from double to quadruple the amount of said benefit.
Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties contained in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.

Article 320

1.	The authority or public official who, knowing of its injustice, has reported favorably on planning instruments, urbanization projects, parceling, re-parceling, construction or building or the granting of licenses contrary to the spatial planning or urban planning regulations in force, or who on the occasion of inspections has concealed the infringement of said regulations or who has omitted the carrying out of inspections of an obligatory nature shall be punished with the penalty established in Article 404 of this Code and, in addition, with that of imprisonment of one year and six months to four years and that of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.
2.	With the same penalties shall be punished the authority or public official who by himself or as a member of a collegiate body has resolved or voted in favor of the approval of the planning instruments, urbanization projects, parceling, re-parceling, construction or building or the granting of the licenses referred to in the preceding paragraph, knowing of their injustice.

CHAPTER II

On offences concerning historical heritage

Article 321

Those who demolish or seriously alter buildings specially protected for their historical, artistic, cultural or monumental interest shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment of six months to three years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and, in all cases, special disqualification for profession or trade for a period of one to five years.
In any case, Judges or Courts, with reasoning, may order, at the expense of the perpetrator of the act, the reconstruction or restoration of the work, without prejudice to compensation due to third parties in good faith.

Article 322

1.	The authority or public official who, knowing of its injustice, has reported favorably on projects for demolition or alteration of specially protected buildings shall be punished in addition to the penalty established in Article 404 of this Code with that of imprisonment of six months to two years or that of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.

2.	With the same penalties shall be punished the authority or public official who by himself or as a member of a collegiate body has resolved or voted in favor of its granting knowing of its injustice.

Article 323

1. Anyone who causes damage to property of historical, artistic, scientific, cultural or monumental value, or to archaeological sites, whether terrestrial or underwater, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from six months to three years or a fine of twelve to twenty-four months. Acts of plundering of the latter shall be punished with the same penalty.
2. If damage of special gravity has been caused or if it has affected property whose historical, artistic, scientific, cultural or monumental value was especially significant, the penalty higher in degree than that indicated in the preceding paragraph may be imposed.
3. In all these cases, the judges or courts may order, at the expense of the perpetrator of the damage, the adoption of measures aimed at restoring, as far as possible, the damaged property.

Article 324

Anyone who through gross negligence causes damage, in an amount exceeding 400 euros, to an archive, registry, museum, library, educational centre, scientific cabinet, similar institution or to property of artistic, historical, cultural, scientific or monumental value, as well as to archaeological sites, shall be punished with a penalty of a fine of three to 18 months, having regard to the importance thereof.

CHAPTER III

Offences against natural resources and the environment

Article 325

1. Anyone who, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature protecting the environment, causes or carries out directly or indirectly emissions, discharges, radiation, extractions or excavations, landfills, noise, vibrations, injections or deposits, in the atmosphere, soil, subsoil or terrestrial, underground or maritime waters, including the high seas, with impact even in cross-border areas, as well as water abstractions which, by themselves or together with others, cause or may cause substantial damage to the quality of air, soil or water, or to animals or plants, shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment from six months to two years, a fine of ten to fourteen months and special disqualification from profession or trade for a period of one to two years.
2. If the foregoing conduct, by itself or together with others, could seriously harm the balance of natural systems, a penalty of imprisonment from two to five years, a fine of eight to twenty-four months and special disqualification from profession or trade for a period of one to three years shall be imposed.
If a risk of serious harm to the health of persons has been created, the penalty of imprisonment shall be imposed in its upper half, and may reach the higher degree.

Article 326

1. Those who, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature, collect, transport, recover, transform, dispose of or exploit waste, or do not adequately control or supervise such activities, in such a way that they cause or may cause substantial damage to the quality of air, soil or water, or to animals or plants, death or serious injury to persons, or may seriously harm the balance of natural systems, shall be punished with the penalties provided for in the preceding article, in their respective cases.
2. Anyone who, outside the case referred to in the preceding paragraph, transfers a non-negligible quantity of waste, whether in the case of one or several transfers that appear to be linked, in any of the cases referred to in the law of the European Union

relating to waste shipments, shall be punished with a penalty of three months to one year of imprisonment, or a fine of six to eighteen months and special disqualification from profession or trade for a period of three months to one year.

Article 326 bis

Those who, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature, carry out the operation of installations in which a dangerous activity is carried out or in which hazardous substances or preparations are stored or used in such a way that they cause or may cause substantial damage to the quality of air, soil or water, to animals or plants, death or serious injury to persons, or may seriously harm the balance of natural systems, shall be punished with the penalties provided for in Article 325, in their respective cases.

Article 327

The acts referred to in the three preceding articles shall be punished with the penalty higher in degree, without prejudice to those that may correspond in accordance with other provisions of this Code, when in the commission of any of the acts described in the preceding article any of the following circumstances concur:
a) That the industry or activity operates clandestinely, without having obtained the mandatory authorisation or administrative approval of its installations.
b) That express orders of the administrative authority to correct or suspend the activities classified in the preceding article have been disobeyed.
c) That information about the environmental aspects thereof has been falsified or concealed.
d) That the inspection activity of the Administration has been obstructed.
e) That a risk of irreversible or catastrophic deterioration has occurred.
f) That an illegal extraction of water occurs in a period of restrictions.

Article 328

When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is liable for the offences contained in this Chapter, the following penalties shall be imposed:
a) A fine of one to three years, or double to quadruple the damage caused when the resulting amount is higher, if the offence committed by the natural person carries a penalty of more than two years of deprivation of liberty.
b) A fine of six months to two years, or double to triple the damage caused if the resulting amount is higher, in other cases.
Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties contained in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.

Article 329

1. The authority or public official who, knowingly, has reported favourably on the granting of manifestly illegal licences authorising the operation of polluting industries or activities referred to in the preceding articles, or who on the occasion of their inspections has concealed the infringement of laws or regulatory provisions of a general nature governing them, or who has omitted the carrying out of inspections of a mandatory nature, shall be punished with the penalty established in Article 404 of this Code and, in addition, with that of imprisonment from six months to three years and that of a fine of eight to twenty-four months.
2. The authority or public official who by himself or as a member of a collegiate body has resolved or voted in favour of its granting knowing of its unlawfulness shall be punished with the same penalties.

Article 330

Anyone who, in a protected natural area, seriously damages any of the elements that have served to classify it, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment from one to four years and a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.

Article 331

The acts provided for in this chapter shall be punished, where appropriate, with the penalty lower in degree, in their respective cases, when they have been committed through gross negligence.

CHAPTER IV

Offences against flora and fauna.

Article 332

1.	Whoever, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature, cuts, fells, uproots, collects, acquires, possesses or destroys protected species of wild flora, or traffics in them, their parts, derivatives thereof or their propagules, except where the conduct affects an insignificant quantity of specimens and has no relevant consequences for the conservation status of the species, shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from six months to two years or a fine of eight to twenty-four months, and special disqualification from profession or trade for a period of six months to two years.
The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature, destroys or seriously alters their habitat.
2.	The penalty shall be imposed in its upper half if the species or subspecies are catalogued as endangered.
3.	If the acts had been committed through serious negligence, a sentence of imprisonment from three months to one year or a fine of four to eight months shall be imposed, and special disqualification from profession or trade for a period of three months to two years.

Article 333

Whoever introduces or releases non-native species of flora or fauna in a manner that harms the biological balance, in contravention of laws or provisions of a general nature protecting species of flora or fauna, shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from four months to two years or a fine of eight to twenty-four months and, in any case, special disqualification from profession or trade for a period of one to three years.

Article 334

1.	Whoever, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature, shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from six months to two years or a fine of eight to twenty-four months and, in any case, special disqualification from profession or trade and special disqualification from exercising the right to hunt or fish for a period of two to four years:
a)	hunts, fishes, acquires, possesses or destroys protected species of wild fauna;
b)	traffics in them, their parts or derivatives thereof; or,
c)	carries out activities that prevent or hinder their reproduction or migration.
The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature, destroys or seriously alters their habitat.
2.	The penalty shall be imposed in its upper half if the species or subspecies are catalogued as endangered.
3.	If the acts had been committed through serious negligence, a sentence of imprisonment from three months to one year or a fine of four to eight months shall be imposed and, in any case, special disqualification from profession or trade and special disqualification from exercising the right to hunt or fish for a period of three months to two years.
4.	The penalty of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for a period of between two to four years shall be imposed, when the acts referred to in paragraphs a) and c) of

paragraph 1 had been committed using weapons, in activities related or not to hunting.

Article 335

1.	Whoever hunts or fishes species other than those indicated in the preceding article, when expressly prohibited by specific rules on their hunting or fishing, shall be punished with a fine of eight to twelve months, special disqualification from exercising the right to hunt or fish for a period of two to five years and deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for the same period.
2.	Whoever hunts or fishes or carries out significant shellfish gathering activities on species other than those indicated in the preceding article on public or private land belonging to others, subject to special hunting regulations, without due permission from its owner or subject to shellfish or aquaculture concession or authorization without due enabling administrative title, shall be punished with a fine of four to eight months and special disqualification from exercising the right to hunt, fish or carry out shellfish gathering activities for a period of one to three years and deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for the same period, in addition to the penalties that may correspond, as applicable, for the commission of the offence provided for in paragraph 1 of this article.
3.	If the foregoing conducts produce serious damage to the hunting heritage of land subject to special hunting regulations or to the sustainability of resources in areas of shellfish or aquaculture concession or authorization, a sentence of imprisonment from six months to two years and special disqualification from exercising the rights to hunt, fish, and carry out shellfish gathering activities for a period of two to five years and deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for the same period shall be imposed.

Article 336

Whoever, without being legally authorized, uses poison, explosive means or other instruments or devices of similar destructive or non-selective efficacy for fauna for hunting or fishing, shall be punished with a sentence of imprisonment from four months to two years or a fine of eight to twenty-four months and, in any case, special disqualification from profession or trade and special disqualification from exercising the right to hunt or fish for a period of one to three years, with deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for the same period. If the damage caused were of notorious importance, the aforementioned sentence of imprisonment shall be imposed in its upper half.

Article 337. (Deleted)

Article 337

bis. (Deleted)

CHAPTER V

Common provisions

Article 338

When the conducts defined in this Title affect any protected natural area, penalties superior in degree to those respectively provided shall be imposed.

Article 339

Judges or courts shall order the adoption, at the expense of the perpetrator of the act, of the necessary measures aimed at restoring the disturbed ecological balance, as well as any other precautionary measure necessary for the protection of the legal interests protected in this Title.

Article 340

If the perpetrator of any of the acts classified in this Title had voluntarily proceeded to repair the damage caused, the Judges and Courts shall impose on him the penalty inferior in degree to those respectively provided.

TITLE XVI BIS

Of offences against animals

Article 340 bis

1.	Anyone who, outside legally regulated activities and by any means or procedure, including acts of a sexual nature, causes injury to a domestic, tamed, domesticated animal or one living temporarily or permanently under human control that requires veterinary treatment for the restoration of its health, shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to eighteen months or a fine of six to twelve months and with the penalty of special disqualification for one to three years from exercising a profession, trade or business related to animals and from keeping animals.
If the injuries referred to in the preceding paragraph are caused to a vertebrate animal not included in the preceding paragraph, a prison sentence of three to twelve months or a fine of three to six months shall be imposed, in addition to the penalty of special disqualification for one to three years from exercising a profession, trade or business related to animals and from keeping animals.
If the offence has been committed using firearms, the judge or court may impose, with reasoning, the penalty of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for a period of one to four years.
2.	The penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph shall be imposed in their upper half when any of the following aggravating circumstances are present:
a)	Using weapons, instruments, objects, means, methods or forms that could be dangerous to the life or health of the animal.
b)	Executing the act with cruelty.
c)	Causing the animal the loss or disablement of a sense, organ or principal limb.
d)	Carrying out the act by its owner or by the person entrusted with the care of the animal.
e)	Executing the act in the presence of a minor or a particularly vulnerable person.
f)	Executing the act for profit.
g)	Committing the act to coerce, intimidate, harass or cause psychological harm to a person who is or has been a spouse or to a person who is or has been linked to the perpetrator by a similar relationship of affection, even without cohabitation.
h)	Executing the act at a public event or disseminating it through information or communication technologies.
i)	Using poison, explosive means or other instruments or devices of similar destructive or non-selective efficacy.
3.	When, on the occasion of the acts provided for in the first paragraph of this article, the death of a domestic, tamed, domesticated animal or one living temporarily or permanently under human control is caused, a prison sentence of twelve to twenty-four months shall be imposed, in addition to the penalty of special disqualification for two to four years from exercising a profession, trade or business related to animals and from keeping animals.
When, on the occasion of the acts provided for in the first paragraph of this article, the death of a vertebrate animal not included in the preceding paragraph is caused, a prison sentence of six to eighteen months or a fine of eighteen to twenty-four months shall be imposed, in addition to the penalty of special disqualification for two to four years from exercising a profession, trade or business related to animals and from keeping animals.

If the offence has been committed using firearms, the judge or court may impose, with reasoning, the penalty of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for a period of two to five years.
When any of the circumstances provided for in the preceding paragraph are present, the judge or court shall impose the penalties in their upper half.
4.	If the injuries caused do not require veterinary treatment or the animal has been seriously mistreated without causing injuries, a fine of one to two months or community service of one to thirty days shall be imposed. Likewise, the penalty of special disqualification for three months to one year from exercising a profession, trade or business related to animals and from keeping animals shall be imposed.

Article 340 ter

Anyone who abandons a vertebrate animal under their responsibility in conditions in which its life or integrity may be endangered shall be punished with a fine of one to six months or community service of thirty-one to ninety days. Likewise, the penalty of special disqualification for one to three years from exercising a profession, trade or business related to animals and from keeping animals shall be imposed.

Article 340 quater

1.	When, in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis, a legal person is liable for the offences contained in this title, the following penalties shall be imposed:
a)	Fine of one to three years, if the offence committed by the natural person is punishable by law with a prison sentence exceeding two years.
b)	Fine of six months to two years, in all other cases.
2.	Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, in cases of liability of legal persons, judges and courts may also impose the penalties contained in Article 33.7, paragraphs b) to g).

Article 340 quinquies

Judges or courts may adopt, with reasoning, any precautionary measure necessary for the protection of the legal interests protected in this Title, including provisional changes to the ownership and care of the animal.
When the penalty of special disqualification from exercising a profession, trade or business related to animals and from keeping animals is imposed on the person who had been assigned ownership or care of the mistreated animal, the judge or court, ex officio or at the request of a party, shall adopt the appropriate measures regarding the ownership and care of the animal.

TITLE XVII

Offences against collective security

CHAPTER I

Offences of catastrophic risk

Section 1

Offences relating to nuclear energy and ionising radiation

Article 341

Anyone who releases nuclear energy or radioactive elements that endanger the life or health of persons or their property, even if no explosion occurs, shall be punished with

a prison sentence of fifteen to twenty years, and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of ten to twenty years.

Article 342

Anyone who, without being covered by the preceding article, disrupts the operation of a nuclear or radioactive installation, or alters the development of activities involving materials or equipment producing ionising radiation, creating a situation of serious danger to the life or health of persons, shall be punished with a prison sentence of four to ten years, and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of six to ten years.

Article 343

1. Whoever, by means of the discharge, emission or introduction into the air, soil or waters of a quantity of materials or ionising radiation, or exposure by any other means to such radiation, endangers the life, physical integrity, health or property of one or more persons, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six to twelve years and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of six to ten years. The same penalty shall be imposed when this conduct endangers the quality of the air, soil or waters or animals or plants.
2. When, on the occasion of the conduct described in the preceding paragraph, in addition to the risk prevented, a harmful result constituting an offence is produced, whatever its seriousness, the judges or courts shall consider only the more seriously punished offence, applying the penalty in its upper half.
3. When, in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis, a legal person is liable for the offences set out in this Article, it shall be subject to a fine of two to five years.
Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, the judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.

Article 344

The acts provided for in the preceding articles shall be punished with the penalty one degree lower, in their respective cases, when they have been committed through gross negligence.

Article 345

1. Whoever, in contravention of laws or other provisions of a general nature, acquires, possesses, traffics in, facilitates, treats, transforms, uses, stores, transports or disposes of nuclear materials or other dangerous radioactive substances that cause or may cause death or serious injury to persons, or substantial damage to the quality of the air, the quality of the soil or the quality of the waters or to animals or plants, shall be punished with a prison sentence of one to five years, a fine of six to eighteen months, and special disqualification from profession or trade for a period of one to three years.
2. Whoever produces such materials or substances without due authorisation shall be punished with the penalty one degree higher.
3. If the acts referred to in the preceding paragraphs had been committed through gross negligence, the penalty one degree lower than that indicated therein shall be imposed.

Section 2 On devastation

Article 346

1. Those who, by causing explosions or using any other means of similar destructive power, cause the destruction of airports, ports, stations, buildings, public premises, depots containing flammable or explosive materials, communication routes, means of collective transport, or the sinking or stranding of a vessel, flooding, explosion of a mine or industrial installation, lifting of the rails of a railway line, malicious alteration of the signals used in its service for the safety of means of transport, blowing up of a bridge, destruction of a public road,

damage to oil pipelines, serious disturbance of any kind or means of communication, disturbance or interruption of the supply of water, electricity, hydrocarbons or other fundamental natural resource shall incur a prison sentence of ten to twenty years, when the devastation necessarily entails a danger to the life or physical integrity of persons.
2. When such danger does not exist, they shall be punished with a penalty of four to eight years' imprisonment.
3. If, in addition to the danger, injury to the life, physical integrity or health of persons has occurred, the acts shall be punished separately with the penalty corresponding to the offence committed.

Article 347

Whoever through gross negligence causes an offence of devastation shall be punished with a prison sentence of one to four years.

Section 3 On other offences of risk caused by explosives and other agents

Article 348

1. Those who, in the manufacture, handling, transport, possession or marketing of explosives, flammable or corrosive, toxic and asphyxiating substances, or any other materials, devices or contrivances that may cause devastation, contravene the established safety standards, placing in concrete danger the life, physical integrity or health of persons, or the environment, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to three years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of six to twelve years. The same penalties shall be imposed on whoever illegally produces, imports, exports, markets or uses ozone-depleting substances.
2. Those responsible for the surveillance, control and use of explosives that may cause devastation who, in contravention of the regulations on explosives, have facilitated their actual loss or theft shall be punished with prison sentences of six months to three years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for six to twelve years.
3. In the cases set out in the preceding paragraphs, when a legal person is liable for the acts in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis of this Code, it shall be subject to a fine of one to three years, unless, once the damage caused has been proven, its amount is greater, in which case the fine shall be double to quadruple the amount of such damage.
Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, the judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.
The penalties established in the preceding paragraphs shall be imposed in their upper half when they concern the directors, administrators or managers of the company, business, organisation or operation.
4. Those responsible for factories, workshops, means of transport, depots and other establishments relating to explosives that may cause devastation shall be punished with prison sentences of six months to one year, a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of three to six years when they engage in any one or more of the following conducts:
a) Obstructing the inspection activity of the Administration in matters of explosives safety.
b) Falsifying or concealing from the Administration relevant information on compliance with mandatory safety measures relating to explosives.
c) Disobeying express orders of the Administration aimed at remedying serious anomalies detected in matters of explosives safety.

Article 349

Those who, in the handling, transport or possession of organisms, contravene the established standards or safety measures, placing in concrete danger the life, physical integrity or health of persons, or the environment, shall be punished with prison sentences of six months to two years, a fine of six to twelve months, and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade for a period of three to six years.

Article 350

Without prejudice to the provisions of Article 316, those who, in the opening of wells or excavations, in the construction or demolition of buildings, dams, pipelines or similar works or, in their conservation, conditioning or maintenance, infringe the established safety standards whose non-observance may cause catastrophic results, and place in concrete danger the life, physical integrity of persons or the environment, shall incur the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

CHAPTER II

On fires

Section 1 On the offences of fire

Article 351

Those who cause a fire that entails a danger to the life or physical integrity of persons shall be punished with a prison sentence of ten to twenty years. Judges or Courts may impose the penalty one degree lower taking into account the lesser extent of the danger caused and the other circumstances of the act.
When such danger to the life or physical integrity of persons does not exist, the acts shall be punished as damage provided for in Article 266 of this Code.

Section 2 On forest fires

Article 352

Those who set fire to mountains or forest masses shall be punished with prison sentences of one to five years and a fine of twelve to eighteen months.
If there has been danger to the life or physical integrity of persons, the act shall be punished in accordance with the provisions of Article 351, imposing, in any case, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.

Article 353

1.	The acts referred to in the preceding article shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to six years and a fine of eighteen to twenty-four months when the fire reaches special gravity, taking into account the concurrence of any of the following circumstances:
1.	That it affects a surface of considerable importance.
2.	That large or serious erosive effects on the soil result.
3.	That it significantly alters the conditions of animal or plant life, or affects any protected natural area.
4.	That the fire affects areas close to population centres or inhabited places.
5.	That the fire is caused at a time when the climatological or terrain conditions significantly increase the risk of its propagation.
6.	In any case, when serious deterioration or destruction of the affected resources is caused.

2.	The same penalty shall be imposed when the perpetrator acts to obtain an economic benefit from the effects derived from the fire.

Article 354

1.	Whoever sets fire to mountains or forest masses without the fire spreading shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to one year and a fine of six to twelve months.
2.	The conduct provided for in the preceding paragraph shall be exempt from punishment if the fire does not spread due to the voluntary and positive action of its perpetrator.

Article 355

In all cases provided for in this section, Judges or Courts may agree that the classification of the land in the areas affected by a forest fire may not be modified for a period of up to thirty years. They may also agree to limit or suppress the uses that had been carried out in the areas affected by the fire, as well as the administrative intervention of the burnt wood from the fire.

Section 3 On fires in non-forest areas

Article 356

Whoever sets fire to non-forest vegetation areas seriously damaging the natural environment shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to two years and a fine of six to twenty-four months.

Section 4 On fires in own property

Article 357

The arsonist of own property shall be punished with a prison sentence of one to four years if he had the purpose of defrauding or harming third parties, had caused fraud or harm, there existed danger of propagation to a building, woodland or plantation belonging to others, or had seriously harmed the conditions of wildlife, forests or natural areas.

Section 5 Common provisions

Article 358

Whoever through serious negligence causes any of the fire offences punished in the preceding sections shall be punished with the penalty one degree lower than those respectively provided for each case.

Article 358 bis

The provisions of Articles 338 to 340 shall also be applicable to the offences regulated in this Chapter.

CHAPTER III

On offences against public health

Article 359

Whoever, without being duly authorised, manufactures substances harmful to health or chemical products that may cause havoc, or dispenses or supplies them, or trades in them, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to three years and a fine of six to twelve months, and special disqualification for profession or industry for a period of six months to two years.

Article 360

Whoever, being authorised for the trade of the substances or products referred to in the preceding article, dispenses or supplies them without complying with the formalities provided for in the respective Laws and Regulations, shall be punished with a fine of six to twelve months and disqualification for the profession or trade of six months to two years.

Article 361

Whoever manufactures, imports, exports, supplies, intermediates, markets, offers or places on the market, or stores for these purposes, medicines, including those for human and veterinary use, as well as investigational medicines, that lack the necessary authorisation required by law, or medical devices that do not have the conformity documents required by general provisions, or that are deteriorated, expired or fail to meet the technical requirements relating to their composition, stability and efficacy, and thereby generates a risk to the life or health of persons, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to three years, a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification for profession or trade of six months to three years.

Article 361 bis

The distribution or public dissemination through the Internet, telephone or any other information or communication technology of content specifically intended to promote or facilitate, among minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection, the consumption of products, preparations or substances or the use of techniques for ingestion or elimination of food products whose use is likely to generate risk to the health of persons shall be punished with a fine of six to twelve months or a prison sentence of one to three years.
The judicial authorities shall order the adoption of the necessary measures for the removal of the content referred to in the preceding paragraph, for the interruption of services that predominantly offer such content or for the blocking of both when they are located abroad.

Article 362

1.	Anyone who prepares or produces shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from six months to four years, a fine of six to eighteen months and special disqualification from profession or trade from one to three years,
a)	a medicinal product, including those for human and veterinary use, as well as investigational medicinal products; or an active substance or an excipient of such medicinal product;
b)	a medical device, as well as accessories, elements or materials that are essential for its integrity;
in such a way that the following is deceptively presented: its identity, including, where applicable, the packaging and labelling, the expiry date, the name or composition of any of its components, or, where applicable, the dosage thereof; its origin, including the manufacturer, the country of manufacture, the country of origin and the holder of the marketing authorisation or conformity documents; data relating to compliance with legal requirements or demands, licences, conformity documents or authorisations; or its history, including records and documents relating to the distribution channels used, provided that they were intended for public consumption or use by third parties, and generate a risk to the life or health of persons.
2.	The same penalties shall be imposed on anyone who alters, during manufacture or preparation or at a later time, the quantity, dose, expiry date or genuine composition, as authorised or declared, of any of the medicinal products, substances, excipients, medical devices, accessories, elements or materials mentioned in the preceding paragraph, in a manner that reduces their safety, efficacy or quality, generating a risk to the life or health of persons.

Article 362 bis

Anyone who, with knowledge of their falsification or alteration, imports, exports, advertises or publicises, offers, exhibits, sells, facilitates, dispenses, dispatches, packages, supplies, including intermediation, traffics, distributes or places on the market, any of the medicinal products, active substances, excipients, medical devices, accessories, elements or materials referred to in the preceding article, and thereby generates a risk to the life or health of persons, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from six months to four years, a fine of six to eighteen months and special disqualification from profession or trade from one to three years.
The same penalties shall be imposed on anyone who acquires or holds them in storage with the purpose of destining them for public consumption, use by third parties or any other use that may affect public health.

Article 362 ter

Anyone who prepares any false document or document with mendacious content referring to any of the medicinal products, active substances, excipients, medical devices, accessories, elements or materials referred to in paragraph 1 of Article 362, including their packaging, labelling and instructions for use, in order to commit or facilitate the commission of one of the offences in Article 362, shall be punished with the penalty of six months to two years' imprisonment, a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification from profession or trade from six months to two years.

Article 362 quater

Penalties higher in degree than those indicated in Articles 361, 362, 362 bis or 362 ter shall be imposed when the offence is perpetrated with any of the following circumstances:
1.	ª That the offender was an authority, public official, physician, health professional, teacher, educator, physical or sports trainer, and acted in the exercise of their position, profession or trade.
2.	ª That the medicinal products, active substances, excipients, medical devices, accessories, elements or materials referred to in Article 362:
a)	had been offered through large-scale media; or
b)	had been offered or facilitated to minors, persons with disabilities in need of special protection, or persons especially vulnerable in relation to the product facilitated.
3.	ª That the offender belonged to a criminal organisation or group whose purpose was the commission of this type of offence.
4.	ª That the acts were carried out in establishments open to the public by those responsible for them or their employees.

Article 362 quinquies

1.	Those who, without therapeutic justification, prescribe, provide, dispense, supply, administer, offer or facilitate to non-competitive federated athletes, non-federated athletes who practise sport for recreation, or athletes who participate in competitions organised in Spain by sports entities, prohibited substances or pharmacological groups, as well as non-regulatory methods, intended to increase their physical capacities or to modify the results of competitions, which by their content, repeated intake or other concurrent circumstances, endanger their life or health, shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment from six months to two years, a fine of six to eighteen months and special disqualification from public employment or position, profession or trade, from two to five years.
2.	The penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph in their upper half shall be imposed when the offence is perpetrated with any of the following circumstances:
1.	ª That the victim is a minor.
2.	ª That deception or intimidation has been used.

3.	ª That the person responsible has taken advantage of a relationship of employment or professional superiority.

Article 362 sexies

In the offences provided for in the preceding articles of this Chapter, the substances and products referred to in Articles 359 and following shall be subject to confiscation, as well as the assets, means, instruments and profits in accordance with the provisions of Articles 127 to 128.

Article 363

Producers, distributors or traders who endanger the health of consumers shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from one to four years, a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification from profession, trade, industry or commerce for a period of three to six years:
1.	Offering on the market food products with omission or alteration of the requirements established in laws or regulations on expiry date or composition.
2.	Manufacturing or selling beverages or foodstuffs intended for public consumption and harmful to health.
3.	Trafficking in spoiled goods.
4.	Producing products whose use is not authorised and is harmful to health, or trading in them.
5.	Concealing or removing items intended to be rendered unusable or disinfected, in order to trade in them.

Article 364

1.	Anyone who adulterates with additives or other unauthorised agents capable of causing harm to the health of persons the foodstuffs, substances or beverages intended for the food trade, shall be punished with the penalties of the preceding article. If the offender were the owner or the person responsible for production of a food products factory, the penalty of special disqualification from profession, trade, industry or commerce from six to ten years shall also be imposed.
2.	The same penalty shall be imposed on anyone who carries out any of the following conducts:
1.	º Administering to animals whose meat or products are intended for human consumption substances not permitted that generate risk to the health of persons, or in higher doses or for purposes other than those authorised.
2.	º Slaughtering animals for consumption or destining their products for human consumption, knowing that the substances mentioned in the preceding number have been administered to them.
3.	º Slaughtering animals for consumption to which therapeutic treatments have been applied by means of substances referred to in paragraph 1.º
4.	º Dispatching for public consumption the meat or products of animals for consumption without respecting the withdrawal periods provided for by regulation where applicable.

Article 365

Any person who poisons or adulterates drinking water or foodstuffs intended for public use or consumption by a community of persons with infectious substances, or other substances that may be seriously harmful to health, shall be punished with a term of imprisonment of two to six years.

Article 366

When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is liable for the offences set out in the preceding articles of this Chapter, it shall be imposed a fine of one to three years, or double to five times the value of the substances and products referred to in Articles 359 et seq., or the benefit that has been or could have been obtained, whichever amount is higher.

Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in paragraphs b) to g) of section 7 of Article 33.

Article 367

If the acts provided for in all the preceding articles were committed through gross negligence, the penalties lower in degree shall be imposed, respectively.

Article 368

Those who carry out acts of cultivation, production or trafficking, or otherwise promote, favour or facilitate the illegal consumption of toxic drugs, narcotics or psychotropic substances, or possess them for such purposes, shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of one to three times the value of the drug that is the object of the offence if the substances or products in question cause serious harm to health, and imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of one to two times in other cases.
Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraph, the courts may impose the penalty lower in degree than those indicated having regard to the minor nature of the act and the personal circumstances of the offender. This power may not be exercised if any of the circumstances referred to in Articles 369 bis and 370 are present.

Article 369

1.	The penalties higher in degree than those indicated in the preceding article and a fine of one to four times shall be imposed when any of the following circumstances are present:
1.	ª The offender is an authority, public official, medical practitioner, social worker, teacher or educator and acts in the exercise of his office, profession or occupation.
2.	ª The offender participates in other organised activities or whose execution is facilitated by the commission of the offence.
3.	ª The acts are carried out in establishments open to the public by those responsible for or employees of such establishments.
4.	ª The substances referred to in the preceding article are supplied to persons under 18 years of age, to persons with mental disabilities or to persons undergoing treatment for withdrawal or rehabilitation.
5.	ª The quantity of the said substances that are the object of the conduct referred to in the preceding article is of notorious importance.
6.	ª The said substances are adulterated, manipulated or mixed with each other or with others, increasing the possible harm to health.
7.	ª The conduct described in the preceding article takes place in educational centres, in military centres, establishments or units, in penitentiary establishments or in withdrawal or rehabilitation centres, or in their vicinity.
8.	ª The offender uses violence or displays or makes use of weapons to commit the act.

Article 369 bis

When the acts described in Article 368 have been carried out by persons belonging to a criminal organisation, the penalties of imprisonment of nine to twelve years and a fine of one to four times the value of the drug shall be imposed if the substances and products in question cause serious harm to health, and imprisonment of four years and six months to ten years and the same fine in other cases.
The leaders, managers or administrators of the organisation shall be imposed the penalties higher in degree than those indicated in the first paragraph.
When in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is liable for the offences set out in the two preceding articles, it shall be imposed the following penalties:

a)	A fine of two to five years, or three to five times the value of the drug when the resulting amount is higher, if the offence committed by the natural person carries a penalty of imprisonment of more than five years.
b)	A fine of one to three years, or double to four times the value of the drug when the resulting amount is higher, if the offence committed by the natural person carries a penalty of imprisonment of more than two years not included in the preceding subparagraph.
Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in paragraphs b) to g) of section 7 of Article 33.

Article 370

The penalty higher by one or two degrees than that indicated in Article 368 shall be imposed when:
1.	º Persons under 18 years of age or persons with mental disabilities are used to commit these offences.
2.	º It concerns the leaders, administrators or managers of the organisations referred to in circumstance 2.ª of section 1 of Article 369.
3.	º The conduct described in Article 368 is of extreme gravity.
Cases in which the quantity of the substances referred to in Article 368 significantly exceeds that considered to be of notorious importance, or ships, vessels or aircraft have been used as a specific means of transport, or the conduct indicated has been carried out by simulating international trade operations between companies, or it concerns international networks dedicated to this type of activity, or when three or more of the circumstances provided for in Article 369.1 are present, shall be considered to be of extreme gravity.
In the cases referred to in the preceding numbers 2.º and 3.º, the offenders shall also be imposed a fine of one to three times the value of the drug that is the object of the offence.

Article 371

1.	Any person who manufactures, transports, distributes, trades in or has in his possession equipment, materials or substances listed in Table I and Table II of the United Nations Convention, done in Vienna on 20 December 1988, against Illicit Traffic in Narcotic Drugs and Psychotropic Substances, and any other products added to the same Convention or included in other future conventions of the same nature, ratified by Spain, knowing that they are going to be used in the illicit cultivation, production or manufacture of toxic drugs, narcotics or psychotropic substances, or for these purposes, shall be punished with a term of imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of one to three times the value of the goods or effects.
2.	The penalty indicated in its upper half shall be imposed when the persons who carry out the acts described in the preceding section belong to an organisation dedicated to the purposes indicated therein, and the penalty higher in degree when it concerns the leaders, administrators or managers of the said organisations or associations.
In such cases, judges or courts shall impose, in addition to the corresponding penalties, that of special disqualification of the offender from the exercise of his profession or industry for a period of three to six years, and the other measures provided for in Article 369.2.

Article 372

If the acts provided for in this chapter were committed by a business person, intermediary in the financial sector, medical practitioner, public official, social worker, teacher or educator, in the exercise of their position, profession or trade, they shall be subject, in addition to the corresponding penalty, to special disqualification from employment or public office, profession or trade, industry or commerce, for three to ten years. The penalty of absolute disqualification for ten to twenty years shall be imposed when the aforementioned acts were committed by an authority or agent thereof, in the exercise of their position.
For this purpose, medical practitioners are understood to be doctors, psychologists, persons holding a health qualification, veterinarians, pharmacists and their employees.

Article 373

Incitement, conspiracy and proposal to commit the offences provided for in articles 368 to 372 shall be punished with a penalty one to two degrees lower than that corresponding, respectively, to the acts provided for in the preceding provisions.

Article 374

In the offences provided for in the second paragraph of section 1 of article 301 and in articles 368 to 372, in addition to the penalties to be imposed for the offence committed, toxic drugs, narcotics or psychotropic substances, the equipment, materials and substances referred to in article 371, as well as the property, means, instruments and proceeds shall be subject to confiscation in accordance with the provisions of articles 127 to 128 and the following special rules:
1.	ª Once the judgment is final, the samples that have been set aside shall be destroyed, or the totality of what has been seized shall be destroyed, in the event that the competent judicial body has ordered its preservation.
2.	ª The property, means, instruments and proceeds definitively confiscated by judgment, which may not be applied to the satisfaction of civil liabilities arising from the offence or procedural costs, shall be awarded in full to the State.

Article 375

Convictions by foreign judges or courts for offences of the same nature as those provided for in articles 361 to 372 of this Chapter shall produce the effects of recidivism, unless the criminal record has been cancelled or can be cancelled in accordance with Spanish law.

Article 376

In the cases provided for in articles 361 to 372, judges or courts, giving reasons in the judgment, may impose a penalty one or two degrees lower than that prescribed by law for the offence in question, provided that the subject has voluntarily abandoned their criminal activities and has actively cooperated with the authorities or their agents either to prevent the commission of the offence, or to obtain decisive evidence for the identification or capture of other persons responsible or to prevent the operation or development of the organisations or associations to which they have belonged or with which they have cooperated.
Likewise, in the cases provided for in articles 368 to 372, judges or courts may impose a penalty one or two degrees lower on the offender who, being drug-dependent at the time of commission of the acts, sufficiently proves that they have successfully completed a rehabilitation treatment, provided that the quantity of toxic drugs, narcotics or psychotropic substances was not of notorious importance or extreme gravity.

Article 377

For the determination of the amount of fines to be imposed in application of articles 368 to 372, the value of the drug that is the object of the offence or of the goods or effects seized shall be the final price of the product or, as the case may be, the reward or gain obtained by the offender, or that they could have obtained.

Article 378

Payments made by the convicted person for one or more of the offences referred to in articles 361 to 372 shall be allocated in the following order:
1.	º To the reparation of the damage caused and compensation for harm.
2.	º To compensation to the State for the amount of expenses incurred on its account in the proceedings.
3.	º To the fine.

4.	º To the costs of the private or particular prosecutor when their payment is imposed in the judgment.
5.	º To other procedural costs, including those of the defence of the accused, without preference among the interested parties.

CHAPTER IV

Offences against Road Safety

Article 379

1.	Anyone who drives a motor vehicle or a moped at a speed exceeding by sixty kilometres per hour in an urban road or by eighty kilometres per hour on an interurban road the speed permitted by regulation, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment for three to six months or a fine of six to twelve months or community service for thirty-one to ninety days, and, in any case, with deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for a period exceeding one year and up to four years.
2.	Anyone who drives a motor vehicle or moped under the influence of toxic drugs, narcotics, psychotropic substances or alcoholic beverages shall be punished with the same penalties. In any case, anyone who drives with a breath alcohol level exceeding 0.60 milligrams per litre or with a blood alcohol level exceeding 1.2 grams per litre shall be convicted with said penalties.

Article 380

1.	Anyone who drives a motor vehicle or a moped with manifest recklessness and places the life or physical integrity of persons in concrete danger shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment for six months to two years and deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for a period exceeding one year and up to six years.
2.	For the purposes of this provision, driving shall be deemed manifestly reckless when the circumstances provided for in the first paragraph and in the second part of the second paragraph of the preceding article are present.

Article 381

1.	Anyone who, with manifest disregard for the lives of others, commits the conduct described in the preceding article shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment for two to five years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for a period of six to ten years.
2.	When the life or physical integrity of persons has not been placed in concrete danger, the penalties shall be imprisonment for one to two years, a fine of six to twelve months and deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for the period provided for in the preceding paragraph.

Article 382

When the acts punished in articles 379, 380 and 381 cause, in addition to the anticipated risk, a harmful result constituting an offence, whatever its gravity, the Judges or Courts shall consider only the more seriously punished infringement, applying the penalty in its upper half and ordering, in any case, compensation for the civil liability that has arisen.
When the harmful result occurs together with an offence under article 381, the penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds provided for in this provision in its upper half shall be imposed in any case.

Article 382 bis

1. The driver of a motor vehicle or moped who, outside the cases contemplated in Article 195, voluntarily and without risk to himself or third parties, abandons the scene of the events after causing an accident in which one

or more persons died or in which they were caused any of the injuries referred to in Articles 147.1, 149 and 150, shall be punished as the perpetrator of an offence of abandoning the scene of the accident.
2. The events contemplated in this article that had their origin in a negligent action by the driver shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to four years and deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from one to four years.
3. If the origin of the events giving rise to the abandonment was fortuitous, a sentence of three to six months' imprisonment and deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from six months to two years shall apply.

Article 383

The driver who, when required by an agent of the authority, refuses to submit to the legally established tests for verification of blood alcohol levels and the presence of toxic drugs, narcotics and psychotropic substances referred to in the preceding articles, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to one year and deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for a period exceeding one and up to four years.

Article 384

Whoever drives a motor vehicle or moped in cases of loss of validity of the permit or licence due to total loss of the legally assigned points shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to six months or a fine of twelve to twenty-four months or community service of thirty-one to ninety days.
The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever drives after having been deprived provisionally or definitively of the permit or licence by judicial decision and on whoever drives a motor vehicle or moped without ever having obtained a driving permit or licence.

Article 385

Whoever creates a serious risk to traffic in any of the following ways shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to two years or fines of twelve to twenty-four months and community service of ten to forty days:
1. ª By placing unforeseeable obstacles on the road, spilling slippery or flammable substances or changing, removing or cancelling signage or by any other means.
2. ª By not restoring the safety of the road when there is an obligation to do so.

Article 385 bis

The motor vehicle or moped used in the acts provided for in this Chapter shall be considered an instrument of the offence for the purposes of Articles 127 and 128.

Article 385 ter

In the offences provided for in Articles 379, 383, 384 and 385, the Judge or Court, giving reasons in the judgment, may reduce the prison sentence by one degree in view of the lesser extent of the risk caused and the other circumstances of the act.

TITLE XVIII

Of falsifications

CHAPTER I

Of the counterfeiting of currency and stamped effects

Article 386

1. The following shall be punished with a prison sentence of eight to twelve years and a fine of the equivalent to ten times the apparent value of the currency:
1. º Whoever alters currency or manufactures counterfeit currency.
2. º Whoever exports counterfeit or altered currency or imports it into Spain or any other Member State of the European Union.
3. º Whoever transports, distributes or circulates counterfeit or altered currency with knowledge of its falsity.
2. If the counterfeit currency is put into circulation, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half.
The possession, receipt or obtaining of counterfeit currency for its distribution or circulation shall be punished with a penalty lower by one or two degrees, taking into account the value thereof and the degree of complicity with the counterfeiter, alterer, importer or exporter.
3. Whoever, having received counterfeit currency in good faith, distributes or circulates it after becoming aware of its falsity shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to six months or a fine of six to twenty-four months. However, if the apparent value of the currency does not exceed 400 euros, a fine of one to three months shall be imposed.
4. If the guilty party belongs to a society, organisation or association, even of a temporary nature, engaged in these activities, the judge or court may impose one or more of the consequences provided for in Article 129 of this Code.
5. When, in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis, a legal person is responsible for the aforementioned offences, it shall be imposed a fine of three to ten times the apparent value of the currency. Taking into account the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.

Article 387

For the purposes of the preceding article, currency means metallic currency and paper money of legal tender and that which has not yet been issued or officially put into circulation but which is intended for circulation as legal tender currency. The currencies of other countries of the European Union and foreign currencies shall be treated as equivalent to national currency.
