Currency shall also be considered counterfeit when, despite being produced in legal facilities and with legal materials, it is produced knowingly in breach of the conditions of issue imposed by the competent authority or when it is issued without any order of issue.

Article 388

The conviction of a foreign Court, imposed for an offence of the same nature as those included in this chapter, shall be treated as equivalent to the judgments of Spanish Judges or Courts for the purposes of recidivism, unless the criminal record has been cancelled or could be cancelled under Spanish law.

Article 389

Whoever counterfeits or distributes, in complicity with the counterfeiter, postage stamps or stamped effects, or introduces them into Spain knowing of their falsity, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to three years.

The purchaser in good faith of postage stamps or stamped effects who, knowing of their falsity, distributes or uses them shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to six months or a fine of six to twenty-four months. However, if the apparent value of the stamps or stamped effects does not exceed 400 euros, a fine of one to three months shall be imposed.

CHAPTER II

Of documentary falsifications

Section 1.ª

Of the falsification of public, official and commercial documents and of dispatches transmitted by telecommunication services

Article 390

1. The authority or public official who, in the exercise of his functions, commits falsification shall be punished with prison sentences of three to six years, a fine of six to twenty-four months and special disqualification for a period of two to six years:
1. º By altering a document in any of its essential elements or requirements.
2. º By simulating a document in whole or in part, in such a way as to mislead as to its authenticity.
3. º By assuming in an act the participation of persons who have not participated, or attributing to those who have participated in it declarations or statements different from those they would have made.
4. º By failing to tell the truth in the narration of the facts.
2. The person responsible for any religious confession who engages in any of the conducts described in the preceding numbers, with respect to acts and documents that may have effect on the status of persons or in the civil order, shall be punished with the same penalties as those indicated in the preceding paragraph.

Artículo 391

The authority or public official who through gross negligence incurs in any of the falsifications provided for in the preceding article or gives rise to another committing them, shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of six to twelve months and suspension from employment or public office for a period of six months to one year.

Artículo 392

1.	The private individual who commits in a public, official or commercial document, any of the falsifications described in the first three numbers of paragraph 1 of article 390, shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment from six months to three years and a fine of six to twelve months.
2.	The same penalties shall be imposed on whoever, without having intervened in the falsification, traffics in any way with a false identity document. The penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year and a fine of three to six months shall be imposed on whoever knowingly makes use of a false identity document.
This provision is applicable even when the false identity document appears as belonging to another State of the European Union or to a third State or has been falsified or acquired in another State of the European Union or in a third State if it is used or trafficked with in Spain.

Artículo 393

Whoever, knowing of its falsity, presents in court or, to harm another, makes use of a false document of those included in the preceding articles, shall be punished with the penalty one degree lower than that assigned to the falsifiers.

Artículo 394

1.	The authority or public official in charge of telecommunication services who fabricates or falsifies a telegraphic dispatch or another proper to said services, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment from six months to three years and special disqualification for a period of two to six years.
2.	Whoever, knowing of its falsity, makes use of the false dispatch to harm another, shall be punished with the penalty one degree lower than that assigned to the falsifiers.

Sección 2.ª De la falsificación de documentos privados

Artículo 395

Whoever, to harm another, commits in a private document any of the falsifications provided for in the first three numbers of paragraph 1 of article 390, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years.

Artículo 396

Whoever, knowing of its falsity, presents in court or, to harm another, makes use of a false document of those included in the preceding article, shall incur the penalty one degree lower than that assigned to the falsifiers.

Sección 3.ª De la falsificación de certificados

Artículo 397

The professional who issues a false certificate shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of three to twelve months.

Artículo 398

The authority or public official who issues a false certification with little significance in legal transactions shall be punished with the penalty of suspension from six months to two years.
This provision shall not be applicable to certificates relating to Social Security and the Public Treasury.

Artículo 399

1.	The private individual who falsifies a certification of those designated in the preceding articles shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of three to six months.
2.	The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever knowingly makes use of the certification, as well as on whoever, without having intervened in its falsification, traffics with it in any way.
3.	This provision is applicable even when the certificate appears as belonging to another State of the European Union or to a third State or has been falsified or acquired in another State of the European Union or in a third State if it is used in Spain.

Sección 4.ª

De la falsificación de tarjetas de crédito y débito, cheques de viaje y demás instrumentos de pago distintos del efectivo.

Artículo 399 bis

1.	Whoever alters, copies, reproduces or in any other way falsifies credit or debit cards, traveller's cheques or any other payment instrument other than cash, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from four to eight years.
The penalty shall be imposed in its upper half when the falsified instruments affect a generality of persons or when the acts are committed within the framework of a criminal organization dedicated to these activities.
When in accordance with the provisions of article 31 bis a legal person is responsible for the preceding offences, it shall be imposed the penalty of a fine of two to five years.

In accordance with the rules established in article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of article 33.
2.	The possession of credit or debit cards, traveller's cheques or any other falsified payment instruments other than cash, intended for distribution or trafficking shall be punished with the penalty assigned to falsification.
3.	Whoever without having intervened in the falsification uses, to the detriment of another and knowing of the falsity, falsified credit or debit cards, traveller's cheques or any other payment instruments other than cash, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from two to five years.
4.	Whoever, for fraudulent use and knowing of their falsity, possesses or obtains, for himself or for a third party, credit or debit cards, traveller's cheques or any other payment instrument other than cash shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from one to two years.

Artículo 399 ter

For the purposes of this Code, payment instrument other than cash means any device, object or protected record, material or immaterial, or a combination of these, except legal tender currency, which, by itself or in combination with a procedure or set of procedures, allows the holder or user to transfer money or monetary value including through digital means of exchange.

CAPÍTULO III

Disposiciones generales

Artículo 400

The manufacture, reception, obtaining, possession, distribution, making available or commercialization of tools, materials, instruments, substances, data and computer programs, devices, security elements or any other means specifically designed or adapted for the commission of the offences described in the preceding chapters, shall be punished with the penalty assigned in each case to the perpetrators.

Artículo 400 bis

In the cases described in articles 392, 393, 394, 396 and 399 of this Code, use of false documents, dispatches, certifications or identity documents shall also be understood as the use of the corresponding authentic documents, dispatches, certifications or identity documents carried out by someone who is not authorized to do so.

CAPÍTULO IV

De la usurpación del estado civil

Artículo 401

Whoever usurps the civil status of another shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to three years.

CAPÍTULO V

De la usurpación de funciones públicas y del intrusismo

Artículo 402

Whoever illegitimately exercises acts proper to an authority or public official attributing official character to himself, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from one to three years.

Artículo 402 bis

Whoever without being authorized publicly and improperly uses a uniform, suit or insignia that attributes official character to him shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of one to three months.

Article 403

1.	Whoever performs acts proper to a profession without possessing the corresponding academic degree issued or recognised in Spain in accordance with current legislation, shall incur a fine of twelve to twenty-four months. If the professional activity carried out requires an official degree that accredits the necessary qualification and legally enables its practice, and one is not in possession of said degree, a fine of six to twelve months shall be imposed.
2.	A penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years shall be imposed if any of the following circumstances are present:
a)	If the offender, in addition, publicly attributes to himself the status of a professional protected by the aforementioned degree.
b)	If the offender performs the acts referred to in the preceding paragraph in premises or an establishment open to the public in which the provision of services proper to that profession is advertised.

TITLE XIX

Offences against the Public Administration

CHAPTER I

On the malfeasance of public officials and other unjust conduct

Article 404

An authority or public official who, knowingly of its injustice, issues an arbitrary decision in an administrative matter shall be punished with the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of nine to fifteen years.

Article 405

An authority or public official who, in the exercise of his competence and knowingly of its illegality, proposes, appoints or gives possession for the exercise of a particular public office to any person without the legally established requirements being met, shall be punished with the penalties of a fine of three to eight months and suspension from public employment or office for a period of one to three years.

Article 406

The same fine shall be imposed on the person who accepts the proposal, appointment or taking of possession mentioned in the preceding article, knowing that he lacks the legally required qualifications.

CHAPTER II

On the abandonment of post and the omission of the duty to prosecute offences

Article 407

1.	An authority or public official who abandons his post with the purpose of not preventing or not prosecuting any of the offences included in Titles XXI, XXII, XXIII and XXIV shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from one to four years and absolute disqualification from public employment or office for a period of six to ten years. If he has carried out the

abandonment in order not to prevent or not prosecute any other offence, the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of one to three years shall be imposed.
2.	The same penalties shall be imposed, respectively, when the abandonment has the purpose of not executing the penalties corresponding to these offences imposed by the competent judicial authority.

Article 408

An authority or official who, failing in the obligation of his office, intentionally omits to promote the prosecution of offences of which he has knowledge or of those responsible for them, shall incur the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of six months to two years.

Article 409

Authorities or public officials who promote, direct or organise the collective and manifestly unlawful abandonment of a public service shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of eight to twelve months and suspension from public employment or office for a period of six months to two years.
Authorities or public officials who merely take part in the collective or manifestly unlawful abandonment of an essential public service and with serious harm to it or to the community, shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of eight to twelve months.

CHAPTER III

On disobedience and denial of assistance

Article 410

1.	Authorities or public officials who openly refuse to give due compliance to judicial decisions, decisions or orders of a superior authority, issued within the scope of their respective competence and clothed with legal formalities, shall incur the penalty of a fine of three to twelve months and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of six months to two years.
2.	Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraph, authorities or officials shall not incur criminal liability for not complying with an order that constitutes a manifest, clear and categorical infringement of a provision of Law or of any other general regulation.

Article 411

An authority or public official who, having suspended, for any reason other than that expressed in the second paragraph of the preceding article, the execution of the orders of his superiors, disobeys them after the latter have disapproved the suspension, shall incur the penalties of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months, and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of one to three years.

Article 412

1.	A public official who, when required by a competent authority, does not provide the due assistance for the Administration of Justice or another public service, shall incur the penalties of a fine of three to twelve months, and suspension from public employment or office for a period of six months to two years.
2.	If the person required is an authority, chief or person in charge of a public force or an agent of the authority, the penalties of a fine of twelve to eighteen months and suspension from public employment or office for a period of two to three years shall be imposed.
3.	An authority or public official who, when required by a private individual to provide some assistance to which he is obliged by reason of his office to prevent an offence against the life of persons, refrains from providing it, shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of eighteen to twenty-four months and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of three to six years.

If it concerns an offence against the integrity, sexual freedom, health or freedom of persons, he shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of twelve to eighteen months and suspension from public employment or office of one to three years.
In the event that such requirement was to prevent any other offence or other harm, he shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of three to twelve months and suspension from public employment or office for a period of six months to two years.

CHAPTER IV

On infidelity in the custody of documents and the violation of secrets

Article 413

An authority or public official who, knowingly, removes, destroys, renders useless or conceals, totally or partially, documents whose custody is entrusted to him by reason of his office, shall incur the penalties of imprisonment from one to four years, a fine of seven to twenty-four months, and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of three to six years.

Article 414

1.	An authority or public official who, by reason of his office, is entrusted with the custody of documents in respect of which the competent authority has restricted access, and who knowingly destroys or renders useless the means put in place to prevent such access or consents to their destruction or rendering useless, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year or a fine of six to twenty-four months and, in any case, special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of one to three years.
2.	A private individual who destroys or renders useless the means referred to in the preceding paragraph, shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of six to eighteen months.

Article 415

An authority or public official not covered by the preceding article who, knowingly and without due authorisation, accesses or permits access to secret documents whose custody is entrusted to him by reason of his office, shall incur the penalty of a fine of six to twelve months, and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of one to three years.

Article 416

Private individuals temporarily entrusted with the dispatch or custody of documents, by commission of the Government or of the public authorities or officials to whom they have been entrusted by reason of their office, who engage in the conduct described therein, shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment or fine immediately lower than those respectively indicated in the three preceding articles.

Article 417

1.	The public authority or official who reveals secrets or information of which he has knowledge by reason of his office or position and which should not be disclosed, shall incur the penalty of a fine of twelve to eighteen months and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of one to three years.
If serious harm to the public interest or to a third party results from the revelation referred to in the preceding paragraph, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to three years, and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of three to five years.
2.	If the secrets of a private individual are involved, the penalties shall be imprisonment of two to four years, a fine of twelve to eighteen months, and suspension from public employment or office for a period of one to three years.

Article 418

The private individual who takes advantage for himself or for a third party of the secret or privileged information obtained from a public official or authority, shall be punished with a fine of one to three times the benefit obtained or facilitated and the loss of the possibility of obtaining public subsidies or aid and of the right to enjoy tax or Social Security benefits or incentives for a period of one to three years. If serious harm results to the public interest or to a third party, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to six years and the loss of the possibility of obtaining public subsidies or aid and of the right to enjoy tax or Social Security benefits or incentives for a period of six to ten years.

CHAPTER V

On bribery

Article 419

The public authority or official who, for his own benefit or that of a third party, receives or solicits, directly or through an intermediary, a gift, favour or remuneration of any kind or accepts an offer or promise to perform in the exercise of his office an act contrary to the duties inherent thereto or not to perform or unjustifiably delay that which he should perform, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment of three to six years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months, and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of nine to twelve years, without prejudice to the penalty corresponding to the act performed, omitted or delayed by reason of the remuneration or promise, if it constitutes an offence.

Article 420

The public authority or official who, for his own benefit or that of a third party, receives or solicits, directly or through an intermediary, a gift, favour or remuneration of any kind or accepts an offer or promise to perform an act proper to his office, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment of two to four years, a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of five to nine years.

Article 421

The penalties indicated in the preceding articles shall also be imposed when the gift, favour or remuneration is received or solicited by the public authority or official, in their respective cases, as a reward for the conduct described in said articles.

Article 422

The public authority or official who, for his own benefit or that of a third party, admits, directly or through an intermediary, a gift or present offered to him in consideration of his office or function, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment of six months to one year and suspension from public employment and office for one to three years.

Article 423

The provisions of the preceding articles shall be equally applicable to jurors and arbitrators, national or international, as well as to mediators, experts, judicially appointed administrators or receivers, insolvency administrators or any persons who participate in the exercise of public functions.

Article 424

1.	The private individual who offers or delivers a gift or remuneration of any other kind to a public authority, official or person who participates in the exercise of public functions to perform an act contrary to the duties inherent to his office or an act proper to his office, not to perform or delay that which he should perform, or in consideration of his office or function, shall be punished in their respective cases, with the same penalties of imprisonment and fine as the corrupted authority, official or person.
2.	When a private individual delivers the gift or remuneration in response to the request of the public authority, official or person who participates in the exercise of public functions, the same penalties of imprisonment and fine as correspond to them shall be imposed.
3.	If the action obtained or sought from the authority or official is related to a procurement, subsidy or auction procedure called by the Administrations or public entities, the private individual and, where applicable, the company, association or organisation he represents shall be subject to the penalty of disqualification from obtaining public subsidies and aid, from contracting with entities, bodies or entities that form part of the public sector and from enjoying tax and Social Security benefits or incentives for a period of five to ten years.

Article 425

When bribery occurs in a criminal case in favour of the accused by his spouse or another person to whom he is bound in a stable manner by an analogous relationship of affection, or by an ascendant, descendant or sibling by nature, by adoption or related in the same degrees, the briber shall be subject to the penalty of imprisonment of six months to one year.

Article 426

The private individual who, having occasionally acceded to the request for a gift or other remuneration made by a public authority or official, reports the fact to the authority having the duty to investigate it before the opening of proceedings, provided that no more than two months have elapsed since the date of the facts, shall be exempt from penalty for the offence of bribery.

Article 427

The provisions of the preceding articles shall also be applicable when the conduct described is performed by or affects:
a)	Any person holding a legislative, administrative or judicial office or position in a country of the European Union or any other foreign country, whether by appointment or election.
b)	Any person exercising a public function for a country of the European Union or any other foreign country, including a public body or public enterprise, for the European Union or for another public international organisation.
c)	Any official or agent of the European Union or of a public international organisation.
d)	Any person to whom a public service function has been assigned and who is exercising it, consisting of the management, in Member States or in third countries, of financial interests of the European Union or of making decisions on those interests.

Article 427 bis

Where in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is liable for the offences set out in this Chapter, the following penalties shall be imposed:
a)	A fine of two to five years, or triple to quintuple the benefit obtained where the resulting amount is higher, if the offence committed by the natural person carries a penalty of imprisonment of more than five years.
b)	A fine of one to three years, or double to quadruple the benefit obtained where the resulting amount is higher, if the offence committed by the natural person carries a penalty of more than two years' deprivation of liberty not included in the preceding paragraph.
c)	A fine of six months to two years, or double to triple the benefit obtained if the resulting amount is higher, in all other cases.

Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.

CHAPTER VI

On trafficking in influence

Article 428

A public official or authority who influences another public official or authority by taking advantage of the exercise of the powers of his office or any other situation arising from his personal or hierarchical relationship with the latter or with another official or authority in order to obtain a decision that may generate directly or indirectly an economic benefit for himself or for a third party, shall incur the penalties of imprisonment from six months to two years, a fine of the equivalent to double the benefit sought or obtained and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of five to nine years. If he obtains the benefit sought, these penalties shall be imposed in their upper half.

Article 429

A private individual who influences a public official or authority by taking advantage of any situation arising from his personal relationship with the latter or with another public official or authority in order to obtain a decision that may generate directly or indirectly an economic benefit for himself or for a third party, shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment from six months to two years, a fine of the equivalent to double the benefit sought or obtained, and prohibition from contracting with the public sector, as well as the loss of the possibility of obtaining subsidies or public aid and of the right to enjoy tax and Social Security benefits or incentives for a period of six to ten years. If he obtains the benefit sought, these penalties shall be imposed in their upper half.

Article 430

Those who, offering to carry out the conduct described in the two preceding articles, solicit from third parties gifts, presents or any other remuneration, or accept an offer or promise, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year. If the offence is committed by an authority or public official, he shall also be subject to the penalty of special disqualification from public office or employment and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of one to four years.
Where in accordance with the provisions of Article 31 bis a legal person is liable for the offences set out in this Chapter, it shall be subject to the penalty of a fine of six months to two years.
Having regard to the rules established in Article 66 bis, judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33.

Article 431

For the purposes of this chapter, public officials shall be understood to be those determined by Articles 24 and 427.

CHAPTER VII

On embezzlement

Article 432

1.	An authority or public official who, with intent to profit, misappropriates or consents to a third party, with the same intent, misappropriating public assets in his charge by reason of his functions or on the occasion thereof, shall be punished with a penalty of

imprisonment from two to six years, special disqualification from public office or employment and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of six to ten years.
2.	The penalties of imprisonment from four to eight years and absolute disqualification for a period of ten to twenty years shall be imposed if any of the following circumstances were present in the facts referred to in the preceding paragraph:
a)	serious damage or hindrance to the public service had been caused,
b)	the value of the damage caused or of the public assets misappropriated exceeded 50,000 euros,
c)	the embezzled items were of artistic, historical, cultural or scientific value; or if they were effects intended to alleviate a public calamity.
If the value of the damage caused or of the public assets misappropriated exceeded 250,000 euros, the penalty of imprisonment shall be imposed in its upper half, and may reach the higher degree.
3.	The facts referred to in this article shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from one to two years and a fine of three months and one day to twelve months, and in all cases special disqualification from public office or employment and the right of passive suffrage for a period of one to five years, where the damage caused or the value of the public assets is less than 4,000 euros.

Article 432 bis

An authority or public official who, without intent to misappropriate it, devotes to private uses the public assets placed in his charge by reason of his functions or on the occasion thereof, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment from six months to three years, and suspension from public employment or office from one to four years.
If the guilty party does not return the same elements of the public assets diverted within ten days following the initiation of proceedings, the penalties of the preceding article shall be imposed.

Article 433

An authority or public official who, not being covered by the preceding articles, gives to the public assets he administers a public application different from that for which they were intended, shall incur the penalties of imprisonment from one to four years and special disqualification from public employment or office from two to six years, if serious damage or hindrance to the service for which they were allocated results, and disqualification from public employment or office from one to three years and a fine of three to twelve months, if it does not result.

Article 433 bis

1.	An authority or public official who, in a manner capable of causing economic damage to the public entity on which he depends, and outside the cases provided for in Article 390, falsifies its accounts, the documents that must reflect its economic situation or the information contained therein, shall be punished with the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of one to ten years and a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.
2.	The same penalties shall be imposed on an authority or public official who, in a manner capable of causing economic damage to the public entity on which he depends, provides third parties with false information relating to its economic situation or any of the documents or information referred to in the preceding paragraph.
3.	If economic damage to the entity is actually caused, the penalties of imprisonment from one to four years, special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of three to ten years and a fine of twelve to twenty-four months shall be imposed.

Article 433 ter

For the purposes of this Code, public assets shall be understood to mean the entire set of assets and rights, of economic-patrimonial content, belonging to public administrations.

Article 434

If the perpetrator of any of the acts classified in this chapter had effectively and fully repaired the damage caused to public assets before the start of the oral trial, or had actively and effectively collaborated with the authorities or their agents to obtain decisive evidence for the identification or capture of other responsible parties or for the complete clarification of the criminal acts, the judges and courts shall impose on the person responsible for this crime a penalty reduced by one or two degrees.

Article 435

The provisions of this chapter are extended to:
1.	º Those who are entrusted by any means with funds, revenues or assets of Public Administrations.
2.	º Private individuals legally designated as depositaries of public funds or assets.
3.	º Administrators or depositaries of money or property seized, sequestered or deposited by public authority, even if they belong to private individuals.
4.	º Insolvency administrators, in relation to the insolvency estate or the economic interests of creditors. In particular, the interests of creditors shall be considered affected when the order of payment of credits established by law is fraudulently altered.
5.	º Legal persons that in accordance with the provisions of article 31 bis are responsible for the crimes set out in this Chapter. In these cases the following penalties shall be imposed:
a)	Fine of two to five years, or triple to quintuple the value of the damage caused or of the property or assets misappropriated when the resulting amount is higher, if the crime committed by the natural person carries a penalty of imprisonment of more than five years.
b)	Fine of one to three years, or double to quadruple the value of the damage caused or of the property or assets misappropriated when the resulting amount is higher, if the crime committed by the natural person carries a penalty of more than two years of deprivation of liberty not included in the preceding subparagraph.
c)	Fine of six months to two years, or double to triple the value of the damage caused or of the property or assets misappropriated if the resulting amount is higher, in all other cases.
Having regard to the rules established in article 66 bis, the judges and courts may also impose the penalties set out in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of article 33.

Article 435 bis

For the purposes of this chapter, public official shall mean those determined by articles 24 and 427.

CHAPTER VIII

Fraud and illegal exactions

Article 436

The authority or public official who, intervening by reason of his office in any of the acts of the modalities of public procurement or in settlements of public assets or funds, conspires with interested parties or uses any other artifice to defraud any public entity, shall incur the penalties of imprisonment of two to six years and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of six to ten years. The private individual who has conspired with the authority or public official shall be imposed the same penalty of imprisonment as them, as well as disqualification from obtaining subsidies and public aid, from contracting with entities, bodies or organizations that form part of the public sector and from

enjoying tax and Social Security benefits or incentives for a period of two to seven years.

Article 437

The authority or public official who demands, directly or indirectly, fees, tariffs by scales or bills that are not due or in an amount greater than that legally established, shall be punished, without prejudice to the reimbursements to which he may be obliged, with the penalties of fine of six to twenty-four months and suspension from public employment or office for a period of six months to four years.

Article 438

The authority or public official who, abusing his office, commits any crime of fraud or fraud against benefits of the Social Security System under article 307 ter, shall incur the penalties respectively assigned to these, in their upper half, and may reach the higher degree, and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of three to nine years, unless the acts are punished with a more severe penalty in any other provision of this Code.

Article 438 bis

The authority who, during the performance of his function or office and up to five years after having ceased in them, had obtained an increase in assets or a cancellation of obligations or debts for a value exceeding 250,000 euros with respect to his proven income, and openly refuses to duly comply with the requirements of the competent bodies intended to verify its justification, shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment of six months to three years, fine of one to three times the benefit obtained, and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of two to seven years.

CHAPTER IX

Negotiations and activities prohibited to public officials and abuses in the exercise of their function

Article 439

The authority or public official who, being required to intervene by reason of his office in any kind of contract, matter, transaction or activity, takes advantage of such circumstance to force or facilitate for himself any form of participation, directly or through an intermediary, in such business or actions, shall incur the penalty of imprisonment of six months to two years, fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of two to seven years.

Article 440

Experts, arbitrators and partition accountants who conduct themselves in the manner provided in the preceding article, with respect to the property or things in whose valuation, partition or adjudication they have intervened, and guardians, curators or executors with respect to those belonging to their wards or estates, and insolvency administrators with respect to the property and rights included in the insolvency estate, shall be punished with the penalty of fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from public employment or office, profession or trade, guardianship, tutorship or curatorship, as the case may be, for a period of three to six years, unless this conduct is punished with a greater penalty in another provision of this Code.

Article 441

The authority or public official who, outside the cases admitted in laws or regulations, carries out, by himself or through an intermediary, a professional activity or

permanent or occasional advisory services, under the dependence or in the service of private entities or individuals, in a matter in which he must intervene or has intervened by reason of his office, or in those that are processed, reported on or resolved in the office or management center where he is assigned or on which he depends, shall incur the penalties of fine of six to twelve months and suspension from public employment or office for a period of two to five years.

Article 442

The authority or public official who makes use of a secret of which they have knowledge by reason of their office or position, or of privileged information, with the intention of obtaining an economic benefit for themselves or for a third party, shall incur the penalties of a fine of one to three times the benefit pursued, obtained or facilitated and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of two to four years. If they obtain the benefit pursued, the penalties of imprisonment of one to three years, a fine of one to six times the benefit pursued, obtained or facilitated and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of four to six years shall be imposed.
If serious harm results to the public cause or to a third party, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to six years, and special disqualification from public employment or office and from the exercise of the right of passive suffrage for a period of nine to twelve years. For the purposes of this article, privileged information means any information of a specific nature that is held exclusively by reason of public office or position and that has not been notified, published or disclosed.

Article 443

1.	The authority or public official who sexually solicits a person who, for themselves or for their spouse or another person to whom they are linked in a stable manner by an analogous relationship of affection, ascendant, descendant, sibling, by nature, by adoption, or related by affinity in the same degrees, has claims pending resolution by that official or concerning which they must issue a report or submit a query to their superior, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of one to two years and absolute disqualification for a period of six to 12 years.
2.	The official of Penitentiary Institutions, of protection or reform centres for minors, detention centres for foreign nationals, or any other detention or custody centre, including temporary stay, who sexually solicits a person subject to their custody, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of one to four years and absolute disqualification for a period of six to twelve years.
3.	The same penalties shall be incurred when the person solicited is an ascendant, descendant, sibling, by nature, by adoption, or related by affinity in the same degrees of a person under their custody. These penalties shall also be incurred when the person solicited is the spouse of a person under their custody or is linked to that person in a stable manner by an analogous relationship of affection.

Article 444

The penalties provided for in the preceding article shall be imposed without prejudice to those corresponding to offences against sexual freedom actually committed.

CHAPTER X

Provision common to the preceding Chapters

Article 445

Incitement, conspiracy and proposal to commit the offences provided for in this Title shall be punished, respectively, with the penalty lower by one or two degrees.

TITLE XX

Offences against the Administration of Justice

CHAPTER I

On perversion of justice

Article 446

The judge or magistrate who, knowingly, issues an unjust sentence or decision shall be punished:
1.	º With the penalty of imprisonment of one to four years if it concerns an unjust sentence against the accused in criminal proceedings for a serious or less serious offence and the sentence has not been executed, and with the same penalty in its upper half and a fine of twelve to twenty-four months if it has been executed. In both cases, the penalty of absolute disqualification for a period of ten to twenty years shall also be imposed.
2.	º With the penalty of a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of six to ten years, if it concerns an unjust sentence against the accused issued in proceedings for a minor offence.
3.	º With the penalty of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of ten to twenty years, when issuing any other unjust sentence or decision.

Article 447

The Judge or Magistrate who through gross negligence or inexcusable ignorance issues a manifestly unjust sentence or decision shall incur the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of two to six years.

Article 448

The Judge or Magistrate who refuses to adjudicate, without alleging legal cause, or under the pretext of obscurity, insufficiency or silence of the Law, shall be punished with the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of six months to four years.

Article 449

1.	The Judge, Magistrate or Judicial Secretary guilty of malicious delay in the Administration of Justice shall incur the same penalty specified in the preceding article. Delay caused to achieve any illegitimate purpose shall be understood as malicious.
2.	When the delay is attributable to an official other than those mentioned in the preceding paragraph, the penalty indicated, in its lower half, shall be imposed.

CHAPTER II

On the omission of duties to prevent offences or to promote their prosecution

Article 450

1.	A person who, being able to do so with their immediate intervention and without risk to themselves or others, does not prevent the commission of an offence affecting persons in their life, integrity or health, liberty or sexual freedom, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of six months to two years if the offence is against life, and with a fine of six to twenty-four months in other cases, unless the offence not prevented carries an equal or lesser penalty, in which case the penalty lower in degree to that of the offence shall be imposed.
2.	The same penalties shall be incurred by a person who, being able to do so, does not go to the authority or its agents so that they prevent an offence of those provided for in the preceding paragraph and of whose imminent or current commission they have notice.

CHAPTER III

On concealment

Article 451

A person who, with knowledge of the commission of an offence and without having participated in it as perpetrator or accomplice, intervenes after its execution, in any of the following ways, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of six months to three years:
1.	º Assisting the perpetrators or accomplices to benefit from the profit, product or price of the offence, without intent of personal gain.
2.	º Concealing, altering or rendering useless the body, effects or instruments of an offence, to prevent its discovery.
3.	º Helping the alleged perpetrators of an offence to evade investigation by the authority or its agents, or to escape their search or capture, provided that any of the following circumstances exists:
a)	That the concealed act constitutes treason, homicide of the King or Queen or any of their ascendants or descendants, of the Queen Consort or the Consort of the Queen, of the Regent or any member of the Regency, or of the Prince or Princess of Asturias, genocide, crime against humanity, offence against persons and property protected in case of armed conflict, rebellion, terrorism, homicide, piracy, trafficking in human beings or illegal trafficking in organs.
b)	That the concealer has acted with abuse of public functions. In this case, in addition to the penalty of deprivation of liberty, the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of two to four years shall be imposed if the concealed offence is less serious, and absolute disqualification for a period of six to twelve years if it is serious.

Article 452

In no case may a custodial sentence exceeding that prescribed for the concealed offence be imposed. If the latter is punishable by a penalty of another nature, the custodial sentence shall be replaced by a fine of six to twenty-four months, unless the concealed offence has an equal or lesser penalty assigned to it, in which case the offender shall be imposed the penalty for that offence in its lower half.

Article 453

The provisions of this chapter shall apply even when the perpetrator of the concealed act is not criminally responsible or is personally exempt from penalty.

Article 454

Exempt from the penalties imposed on concealers are those who conceal their spouse or a person to whom they are bound in a stable relationship of similar affection, their ascendants, descendants, siblings, by nature, by adoption, or relatives by affinity in the same degrees, with the sole exception of concealers who fall within the circumstances of number 1 of Article 451.

CHAPTER IV

On the arbitrary realisation of one's own right

Article 455

1.	Whoever, in order to realise a right of their own, acting outside legal channels, employs violence, intimidation or force against property, shall be punished with a fine of six to twelve months.
2.	The penalty in the higher degree shall be imposed if weapons or dangerous objects are used for the intimidation or violence.

CHAPTER V

On false accusation and denunciation and the simulation of offences

Article 456

1.	Those who, with knowledge of their falsity or reckless disregard for the truth, impute to any person acts which, if true, would constitute a criminal offence, if such imputation is made before a judicial or administrative official who has the duty to investigate it, shall be punished:
1.	With the penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years and a fine of twelve to twenty-four months, if a serious offence is imputed.
2.	With the penalty of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months, if a less serious offence is imputed.
3.	With the penalty of a fine of three to six months, if a minor offence is imputed.
2.	No proceedings may be brought against the denouncer or accuser except after a final judgment or final order of dismissal or closure by the judge or court that has heard the imputed offence. These shall order proceedings to be brought ex officio against the denouncer or accuser whenever sufficient indications of the falsity of the imputation result from the main proceedings, without prejudice to the fact that the act may also be prosecuted upon denunciation by the aggrieved party.

Article 457

Whoever, before any of the officials referred to in the preceding article, simulates being responsible for or a victim of a criminal offence or denounces a non-existent one, provoking procedural actions, shall be punished with a fine of six to twelve months.

CHAPTER VI

On false testimony

Article 458

1.	A witness who departs from the truth in their testimony in judicial proceedings shall be punished with the penalties of imprisonment from six months to two years and a fine of three to six months.
2.	If the false testimony is given against the accused in criminal proceedings for an offence, the penalties shall be imprisonment from one to three years and a fine of six to twelve months. If a conviction has been handed down as a consequence of the testimony, the penalties in the higher degree shall be imposed.
3.	The same penalties shall be imposed if the false testimony takes place before international tribunals which, by virtue of treaties duly ratified in accordance with the Spanish Constitution, exercise powers derived from it, or is given in Spain when testifying pursuant to a letter rogatory sent by a foreign court.

Article 459

The penalties of the preceding articles shall be imposed in their upper half on experts or interpreters who maliciously depart from the truth in their report or translation, who shall, in addition, be punished with the penalty of special disqualification for profession or trade, employment or public office, for a period of six to twelve years.

Article 460

When the witness, expert or interpreter, without substantially departing from the truth, alters it with reticence, inaccuracies or by concealing relevant facts or data known to them, they shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of six to twelve months and, where applicable, suspension from employment or public office, profession or trade, from six months to three years.

Article 461

1.	Whoever knowingly presents false witnesses or mendacious experts or interpreters shall be punished with the same penalties as are established for them in the preceding articles.
2.	If the person responsible for this offence is a lawyer, procurator, social graduate or representative of the Public Prosecutor's Office, in professional practice or exercise of their function, the penalty shall be imposed in each case in its upper half and that of special disqualification for employment or public office, profession or trade, for a period of two to four years.

Article 462

Whoever, having given false testimony in criminal proceedings, retracts in due time and form, stating the truth so that it takes effect before judgment is handed down in the proceedings in question, shall be exempt from penalty. If deprivation of liberty has occurred as a consequence of the false testimony, the corresponding penalties in the lower degree shall be imposed.

CHAPTER VII

On obstruction of justice and professional disloyalty

Article 463

1.	Whoever, having been summoned in legal form, voluntarily fails to appear, without just cause, before a court or tribunal in criminal proceedings with an accused in provisional detention, causing the suspension of the oral trial, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from three to six months or a fine of six to 24 months. The penalty of a fine of six to 10 months shall be incurred by whoever, having been warned, does so for the second time in criminal proceedings without an accused in detention, whether or not they have caused the suspension.
2.	If the person responsible for this offence is a lawyer, procurator or representative of the Public Prosecutor's Office, in professional practice or exercise of their function, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half and that of special disqualification for employment or public office, profession or trade, for a period of two to four years.
3.	If the suspension takes place, in the case of paragraph 1 of this article, as a consequence of the non-appearance of the judge or member of the tribunal or of the person exercising the functions of court clerk, the penalty of imprisonment from three to six months or a fine of six to 24 months shall be imposed and, in any case, special disqualification for a period of two to four years.

Article 464

1.	Whoever with violence or intimidation attempts to influence directly or indirectly a person who is a denouncer, party or accused, lawyer, procurator, expert, interpreter or witness in proceedings so that they modify their procedural conduct, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from one to four years and a fine of six to twenty-four months.
If the perpetrator of the act achieves their objective, the penalty shall be imposed in its upper half.
2.	The same penalties shall be imposed on whoever carries out any act against the life, integrity, liberty, sexual freedom or property, as reprisal against the persons mentioned in the preceding paragraph, for their conduct in judicial proceedings, without prejudice to the penalty corresponding to the offence of which such acts are constitutive.

Article 465

1.	Whoever, intervening in proceedings as a lawyer or procurator, with abuse of their function, destroys, renders useless or conceals documents or proceedings of which they have received service in that capacity, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years, a fine of seven to twelve months and special disqualification for their profession, employment or public office from three to six years.
2.	If the acts described in the first paragraph of this article are carried out by a private individual, the penalty shall be a fine of three to six months.

Article 466

1. The lawyer or procurator who reveals procedural actions declared secret by the judicial authority shall be punished with the penalties of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from employment, public office, profession or occupation for one to four years.
2. If the revelation of the actions declared secret were carried out by the Judge or member of the Court, representative of the Public Prosecutor's Office, Judicial Secretary or any official in the service of the Administration of Justice, the penalties provided for in article 417 in their upper half shall be imposed.
3. If the conduct described in the first paragraph were carried out by any other private individual who intervenes in the proceedings, the penalty shall be imposed in its lower half.

Article 467

1. The lawyer or procurator who, having advised or taken the defence or representation of a person, without that person's consent defends or represents in the same matter someone who has contrary interests, shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification for their profession for two to four years.
2. The lawyer or procurator who, by action or omission, manifestly harms the interests entrusted to them shall be punished with the penalties of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months and special disqualification from employment, public office, profession or occupation for one to four years.
If the acts were carried out through serious negligence, the penalties of a fine of six to twelve months and special disqualification for their profession for six months to two years shall be imposed.

CHAPTER VIII

Breach of sentence

Article 468

1. Those who breach their sentence, security measure, imprisonment, precautionary measure, transportation or custody shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year if they were deprived of liberty, and with the penalty of a fine of twelve to twenty-four months in other cases.
2. In all cases the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year shall be imposed on those who breach a penalty of those contemplated in article 48 of this Code or a precautionary or security measure of the same nature imposed in criminal proceedings in which the victim is one of the persons referred to in article 173.2, as well as on those who breach the measure of supervised release.
3. Those who render useless or disturb the normal functioning of technical devices that have been arranged to monitor compliance with penalties, security measures or precautionary measures, do not carry them with them or omit the measures required to maintain their correct state of functioning, shall be punished with a penalty of a fine of six to twelve months.

Article 469

Sentenced persons or prisoners who escape from the place where they are confined, using violence or intimidation against persons or force against things or taking part in a riot, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to four years.

Article 470

1. The private individual who provides the escape of a convicted person, prisoner or detainee, either from the place where they are confined or during their transportation, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to one year and a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.
2. If violence or intimidation against persons, force against things or bribery were used for this purpose, the penalty shall be imprisonment from six months to four years.
3. If it concerns any of the persons mentioned in article 454, they shall be punished with the penalty of a fine of three to six months, and in this case the Judge or Court may impose only the penalties corresponding to the damage caused or to the threats or violence exercised.

Article 471

The penalty superior in degree shall be imposed, in their respective cases, if the guilty party were a public official in charge of the transportation or custody of a convicted person, prisoner or detainee. The official shall be punished, in addition, with the penalty of special disqualification from employment or public office for six to ten years if the fugitive were convicted by final judgment, and with special disqualification from employment or public office for three to six years in other cases.

CHAPTER IX

Offences against the Administration of Justice of the International Criminal Court

Article 471 bis

1. The witness who, intentionally, fails to tell the truth in their testimony before the International Criminal Court, being obliged to tell the truth in accordance with the statutory norms and rules of procedure and evidence of said Court, shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to two years. If the false testimony were given against the accused, the penalty shall be imprisonment from two to four years. If as a consequence of the testimony a conviction were issued, a penalty of imprisonment from four to five years shall be imposed.
2. Whoever presents evidence before the International Criminal Court knowing that it is false or has been falsified shall be punished with the penalties indicated in the preceding paragraph of this article.
3. Whoever intentionally destroys or alters evidence, or interferes in evidentiary proceedings before the International Criminal Court shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from six months to two years and a fine of seven to 12 months.
4. Whoever corrupts a witness, obstructs their appearance or testimony before the International Criminal Court or interferes in them shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from one to four years and a fine of six to 24 months.
5. Whoever obstructs an official of the Court, corrupts or intimidates them, to compel or induce them not to fulfil their functions or to do so improperly shall be punished with imprisonment from one to four years and a fine of six to 24 months.
6. Whoever takes reprisals against an official of the International Criminal Court by reason of functions that they or another official have performed shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment from one to four years and a fine of six to 24 months.
Whoever takes reprisals against a witness for their statement before the Court shall incur the same penalty.
7. Whoever solicits or accepts a bribe in the capacity of an official of the Court and in relation to their official functions shall incur the penalty of imprisonment from two to five years and a fine of the equivalent to triple the value of the gift solicited or accepted.

TITLE XXI

Offences against the Constitution

CHAPTER I

Rebellion

Article 472

Those who rise up violently and publicly for any of the following purposes are guilty of the offence of rebellion:
1. º To repeal, suspend or modify totally or partially the Constitution.

2. º To remove or deprive in whole or in part of their prerogatives and powers the King or the Queen, the Regent or members of the Regency, or compel them to execute an act contrary to their will.
3. º To prevent the free holding of elections for public offices.
4. º To dissolve the Cortes Generales, the Congress of Deputies, the Senate or any Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community, prevent them from meeting, deliberating or resolving, extract from them any resolution or remove from them any of their attributions or competences.
5. º To declare the independence of a part of the national territory.
6. º To replace with another the Government of the Nation or the Governing Council of an Autonomous Community, or use or exercise by themselves or deprive the Government or Governing Council of an Autonomous Community, or any of its members of their powers, or prevent or restrict their free exercise, or compel any of them to execute acts contrary to their will.
7. º To remove any kind of armed force from obedience to the Government.

Article 473

1.	Those who, by inducing the rebels, have promoted or sustain the rebellion, and the principal leaders thereof, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment of fifteen to twenty-five years and absolute disqualification for the same period; those who exercise a subordinate command, with imprisonment of ten to fifteen years and absolute disqualification of ten to fifteen years, and mere participants, with imprisonment of five to ten years and special disqualification for public employment or office for a period of six to ten years.
2.	If weapons have been wielded, or if there has been combat between the force under their command and sectors loyal to the legitimate authority, or the rebellion has caused damage to publicly or privately owned properties, cut telegraph, telephone, radio wave, railway or other communications, exercised serious violence against persons, demanded contributions or diverted public funds from their legitimate use, the penalties of imprisonment shall be, respectively, twenty-five to thirty years for the first, fifteen to twenty-five years for the second and ten to fifteen years for the last.

Article 474

When the rebellion has not come to be organized with known leaders, those who in fact direct the others, or speak for them, or sign documents issued in their name, or exercise other similar acts of direction or representation shall be deemed as such.

Article 475

Those who seduce or gather troops or any other kind of armed force to commit the crime of rebellion shall be punished as rebels with a penalty of imprisonment of five to ten years and absolute disqualification for a period of six to twelve years.
If the rebellion comes to take effect, they shall be deemed promoters and shall suffer the penalty provided in Article 473.

Article 476

1.	The military member who does not employ the means at his disposal to contain the rebellion in the forces under his command shall be punished with penalties of imprisonment of two to five years and absolute disqualification of six to ten years.
2.	The military member who, having knowledge that a crime of rebellion is about to be committed, does not immediately report it to his superiors or to the authorities or officials who, by reason of their office, have the obligation to prosecute the crime, shall be punished with the same penalties provided in the preceding paragraph in its lower half.

Article 477

Provocation, conspiracy and proposal to commit rebellion shall be punished, in addition to the disqualification provided in the preceding articles, with a penalty of imprisonment one or two degrees lower than that of the corresponding crime.

Article 478

In the case that the person who commits any of the crimes provided in this chapter is constituted in authority, the penalty of disqualification that is provided in each case shall be replaced by absolute disqualification for a period of fifteen to twenty years, unless such circumstance is specifically contemplated in the criminal type in question.

Article 479

As soon as the rebellion manifests itself, the governmental authority shall order the insurgents to immediately dissolve and withdraw.
If the insurgents do not abandon their attitude immediately after the order, the authority shall make use of the force at its disposal to dissolve them.
The order shall not be necessary from the moment the rebels open fire.

Article 480

1.	The person who, implicated in a crime of rebellion, reveals it in time to prevent its consequences shall be exempt from penalty.
2.	To mere executors who lay down their weapons before having made use of them, submitting to the legitimate authorities, the penalty of imprisonment one degree lower shall be applied. The same penalty shall be imposed if the rebels dissolve or submit to the legitimate authority before the order or as a consequence of it.

Article 481

Particular crimes committed in a rebellion or on the occasion of it shall be punished, respectively, according to the provisions of this Code.

Article 482

Authorities who have not resisted the rebellion shall be punished with the penalty of absolute disqualification of twelve to twenty years.

Article 483

Officials who continue to perform their duties under the command of the insurgents or who, without having had their resignation from their employment accepted, abandon it when there is danger of rebellion, shall incur the penalty of special disqualification for public employment or office of six to twelve years.

Article 484

Those who accept employment from the rebels shall be punished with the penalty of absolute disqualification of six to twelve years.

CHAPTER II

Crimes against the Crown

Article 485

1.	Whoever kills the King or the Queen or the Prince or the Princess of Asturias shall be punished with the penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment.
2.	Whoever kills any of the ascendants or descendants of the King or the Queen, the Queen consort or the consort of the Queen, the Regent or any member of the Regency, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of twenty to twenty-five years, unless the acts were punished with a more severe penalty in any other provision of this Code.
If two or more aggravating circumstances concur in the crime, the penalty of imprisonment of twenty-five to thirty years shall be imposed.
3.	In the case of attempt of these crimes, the penalty one degree lower may be imposed.

Article 486

1.	Whoever causes to the King, the Queen or any of their ascendants or descendants, the Queen consort or the consort of the Queen, the Regent or any member of the Regency, or the Prince or the Princess of Asturias, injuries of those provided in Article 149, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years.
If it were any of the injuries provided in Article 150, it shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of eight to fifteen years.
2.	Whoever causes them any other injury shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of four to eight years.

Article 487

Whoever deprives the King, the Queen or any of their ascendants or descendants, the Queen consort or the consort of the Queen, the Regent or any member of the Regency, or the Prince or the Princess of Asturias, of their personal liberty, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years, unless the acts are punished with a greater penalty in other provisions of this Code.

Article 488

Provocation, conspiracy and proposal for the crimes provided in the preceding articles shall be punished with the penalty one or two degrees lower than those respectively provided.

Article 489

Whoever with violence or serious intimidation compels the persons referred to in the preceding articles to execute an act against their will shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of eight to twelve years.
In the case provided in the preceding paragraph, if the violence or intimidation were not serious, the penalty one degree lower shall be imposed.

Article 490

1.	Whoever breaks into the dwelling of any of the persons mentioned in the preceding articles with violence or intimidation shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to six years. If there is no violence or intimidation, the sentence shall be two to four years.
2.	Whoever seriously threatens any of the persons mentioned in the preceding paragraph shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to six years, and with a prison sentence of one to three years if the threat is minor.
3.	Whoever slanders or insults the King, the Queen or any of their ascendants or descendants, the Queen consort or the consort of the Queen, the Regent or any member of the Regency, or the Prince or Princess of Asturias, in the exercise of their functions or by reason or occasion thereof, shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to two years if the slander or insult is serious, and with a fine of six to twelve months if it is not.

Article 491

1.	Slander and insults against any of the persons mentioned in the preceding article, and outside the cases provided for therein, shall be punished with a fine of four to twenty months.
2.	A fine of six to twenty-four months shall be imposed on whoever uses the image of the King or the Queen or any of their ascendants or descendants, or of the Queen consort or the consort of the Queen, or of the Regent or any member of the Regency, or of the Prince or Princess of Asturias, in any manner that may damage the prestige of the Crown.

CHAPTER III

Offences against State Institutions and the separation of powers

Section 1 Offences against State institutions

Article 492

Those who, when the Crown is vacant or its Holder is incapacitated for the exercise of his authority, prevent the Cortes Generales from meeting to appoint the Regency or the guardian of the minor Holder, shall be punished with a prison sentence of ten to fifteen years and absolute disqualification for a period of ten to fifteen years, without prejudice to the sentence that may correspond to them for the commission of other more serious offences.

Article 493

Those who, without rising up publicly, invade with force, violence or intimidation the premises of the Congress of Deputies, the Senate or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community, if they are in session, shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to five years.

Article 494

Those who promote, direct or preside over demonstrations or other kinds of gatherings before the premises of the Congress of Deputies, the Senate or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community, when they are in session, disrupting their normal functioning, shall incur a prison sentence of six months to one year or a fine of twelve to twenty-four months.

Article 495

1.	Those who, without rising up publicly, carrying weapons or other dangerous instruments, attempt to enter the premises of the Congress of Deputies, the Senate or the Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community, to present petitions to them in person or collectively, shall incur a prison sentence of three to five years.
2.	The sentence provided for in the preceding paragraph shall be applied in its upper half to those who promote, direct or preside over the group.

Article 496

Whoever seriously insults the Cortes Generales or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community, while in session, or any of their Committees in public acts in which they represent them, shall be punished with a fine of twelve to eighteen months.
The accused of the insults described in the preceding paragraph shall be exempt from punishment if the circumstances provided for in Article 210 are present.

Article 497

1.	Those who, without being members of the Congress of Deputies, the Senate or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community, seriously disrupt the order of their sessions shall incur a prison sentence of six months to one year.
2.	When the disruption of the order of the sessions referred to in the preceding paragraph is not serious, a fine of six to twelve months shall be imposed.

Article 498

Those who use force, violence, intimidation or serious threat to prevent a member of the Congress of Deputies, the Senate or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community from attending their meetings, or, by the same means, restrict the free expression of their opinions or the casting of their vote, shall be punished with a prison sentence of three to five years.

Article 499

The authority or public official who violates the inviolability of the Cortes Generales or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community shall be punished with the penalties of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of ten to twenty years, without prejudice to those that may correspond if the act constitutes another more serious offence.

Article 500

The authority or public official who detains a member of the Cortes Generales or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community outside the cases or without the requirements established by current legislation shall incur, as the case may be, the penalties provided for in this Code, imposed in their upper half, and also special disqualification from public employment or office for six to twelve years.

Article 501

The judicial authority who charges or prosecutes a member of the Cortes Generales or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community without the requirements established by current legislation shall be punished with the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for ten to twenty years.

Article 502

1.	Those who, having been summoned in legal form and under warning, fail to appear before a Committee of Inquiry of the Cortes Generales or a Legislative Assembly of an Autonomous Community, shall be punished as guilty of the offence of disobedience. If the offender is an authority or public official, he shall also be imposed the penalty of suspension from public employment or office for a period of six months to two years.
2.	The same penalties shall be incurred by the authority or official who obstructs the investigation of the Ombudsman, Court of Auditors or equivalent bodies of the Autonomous Communities, by refusing or unduly delaying the sending of the reports they request or by hindering their access to the files or administrative documentation necessary for such investigation.
3.	Whoever, summoned before a parliamentary committee of inquiry, fails to tell the truth in his testimony shall be punished with a prison sentence of six months to one year or a fine of 12 to 24 months.

Article 503

The following shall incur a prison sentence of two to four years:
1.	º Those who violently or with intimidation invade the premises where the Council of Ministers or a Council of Government of an Autonomous Community is constituted.
2.	º Those who restrict or by any means place obstacles to the freedom of the Government meeting in Council or the members of a Government of an Autonomous Community, meeting in Council, unless the acts constitute another more serious offence.

Article 504

1. Those who slander, insult or seriously threaten the Government of the Nation, the General Council of the Judiciary, the Constitutional Court, the Supreme Court, or the Governing Council or the High Court of Justice of an Autonomous Community shall incur a fine of twelve to eighteen months.
The perpetrator of slander or insults in accordance with the provisions of the preceding paragraph shall be exempt from punishment if the circumstances provided for in articles 207 and 210 of this Code, respectively, are present.
A sentence of imprisonment of three to five years shall be imposed on those who use force, violence or intimidation to prevent the members of said Bodies from attending their respective meetings.

2. Those who insult or seriously threaten the Armed Forces, Classes or Bodies and Security Forces shall be punished with a fine of twelve to eighteen months.
The perpetrator of the insults provided for in the preceding paragraph shall be exempt from punishment if the circumstances described in article 210 of this Code are present.

Article 505

1. Those who, without being members of the local corporation, seriously disturb the order of its plenary sessions by preventing access to them, the development of the planned agenda, the adoption of agreements or cause disturbances intended to express support for terrorist organizations or groups, shall incur a sentence of imprisonment of six months to one year.
2. Those who, invoking the existence of terrorist organizations or groups, slander, insult, coerce or threaten members of local corporations shall be punished with a sentence one degree higher than that corresponding to the offence committed.

Section 2 Usurpation of powers

Article 506
