In all cases provided for in the preceding section, the penalty of special disqualification for educational profession or occupation, in the teaching, sports and leisure fields, shall also be imposed for a period between three and five years longer than the duration of the penalty of deprivation of liberty imposed where applicable in the sentence, taking proportionally into account the seriousness of the crime and the circumstances present in the offender.

CHAPTER III

Crimes against persons and property protected in case of armed conflict

Article 608

For the purposes of this chapter, protected persons shall be understood as:
1.	º The wounded, sick or shipwrecked and medical or religious personnel, protected by the I and II Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949 or by Additional Protocol I of 8 June 1977.
2.	º Prisoners of war protected by the III Geneva Convention of 12 August 1949 or by Additional Protocol I of 8 June 1977.
3.	º The civilian population and civilian persons protected by the IV Geneva Convention of 12 August 1949 or by Additional Protocol I of 8 June 1977.
4.	º Persons hors de combat and personnel of the Protecting Power and its substitute protected by the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949 or by Additional Protocol I of 8 June 1977.
5.	º Parliamentarians and persons accompanying them, protected by the II Hague Convention of 29 July 1899.
6.	º United Nations personnel and associated personnel, protected by the Convention on the Safety of United Nations and Associated Personnel, of 9 December 1994.
7.	º Any other person having that status by virtue of Additional Protocol II of 8 June 1977 or any other international treaties to which Spain is a party.

Article 609

Whoever, on the occasion of an armed conflict, physically mistreats or places in serious danger the life, health or integrity of any protected person, subjects them to torture or inhuman treatment, including biological experiments, causes them great suffering or subjects them to any medical procedure that is not indicated by their state of health nor in accordance with generally recognised medical standards that the Party responsible for the action would apply, in analogous medical circumstances, to its own nationals not deprived of liberty, shall be punished with a prison sentence of four to eight years, without prejudice to the penalty that may correspond for the injurious results produced.

Article 610

Whoever, on the occasion of an armed conflict, employs or orders the employment of methods or means of combat that are prohibited or intended to cause unnecessary suffering or superfluous injury, as well as those conceived to cause or from which one may fundamentally foresee that they will cause widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment, compromising the health or survival of the population, or orders that no quarter be given, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 10 to 15 years, without prejudice to the penalty corresponding to the results produced.

Article 611

Whoever, on the occasion of an armed conflict, shall be punished with a prison sentence of ten to fifteen years, without prejudice to the penalty corresponding to the results produced:
1.	º Carries out or orders indiscriminate or excessive attacks or makes the civilian population the object of attacks, reprisals or acts or threats of violence whose principal purpose is to terrorise it.
2.	º Destroys or damages, in violation of the rules of International Law applicable in armed conflicts, a non-military vessel or aircraft of an adverse or neutral Party, unnecessarily and without giving time or without adopting the necessary measures to provide for the safety of persons and the preservation of documentation on board.

3.	º Compels a prisoner of war or civilian person to serve, in any form, in the Armed Forces of the adverse Party, or deprives them of their right to be tried regularly and impartially.
4.	º Deports, forcibly transfers, takes as hostage or illegally detains or confines any protected person or uses them to render certain points, zones or military forces immune from attacks by the adverse party.
5.	º Transfers and settles, directly or indirectly, in occupied territory population of the occupying party, so that they reside there permanently.
6.	º Carries out, orders or maintains, with respect to any protected person, practices of racial segregation and other inhuman and degrading practices based on other distinctions of an adverse character, which constitute an outrage upon personal dignity.
7.	º Prevents or delays, unjustifiably, the release or repatriation of prisoners of war or civilian persons.
8.	º Declares abolished, suspended or inadmissible before a Judge or Tribunal the rights and actions of nationals of the adverse party.
9.	º Commits an offence against the sexual freedom of a protected person by committing acts of rape, sexual slavery, induced or forced prostitution, forced pregnancy, forced sterilisation or any other form of sexual assault.

Article 612

Any person who, on the occasion of an armed conflict, shall be punished with a term of imprisonment of three to seven years, without prejudice to the penalty corresponding to the results produced:
1. º Knowingly violates the protection due to hospitals, installations, material, units and means of sanitary transport, prisoner camps, sanitary and safety zones and localities, neutralized zones, places of internment of the civilian population, undefended localities and demilitarized zones, made known by the appropriate distinctive signs or signals.
2. º Exercises violence against sanitary or religious personnel or members of the medical mission, or of relief societies or against personnel authorized to use the distinctive signs or signals of the Geneva Conventions, in accordance with international law.
3. º Seriously insults, deprives or fails to provide indispensable food or necessary medical assistance to any protected person or subjects them to humiliating or degrading treatment, fails to inform them, without justified delay and in an understandable manner, of their situation, imposes collective punishments for individual acts or violates the prescriptions on the accommodation of women and families or on special protection of women and children established in international treaties to which Spain is a party and, in particular, recruits or enlists persons under eighteen years of age or uses them to participate directly in hostilities.
4. º Improperly uses the protective or distinctive signs, emblems or signals established and recognized in international treaties to which Spain is a party, especially the distinctive signs of the Red Cross, the Red Crescent and the Red Crystal.
5. º Improperly or perfidiously uses the flag, uniform, insignia or distinctive emblem of neutral States, of the United Nations or of other States that are not parties to the conflict or of adverse Parties, during attacks or to cover, favor, protect or obstruct military operations, except in cases expressly excepted provided for in international Treaties to which Spain is a party.
6. º Improperly or perfidiously uses a flag of truce or of surrender, attacks the inviolability or unduly detains a parliamentarian or any of the persons accompanying them, personnel of the Protecting Power or its substitute, or a member of the International Commission of Inquiry.
7. º Despoils of their effects a corpse, wounded, sick, shipwrecked, prisoner of war or interned civilian person.
8. º Intentionally causes starvation of the civilian population as a method of warfare, depriving it of goods indispensable for its survival, including the act of

arbitrarily obstructing relief supplies, carried out in accordance with the Geneva Conventions and their Additional Protocols.
9. º Violates suspension of arms, armistice, capitulation or other agreement concluded with the adverse party.
10. º Intentionally directs attacks against any member of United Nations personnel, associated personnel or participant in a peacekeeping or humanitarian assistance mission, in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, provided they are entitled to the protection given to civilian persons or property, under international law of armed conflicts, or threatens them with such attack to compel a natural or legal person to perform or refrain from performing any act.

Article 613

1. Any person who, on the occasion of an armed conflict, carries out or orders to be carried out any of the following actions shall be punished with a term of imprisonment of four to six years:
a) Attacks or makes the object of reprisals or acts of hostility against cultural property or places of worship that constitute the cultural or spiritual heritage of peoples, provided that such property or places are not situated in the immediate proximity of a military objective or are not used in support of the adversary's military effort and are duly marked;
b) Improperly uses the cultural property or places of worship referred to in letter a) in support of a military action;
c) Appropriates on a large scale, steals, loots or commits acts of vandalism against the cultural property or places of worship referred to in letter a);
d) Attacks or makes the object of reprisals or acts of hostility civilian property of the adverse party, causing its destruction, provided that this does not offer, in the circumstances of the case, a definite military advantage or that such property does not contribute effectively to the military action of the adversary;
e) Attacks, destroys, removes or renders useless property indispensable for the survival of the civilian population, except when the adverse party uses such property in direct support of a military action or exclusively as a means of subsistence for the members of its armed forces;
f) Attacks or makes the object of reprisals works or installations containing dangerous forces, when such attacks may produce the release of those forces and cause, consequently, significant losses in the civilian population, except when such works or installations are used in regular, important and direct support of military operations and such attacks are the only feasible means of terminating such support;
g) Destroys, damages or seizes, without military necessity, things that do not belong to them, compels another to hand them over or carries out any other acts of pillage;
h) Requisitions, improperly or unnecessarily, movable or immovable property in occupied territory or destroys non-military ship or aircraft, and its cargo, of an adverse or neutral party or captures them, in violation of the international rules applicable to armed conflicts at sea;
i) Attacks or carries out acts of hostility against the installations, material, units, private residence or vehicles of any member of the personnel referred to in ordinal 10.º of article 612 or threatens with such attacks or acts of hostility to compel a natural or legal person to perform or refrain from performing any act.
2. When the attack, reprisal, act of hostility or improper use has as its object cultural property or places of worship under special protection or to which protection has been conferred by virtue of special agreements, or immovable cultural property or places of worship under enhanced protection or their immediate surroundings, the penalty may be imposed in the higher degree.
In the other cases provided for in the preceding paragraph of this article, the penalty may be imposed in the higher degree when extensive and significant destruction is caused to the property, works or installations upon which they fall or in cases of extreme gravity.

Article 614

Any person who, on the occasion of an armed conflict, carries out or orders to be carried out any other infractions or acts contrary to the prescriptions of international treaties to which Spain is a party and relating to the conduct of hostilities, regulation of the means and methods of combat, protection of the wounded, sick and shipwrecked, treatment due to prisoners of war, protection of civilian persons and protection of cultural property in case of armed conflict, shall be punished with a term of imprisonment of six months to two years.

Article 614 bis

When any of the conducts contained in this chapter form part of a plan or policy or are committed on a large scale, the respective penalties shall be applied in their upper half.

CHAPTER IV

Common provisions

Article 615

Provocation, conspiracy and proposal for the execution of the offences provided for in the preceding chapters of this Title shall be punished with the penalty lower by one or two degrees than that which would correspond to them.

Article 615 bis

1.	The authority or military chief or whoever effectively acts as such who does not adopt the measures within their reach to prevent the commission, by the forces under their command or effective control, of any of the offences included in chapters II, II bis and III of this title, shall be punished with the same penalty as the perpetrators.
2.	If the above conduct is carried out through gross negligence, the penalty shall be one or two degrees lower.
3.	The authority or military chief or whoever effectively acts as such who does not adopt the measures within their reach to ensure that the offences included in chapters II, II bis and III of this title committed by persons under their command or effective control are prosecuted shall be punished with a penalty two degrees lower than that of the perpetrators.
4.	The superior not included in the preceding paragraphs who, within the scope of their competence, does not adopt the measures within their reach to prevent the commission by their subordinates of any of the offences included in chapters II, II bis and III of this title shall be punished with the same penalty as the perpetrators.
5.	The superior who does not adopt the measures within their reach to ensure that the offences included in chapters II, II bis and III of this title committed by their subordinates are prosecuted shall be punished with a penalty two degrees lower than that of the perpetrators.
6.	The official or authority who, without incurring in the conduct provided for in the preceding paragraphs, and failing in the obligation of their office, fails to promote the prosecution of any of the offences included in chapters II, II bis and III of this title of which they have notice shall be punished with the penalty of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of two to six years.

Article 616

In the case of any of the offences included in the preceding Chapters of this Title being committed, except those provided for in article 614 and in paragraphs 2 and 6 of 615 bis, and in the preceding Title by a public authority or official, they shall be imposed, in addition to the penalties specified therein, that of absolute disqualification for a period of ten to twenty years; if it is a private individual, the judges and courts may impose on them that of special disqualification from public employment or office for a period of one to ten years.

Article 616 bis

The provisions of article 20.7 of this Code shall in no case be applicable to those who comply with orders to commit or participate in the acts included in chapters II and II bis of this title.

CHAPTER V

Offence of piracy

Article 616 ter

Whoever by violence, intimidation or deceit, seizes, damages or destroys an aircraft, ship or other type of vessel or platform at sea, or attacks the persons, cargo or goods found on board them, shall be punished as guilty of the offence of piracy with the penalty of imprisonment of ten to fifteen years.
In any case, the penalty provided for in this article shall be imposed without prejudice to those corresponding to the offences committed.

Article 616 quater

1.	Whoever on the occasion of the prevention or pursuit of the acts provided for in the preceding article, resists or disobeys a warship or military aircraft or other ship or aircraft bearing clear signs and identifiable as a ship or aircraft in the service of the Spanish State and authorised for that purpose, shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of one to three years.
2.	If force or violence is used in the above conduct, the penalty of ten to fifteen years' imprisonment shall be imposed.
3.	In any case, the penalties provided for in this article shall be imposed without prejudice to those corresponding to the offences committed.

BOOK III

Petty offences and their penalties

TITLE I

Petty offences against persons

Articles 617 to 622. (Repealed)

TITLE II

Petty offences against property

Articles 623 to 628. (Repealed)

TITLE III

Petty offences against general interests

Articles 629 to 632. (Repealed)

TITLE IV

Petty offences against public order

Articles 633 to 637. (Repealed)

TITLE V

Common provisions for misdemeanors

Articles 638 and 639. (Repealed)
First additional provision.
When a person is declared exempt from criminal liability due to the concurrence of any of the causes provided for in numbers 1 and 3 of article 20, the Public Prosecutor's Office shall evaluate, taking into account the circumstances of the case, the appropriateness of promoting a process for the judicial adoption of support measures for the person with disability or, in the event that such measures had already been previously agreed upon, for their review.

Second additional provision.
When the governmental authority becomes aware of the existence of a minor or a person with disability in need of special protection who is in a state of prostitution, whether or not by their own will, but with the consent of the persons who exercise family or ethical-social authority over them or de facto, or who lacks such persons, or they have abandoned them and do not take charge of their custody, it shall immediately communicate this to the public entity that in the respective territory has been entrusted with the protection of minors and to the Public Prosecutor's Office, so that they may act in accordance with their respective competences.
Likewise, in cases where the Judge or Court orders special disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, foster care, guardianship, tutorship or curatorship, or deprivation of parental authority, it shall immediately communicate this to the public entity that in the respective territory has been entrusted with the protection of minors and to the Public Prosecutor's Office so that they may act in accordance with their respective competences.

Third additional provision.
When, upon complaint or claim by the injured party, criminal proceedings are initiated for acts constituting offenses provided for and punished in articles 267 and 621 of this Code, all those others involved in the same acts who consider themselves injured may appear in the criminal proceedings that are initiated and become parties, whatever the amount of damages they claim.

First transitional provision.
Crimes and misdemeanors committed until the day this Code enters into force shall be tried in accordance with the legal body and other special criminal laws that are repealed. Once this Code enters into force, if its provisions are more favorable to the defendant, these shall be applied.

Second transitional provision.
For the determination of which law is more favorable, the penalty that would correspond to the act being tried with the application of the complete provisions of one or the other Code shall be taken into account. The provisions on redemption of sentences through work shall only be applicable to all those convicted under the repealed Code and those to whom the provisions of the new Code are applied may not benefit from them.
In any case, the defendant shall be heard.

Third transitional provision.
The Directors of penitentiary establishments shall send with the greatest urgency, from the publication of the new Criminal Code, to the Judges or Courts that are hearing the execution, a list of convicted inmates in the Center they direct, and provisional settlement of the sentence being executed, indicating the days that the inmate has redeemed through work and those that they may redeem, if applicable, in the future in accordance with article 100 of the Criminal Code that is repealed and complementary provisions.

Fourth transitional provision.
The Judges or Courts mentioned in the previous provision shall proceed, once the previous sentence settlement has been received, to refer the matter to the Public Prosecutor's Office, so that it may report on whether it is appropriate to review the sentence and, in such case, the terms of the review. Once the Prosecutor has reported, they shall also proceed to hear the defendant, notifying them of the terms of the proposed review, as well as to refer the matter to the Lawyer who assumed their defense at the oral trial, so that they may state what they deem most favorable for the defendant.

Fifth transitional provision.
The General Council of the Judiciary, within the scope of the competences attributed to it by article 98 of the Organic Law of the Judiciary, may assign to one or more of the Criminal Courts or Sections of the Provincial Courts dedicated exclusively to the execution of criminal sentences, the review of final sentences issued before this Code came into force.
Said Judges or Courts shall proceed to review final sentences in which the convicted person is effectively serving the sentence, applying the more favorable provision considered exhaustively and not by the exercise of judicial discretion. In custodial sentences, this Code shall not be considered more favorable when the duration of the previous sentence imposed on the act with its circumstances is also imposable under the new Code. The case in which this Code contains for the same act the alternative provision of a non-custodial sentence is excepted; in such case the sentence must be reviewed.
Sentences in which the execution of the sentence is suspended shall not be reviewed, without prejudice to doing so in the event that the suspension is revoked and before proceeding to the effective execution of the suspended sentence. The same rule shall apply if the convicted person is in a period of conditional release.
Nor shall sentences be reviewed in which, under the repealed Code and the new one, only a fine penalty corresponds.
Sixth transitional provision.
Sentences in which the sentence is executed or suspended shall not be reviewed, even if other pronouncements of the judgment are pending execution, as well as those already fully executed, without prejudice to the fact that the Judge or Court that in the future could take them into account for purposes of recidivism must previously examine whether the act punished therein has ceased to be a crime or could correspond to a lesser penalty than that imposed under this Code.
In cases of partial pardon, sentences shall not be reviewed when the resulting sentence being served by the convicted person is included in a lower imposable range with respect to the new Code.
Seventh transitional provision.
For purposes of the assessment of the aggravating circumstance of recidivism, those crimes provided for in the legal body that is repealed and that have an analogous denomination and attack in the same way the same legal interest shall be understood to be included in the same Title of this Code.

Eighth transitional provision.
In cases where the penalty that could correspond by application of this Code would be weekend arrest, it shall be considered, to assess its comparative severity, that the duration of the deprivation of liberty is equivalent to two days for each weekend that should be imposed. If the penalty were a fine, it shall be considered that each day of substitute arrest that has been imposed or could be imposed by the Judge or Court under the Code that is repealed, is equivalent to two daily quotas of the fine of this legal body.

Ninth transitional provision.
In sentences issued under the legislation that is repealed and that are not final because they are pending appeal, the following rules shall be observed, once the vacatio period has elapsed:
a)	If it is an appeal, the parties may invoke and the Judge or Court shall apply ex officio the provisions of the new Code, when they are more favorable to the defendant.
b)	If it is a cassation appeal, not yet formalized, the appellant may point out legal violations based on the provisions of the new Code.
c)	If, having filed a cassation appeal, it is being processed, the appellant shall be given again, ex officio or at the request of a party, for a term of eight days, so that they may adapt, if they deem it appropriate, the grounds for cassation alleged to the provisions of the new Code, and the interested parties, the Prosecutor and the reporting Magistrate shall be instructed on the appeal thus modified, continuing the proceedings in accordance with Law.

Tenth transitional provision.
Security measures that are being executed or pending execution, agreed upon in accordance with the Law on Dangerousness and Social Rehabilitation, or in application of numbers 1 and 3 of article 8 or number 1 of article 9 of the Criminal Code that is repealed, shall be reviewed in accordance with the provisions of Title IV of Book I of this Code and the previous rules.
In those cases in which the maximum duration of the measure provided for in this Code is less than the time that those subject to it have effectively served, the Judge or Court shall declare said compliance extinguished and, in the case of an internment measure, shall order their immediate release.

Eleventh transitional provision.
1.	When special criminal or procedural laws are to be applied by the ordinary jurisdiction, the following shall be understood to be substituted:
a)	The penalty of major imprisonment, by that of imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years, with the clause of elevation thereof to the penalty of imprisonment of twenty to twenty-five years when two or more aggravating circumstances concur in the act.
b)	The penalty of minor imprisonment, by that of imprisonment of eight to fifteen years.
c)	The penalty of major prison, by that of imprisonment of three to eight years.
d)	The penalty of minor prison, by that of imprisonment of six months to three years.
e)	The penalty of major arrest, by that of arrest of seven to fifteen weekends.
f)	The fine penalty imposed in an amount exceeding one hundred thousand pesetas established for acts punished as a crime, by that of a fine of three to ten months.
g)	The fine penalty imposed in an amount less than one hundred thousand pesetas established for acts punished as a crime, by that of a fine of two to three months.
h)	The fine penalty imposed for criminal acts in an amount proportional to the profit obtained or the damage caused shall continue to be applied proportionally.
i)	The penalty of minor arrest, by that of arrest of one to six weekends.
j)	The fine penalty established for acts defined as misdemeanors, by the fine of one to sixty days.
k)	Penalties depriving of rights shall be imposed in the terms and for the periods established in this Code.

l)	Any other penalty of those suppressed in this Code, by the penalty or security measure that the Judge or Court deems most analogous and of equal or lesser severity. If none exists or if all are more serious, it shall cease to be imposed.
2.	In case of doubt, the defendant shall be heard.

Twelfth transitional provision. (Repealed)
Sole repealing provision.
1.	The following are repealed:
a)	The consolidated text of the Criminal Code published by Decree 3096/1973, of September 14, in accordance with Law 44/1971, of November 15, with its subsequent modifications, except articles 8.2, 9.3, rule 1 of article 20 insofar as it refers to number 2 of article 8, the second paragraph of article 22, 65 and the first and second additional provisions of Organic Law 3/1989, of June 21.
b)	The Law of March 17, 1908 on conditional sentencing, with its subsequent modifications and complementary provisions.
c)	Law 16/1970, of August 4, on Dangerousness and Social Rehabilitation, with its subsequent modifications and complementary provisions.
d)	The Law of July 26, 1878, on the prohibition of dangerous exercises performed by minors.
e)	The substantive criminal provisions of the following special laws:
Law of September 19, 1896, for the protection of insectivorous birds. Law of May 16, 1902, on industrial property.
Law of July 23, 1903, on begging by minors. Law of February 20, 1942, on river fishing.
Law of December 31, 1946, on fishing with explosives.
Law 1/1970, of April 4, on hunting. The crimes and misdemeanors provided for in said Law, not contained in this Code, shall be considered very serious administrative infractions, punishable by a fine of fifty thousand to five hundred thousand pesetas and withdrawal of the hunting license, or of the power to obtain it, for a period of two to five years.
f)	The following provisions:
Article 256 of the Penitentiary Regulations, approved by Royal Decree 1201/1981, of May 8.
Articles 65 to 73 of the Regulations of prison services, approved by Decree of February 2, 1956.
Articles 84 to 90 of Law 25/1964, of April 29, on Nuclear Energy. Article 54 of Law 33/1971, of July 21, on Emigration.
The second paragraph of article 24 of Organic Law 2/1981, of April 6, on the Ombudsman.
Article 2 of Organic Law 8/1984, of December 26, on the regime of appeals in case of conscientious objection and its criminal regime.
Article 4 of Organic Law 5/1984, of May 24, on Appearance before the Investigative Committees of the Congress and the Senate or both Chambers.
Articles 29 and 49 of Law 209/1964, of December 24, Criminal and Procedural Law of Air Navigation.
The terms "active and" of article 137 of Organic Law 5/1985, of June 19, on the General Electoral Regime.
Article 6 of Law 57/1968, of July 27, on Receipt of Advance Amounts in the Construction and Sale of Housing.
2.	All provisions that are incompatible with the provisions of this Code are also repealed.

First final provision.
The Criminal Procedure Law shall be modified in the following terms:

"Article 14.
Third. For the hearing and judgment of cases for less serious crimes, as well as misdemeanors, whether or not incidental, attributable to the perpetrators of those crimes or to other persons, when the commission of the misdemeanor or its proof were related to them, the Criminal Court of the district where the crime was committed or the Central Criminal Court in the scope proper to it."

"Article 779.
Without prejudice to what is established for other special proceedings, the procedure regulated in this Title shall apply to the prosecution of crimes punishable by custodial sentence not exceeding nine years, or with any other penalties of a different nature, whether single, joint or alternative, whatever their amount or duration."

Second final provision.
Paragraph 2 of article 1 of Organic Law 5/1995, on the Jury Court, is worded in the following terms:
"2. Within the scope of prosecution provided for in the previous paragraph, the Jury Court shall be competent for the hearing and judgment of cases for crimes typified in the following provisions of the Criminal Code:
a)	Homicide (articles 138 to 140).
b)	Threats (

article 169.1.º)

c)	Of the omission of the duty to rescue (articles 195 and 196).
d)	Of trespass (articles 202 and 204).
e)	Of forest fires (articles 352 to 354).
f)	Of infidelity in the custody of documents (articles 413 to 415).
g)	Of bribery (articles 419 to 426).
h)	Of influence peddling (articles 428 to 430).
i)	Of embezzlement of public funds (articles 432 to 434).
j)	Of fraud and illegal exactions (articles 436 to 438)
k)	Of negotiations prohibited to civil servants (articles 439 and 440).
l)	Of infidelity in the custody of prisoners (

article 471).»

Final provision third.
1.	Chapter VI of Law 35/1988, of 22 November, on Assisted Reproduction Techniques, shall be modified in the following terms:
1.	º Letters a), k), l) and v) of section 2.B) of article 20 are deleted.
2.	º The text of letter r) of said section 2.B) shall be replaced by the following: «the transfer of human gametes or pre-embryos into the uterus of another animal species or the reverse operation, as well as fertilisations between human and animal gametes that are not authorised».
2. Article 21 of chapter VII of Law 35/1988, on Assisted Reproduction Techniques, shall become article 24.
Final provision fourth.
Organic Law 1/1982, of 5 May, on the Protection of the Right to Honour, Personal and Family Privacy and Self-Image, shall be modified in the following terms:

«Article 1.º
2.	The criminal nature of the intrusion shall not prevent recourse to the judicial protection procedure provided for in article 9.º of this Law. In any case, the criteria of this Law shall be applicable for the determination of civil liability arising from crime.»

«Article 7.º
7. The imputation of facts or the expression of value judgments through actions or expressions that in any way injure the dignity of another person, undermining their reputation or attacking their self-esteem.»
Final provision fifth.
The second additional provision of Organic Law 6/1995, of 29 June, shall be modified in the following terms:
«The exemption from criminal liability contemplated in the second paragraphs of articles 305, section 4; 307, section 3, and 308, section 4, shall be equally applicable even when the debts subject to regularisation are lower than the amounts established in the cited articles.»

Final provision sixth.
Title V of Book I of this Code, articles 193, 212, 233.3 and 272, as well as the first and second additional provisions, the twelfth transitional provision and the first and third final provisions have the character of ordinary Law.
Final provision seventh.
This Code shall enter into force six months after its complete publication in the
«Official State Gazette» and shall apply to all punishable acts committed from its entry into force.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, the entry into force of its article 19 is excepted until such time as the law regulating the criminal liability of minors referred to in said provision enters into force.
Therefore,
I command all Spaniards, individuals and authorities to observe and enforce this Organic Law.
Madrid, 23 November 1995.
JUAN CARLOS R.
The President of the Government, FELIPE GONZÁLEZ MÁRQUEZ
